**Names.** Declaration names follow the same conventions: the namespace `MainTheorem` (not `Endpoint`); `addApex`, adding the apex cell (not `apexLayer`: "layer" is reserved for the layers of this roadmap); hypotheses named by their statements, as in `strongDecode_locality_of_decode_eq_on_below`; `Rows.bot` for the rows that are constantly bottom (the bottom rows); `block` in every name component of the block coding of `Extension/CodedSection` and `Extension/Encoders` (`blockEncode`, `blockDecode`, `blockEncodeOrd`, `blockDecodeOrd`, `blockTopCode`, `valueBlocks`, `codeBlocks`, `codeBlockEmb`, the private `spreadBlock`, and their lemmas), and `band` only for the band \([\alpha,\alpha+K]\) (`bandMap` and the band rule of `Label/Band`); and, still to be renamed, `commonFaceEmb` for the embedding of the common face (now `Coatom.overlap`). `IsStronglyCoded` is kept, with its definition stated where it is introduced.

**Placement.** A module's `## Placement` section holds one cross-reference to this roadmap: the layer or item of the roadmap the module belongs to. Every note on where a declaration of the module should live (a destination module, an upstream library, or a duplicate to be removed) is recorded in `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Placement record", not in the module.

**Review rubrics.** Pull requests are reviewed against ten rubrics: correctness, reuse, scope, attribution, API design, generality, placement, naming, documentation, and proof quality. An eleventh, informal rubric, mathematical terminology, applies the conventions of this section in context: no workflow vocabulary, one word per notion, necessary jargon defined at first use, declaration names in mathematical terms, Oxford spelling with -ize (labelling, catalogue, normalization), and `## Placement` sections reduced to one cross-reference.

Use the standard library's ordinals, cardinalities, `Finset`, `Set`, embeddings, filters, closure operators, syntax, satisfaction, Scott analysis, coded structure spaces, and isomorphism relation. Use the infinitary-logic library's existing domain-countability and sentence-split results instead of redeveloping parallel abstractions. Read ranks and ordinal multiplication with their actual conventions: \(\omega\cdot\xi\) is not \(\xi\cdot\omega\), and block indices are not automatically Scott ranks.

Define raw finite data separately from the proposition asserting their laws. The cells, scopes, grades, rows, and face geometry are part of the data of a scheme. A stage type consists of its scheme and its label section. An occurrence consists of its literal tuple and its evaluated type. A construction returns concrete data and proves laws about those data. A structure field asserting that the desired receiving diagram exists is not its construction.

Use observations \(\operatorname{obs}_c(q)\), not a presumed lawful cap endomorphism. The observation can take values outside the space of lawful sections. Preserve the permitted-cutoff test \(\bot<c<\alpha\) of receiving and of the density sentence, and keep it apart from the self-visible caps at which bountifulness lifts (both defined in Layer 3). Keep face restriction, stage reduction, and capped observation as different operations.

### Layer 0 — General results with no construction imports

**Finite closure.** Develop finite closure operators and anti-exchange geometry, using standard notions. Prove that a finite hull operator has at most one finitary extension to all subsets, and that equality or equivariance on finite hulls extends to the global closure. The general result is reusable; the concrete global corollaries are optional for the main theorem.

**Capped extension.** For a restriction map between lawful section spaces and compatible observations, prove that lifting a prescribed face while preserving the ambient observation is equivalent to surjectivity from an observation fibre onto the corresponding face fibre. Do not assume injectivity, unrestricted caps, or that the observation itself is lawful.

**Directed finite observations.** Develop the bounded/eventually-constant versus unbounded/escaping dichotomy for monotone natural-valued functions on a directed preorder. Prove finite synchronization and appropriate cofinal-reindexing invariance. Reuse filters and \(\mathbb N_\infty\); do not introduce a new notion of infinitary convergence.

**Countable comparison.** Reuse the standard countable potential-isomorphism theorem. Prove only the specializations needed for rooted extendible families. Atomic compatibility of an arbitrary root is not enough: the root must belong to the selected extension family. The dense-set chain theorem (`Construction/DenseChain.lean`) belongs to the chain construction, which the main theorem does not use (the section on the top-free witnesses, after Layer 3).

**The graded back-and-forth theorem** (retired as a target of this repository: both of its applications compile through InfinitaryLogic's generic form, the next paragraph, of which it is the special case with initial match at the height; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Placement record"). *Let \(M\) and \(N\) be structures in a relational language \(L\) (a relational language is required by the forward Karp agreement theorem `BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR`, the application; `BFEquiv` itself needs none), let \(h\) be an ordinal (the height bound; `Ordinal.{0}` in both applications, the universe of quantifier ranks of \(L_{\omega_1,\omega}\) formulas, so that `BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR` applies without a universe lift), and let \(R_\alpha(n,\bar a,\bar b)\), for ordinals \(\alpha\le h\) and \(n<\omega\), be relations between \(n\)-tuples \(\bar a\) of \(M\) and \(\bar b\) of \(N\), repeated coordinates and the empty tuple allowed, such that: (zero) \(R_0(n,\bar a,\bar b)\) implies that \(\bar a\) and \(\bar b\) have the same atomic type (`SameAtomicType`); (descent) \(R_\alpha(n,\bar a,\bar b)\) implies \(R_\beta(n,\bar a,\bar b)\) whenever \(\beta\le\alpha\le h\), which contains the successor step from \(R_{\alpha+1}\) to \(R_\alpha\) and the limit step from \(R_\lambda\) to every \(R_\beta\) with \(\beta<\lambda\); (forth) if \(\alpha+1\le h\) and \(R_{\alpha+1}(n,\bar a,\bar b)\), then for every \(x\in M\) there is \(y\in N\) with \(R_\alpha(n+1,\bar ax,\bar by)\); (back) if \(\alpha+1\le h\) and \(R_{\alpha+1}(n,\bar a,\bar b)\), then for every \(y\in N\) there is \(x\in M\) with \(R_\alpha(n+1,\bar ax,\bar by)\). Then `BFEquiv α n a b` holds for every \(\alpha\le h\) and every pair with \(R_\alpha(n,\bar a,\bar b)\).* The theorem produces no related pair: in each application, the **initial match**, a pair related at the height, is a separate hypothesis. The proof is an induction on \(\alpha\le h\) through `BFEquiv.zero`, `BFEquiv.succ`, and `BFEquiv.limit` (InfinitaryLogic, available at the pin). The height bound is explicit because the relations of the applications are defined only up to a level: the observations of a presentation up to its level, the reductions of types at \(\lambda_\eta\) up to \(\eta\). The initial match is a hypothesis, never a consequence of the other clauses: the empty relation satisfies all of them. Two applications are intended: approximate comparison of full presentations (the section "Reduction to full presentations"; initial match a given pair of closed tuples with equal observations), and the back-and-forth form of condition 3 of the expansion-domain reduction (initial match the common empty chart). Both applications are compiled through the generic form of the next paragraph, on abstract hypotheses; the statement above is kept as the specification of that special case, and no local proof of it is kept.

**The upstream graded-matching theorem and its two applications.** InfinitaryLogic's `bfEquiv_of_gradedMatching` (`Scott/GradedMatching`; available at the pin (signatures checked)), a generic form of the theorem above, takes a relation `R α n a b` defined at every ordinal `α`, with any language (no relationality), and guards its laws by the height: lowering (`β ≤ α → α ≤ height → R α n a b → R β n a b`), and forth and back from `R (α + 1)` into `R α` under `α + 1 ≤ height`; from `α ≤ height` and a related pair `R α n a b` (the initial match) it concludes `BFEquiv α n a b`. Because `R` is defined at every ordinal while the relations of both applications are defined only up to a level, each application puts the height guard \(\alpha\le\eta\) and the selection of coordinates inside `R`, with height \(\eta\). (i) *Approximate comparison* (the section "Reduction to full presentations"): `R α m a' b'` holds when there are a proof of \(\alpha\le\eta\) (the observations at \(\alpha\) take it), closed tuples \(\bar a\) and \(\bar b\) of one length \(n\) with equal observations at \(\alpha\), and a selection \(s\colon\mathrm{Fin}\,m\to\mathrm{Fin}\,n\) of coordinates, repetitions allowed, with \(\bar a\circ s=a'\) and \(\bar b\circ s=b'\); the initial match is the given pair of closed tuples, with the given selection \(s\). (ii) *Condition 3* (the reduction to expansion domains): a match at level \(\alpha\le\eta\) consists of model expansions of the two fixed base models to \(\lambda_\alpha\), a common chart (a stage type at \(\lambda_\alpha\) on \(k\) points), two covers (an actual occurrence of that chart in each expansion), and a selector \(\mathrm{Fin}\,n\to\mathrm{Fin}\,k\) through which the two tuples factor; the initial match is the common empty chart; lowering reduces both expansions and the chart, so no uniqueness of expansions is needed. The initial match of (ii) is two separate lemmas: existence (the empty set is closed in both expansions, so each has an actual occurrence of the empty chart) and compatibility (the two empty charts are equal, `StageType.eq_of_zero`). Both applications are compiled through the upstream theorem, on abstract hypotheses: `FullPresentation.bfEquiv_comp_of_obs_eq` and `ExpansionMatchData.bfEquiv_of_expansionMatch` (`Comparison/GradedMatchingApplications`), each compiled in this repository (theorem named); their instantiation to the construction (the presentations of the full-presentation route for (i), the expansion domains of Layer 5 for (ii)) is not elaborated. The local theorem above is therefore retired in favour of the upstream one: the construction layers supply only atomic agreement, lowering, the forth and back clauses, and the initial match, without an ordinal induction of their own. **From a match of closed sets to the tuple relation.** In both applications the matched objects are closed sets, enumerated (closed tuples, or covers of a common chart), while `bfEquiv_of_gradedMatching` relates tuples of every length; the relation is carried to tuples by a selector \(\mathrm{Fin}\,n\to\mathrm{Fin}\,k\), repetitions allowed, so that a tuple with repeated coordinates is related as a non-injective selection from a matched closed tuple, and the empty tuples as the empty selection, from \(\mathrm{Fin}\,0\). The compiled forms work this way: `FullPresentation.ObsMatch` relates the selections from two closed tuples with equal observations, `ExpansionMatchData.Match` the selections from two covers of one chart, and the initial match of `ExpansionMatchData.bfEquiv_of_expansionMatch` is the empty selection from two covers of an empty chart, obtained from its two separate hypotheses `exists_empty_chart` (an occurrence of a chart on zero points in some expansion of each structure at the height) and `compat` (the two empty charts so obtained are equal). A match of closed sets is not itself back-and-forth equivalence of tuples: `BFEquiv` is concluded for the selected tuples only. Nothing is needed from ComputableModelTheory for it.

**Classical model theory quoted from Mathlib and the two libraries.** The following are general theorems with no construction imports; the development applies them and does not reprove them. The libraries are the infinitary-logic library (InfinitaryLogic) and the computable-model-theory library (ComputableModelTheory). Mathlib's items are available now. The other statements carry one of three markers (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"): *available at the pin* (the InfinitaryLogic statements at the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked, among them the bounded back-and-forth separation interface, `Descriptive/BFSeparation`, the ordinal-indexed \(\Sigma^{\mathrm{in}}_\alpha\)/\(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_\alpha\) hierarchy, `Lomega1omega/InHierarchy`, Montalbán's explicit Scott sentence from a family of orbit formulas, `Scott/MontalbanSentence`, with its complexity bound in the signed hierarchy, `Scott/MontalbanComplexity`, the forgetting of finitely many parameters, `Scott/ForgetParameters`, rank tails and the least level of a cover, `OrdinalCountability`, and the graded-matching theorem, `Scott/GradedMatching`, a generic form of the graded back-and-forth theorem; the bound of an orbit rank by the quantifier rank of an infinitary orbit formula, `orbitRank_le_lift_qrank_of_infinitaryOrbitFormula`, with its corollary `internalScottRank_le_of_infinitaryOrbitFormulas`, and the bounds of the stabilization ordinal by the rank of a characterizing sentence, `stabilizesAt_of_sentence_rank` and `stabilizationOrdinal_le_of_sentence_rank`, with their forms for formulas with no free variables (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Quantitative reconstruction", targets 2 and 3), and thinness from countably many back-and-forth classes at every level, `Descriptive/BFScattered` (`BFScattered`, `not_hasCantorAntichainOn_of_bfScattered`, `isThinOn_of_bfScattered`) and `Descriptive/BFScatteredSentence` (`Sentenceω.isThinOnNatModels_of_bfScattered`), whose compiled applications here are `isThinOn_of_countable_bfClasses` and `isThinOnNatModels_of_countable_bfClasses` (`MainTheorem/Scatteredness`), quotations of `isThinOn_of_bfScattered` and `Sentenceω.isThinOnNatModels_of_bfScattered`; since the repin to `e460cb6`, thinness from countable back-and-forth observations, `Descriptive/BFScattered` (`bfScattered_of_countable_bfObservations`, `not_hasCantorAntichainOn_of_countable_bfObservations`, `isThinOn_of_countable_bfObservations`), the closure of the last free variables with its semantics and rank, `existsTupleFrom`, `realize_existsTupleFrom` and `qrank_existsTupleFrom` (`Scott/MontalbanSentence`, `Scott/MontalbanQuantifierRank`), with which the existential closure `existsLastVars` of `Definability/Syntax` agrees (compiled in `Definability/BlockFormulasExamples`), uniform fixation for stage projections, `UniformFixation` (`StageProjection.exists_uniform_fixing_stage`, `StageProjection.exists_classwise_labelRank_bound`), to be applied (prospective) in item 5 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)", and a countable isolating level for a countable family of countable structures, `Scott/IsolatingLevel` (`exists_isolating_level`, `not_countable_of_forall_unisolated`), the isolating-level lower bound in back-and-forth form, not used by the Scott route to maximal presentations (item 5), which isolates one class at a time; and the ComputableModelTheory statements at our pinned dependency `a1fe761`: representative classes, extension-rich families and direct limits, the factorization of tuples through the age, orbit isolation, and countable prime structures, classical Fraïssé existence, rooted universality and uniqueness, and isolation and primeness over named finite parameters, signatures checked, all imported through the entry module `ComputableModelTheory.Classical`); *available upstream, not yet at our pinned dependency* (of InfinitaryLogic, none `#check`ed in the sketches: at `30c186f`, concentration at back-and-forth levels, `Descriptive/BFConcentration`, named in prose only; at `c16de09`, the attainment of a greatest countable stage, `OrdinalUtil` (`exists_greatest_stage_lt_omega1`, with the general forms `exists_forall_iff_le_of_bounded_of_isSuccLimit_closed` and `exists_isGreatest_setOf_of_bounded_of_isSuccLimit_closed`; available upstream, not yet at our pinned dependency: signatures verified against the upstream source at `2cd44c3`, not compiled here): a predicate on stages that holds at \(0\), is closed downward and under successor limits below \(\omega_1\), and is bounded by a countable \(A\) on the countable stages has a greatest stage \(\rho\le A\), with \(P(\xi)\iff\xi\le\rho\) at every ordinal; it is to be applied as bounded-stage attainment (4 ⇒ 5 of the five criteria of "Manuscript correspondence (required)", item 5, row 34) and in step 5 of the Scott route to maximal presentations (row 40); at `2cd44c3`, Scott separation (available upstream, not yet at our pinned dependency: signatures verified against the upstream source at `2cd44c3`, not compiled here), `OrdinalCountability` (`notMem_of_isolating_of_uniform`, `lt_index_of_isolating_of_antitone`, `lt_rank_of_isolating_of_antitone`, `stage_lt_rank_of_isolating`, `exists_countable_strict_stage_bound_of_isolation`), `Lomega1omega/QuantifierRank` (`BoundedFormulaω.qrank_lt_omega1`, `Sentenceω.qrank_lt_omega1`) and `Descriptive/ScottDefinability` (`IsolatedPresentation.exists_qrank_lt_omega1`, `IsolatedPresentation.exists_countable_strict_stage_bound`): an observation isolating a class excludes it from every domain of two or more members on which that observation is constant, so along decreasing domains of two or more members on which the observations of rank at most the stage agree, a class isolated by a sentence leaves them strictly before a countable stage, every formula of \(L_{\omega_1,\omega}\) having countable quantifier rank; it is to be applied in the lower-bound criterion ("Reduction to full presentations") and in step 4 of the Scott route to maximal presentations (item 5, row 40); of ComputableModelTheory, none; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"); and *prospective* (neither available upstream nor pinned, specified here and not yet written or merged: of InfinitaryLogic, invariant Borel observations and the analogue for `BlockBFEquiv` of the chain-limit lemma (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "The full-presentation route"); the graded back-and-forth theorem above is retired, not prospective). Three kinds of availability are kept apart: a declaration of Mathlib (at the pinned Mathlib commit, named as such); a statement of one of the two libraries *available at the pin* (signatures checked: a `#check` in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean` establishes its name and signature, not that its hypotheses hold in any setting of this roadmap); and an application compiled in this repository, cited by the name of the theorem that applies it (in the library, or in a sketch, on abstract hypotheses). Where no compiled application is cited, the application of a library theorem to a construction of this roadmap is expected, not elaborated. The names below are those of the statements as specified here; where the pinned version of a library names a statement differently, its name prevails. Each statement is recorded with its hypotheses, which are part of it.

- *Fraïssé theory in Mathlib* (`Mathlib/ModelTheory/Fraisse.lean`, available now). For `L : Language.{u, v}` and `K : Set (Bundled.{w} L.Structure)`: the age `L.age M` (the finitely generated structures that embed in `M`); `Hereditary K`, `JointEmbedding K`, and `Amalgamation K`, the last with the literal commuting square `NQ.comp MN = PQ.comp MP`; `IsFraisse K` (nonempty, finitely generated members, countably many isomorphism types, hereditary, joint embedding, amalgamation); `L.IsUltrahomogeneous M` (every embedding of a finitely generated substructure into `M` extends to an automorphism); `IsFraisseLimit K M`, under `[Countable (Σ l, L.Functions l)] [Countable M]` (ultrahomogeneous with age `K`); `IsUltrahomogeneous.extend_embedding`: for finitely generated `S`, `f : S ↪[L] M`, and `g : S ↪[L] T` with `[Nonempty (T ↪[L] M)]`, some `f' : T ↪[L] M` has `f = f'.comp g`; and uniqueness of countable limits, `IsFraisseLimit.nonempty_equiv`. Mathlib has no existence theorem for Fraïssé limits.
- *Classical Fraïssé theory and existence* (ComputableModelTheory; each module imports Mathlib only, and the narrow import is the entry module `ComputableModelTheory.Classical`, which imports exactly the classical modules and defines nothing). Available at the pin, representative classes: for `F : I → Bundled.{w} L.Structure`, `representativeClass F` is the isomorphism closure of the family, and `isFraisse_representativeClass` makes it a Fraïssé class from an inhabited countable index, finitely generated representatives, hereditary closure checked on the finitely generated substructures of representatives, joint embedding, and amalgamation of representatives with the literal commuting square; the countability is of isomorphism types (`representativeClass_countable_quotient`), not of a bundled set. Extension-rich families (`FGCofinal`, `ExtensionRich`, `isFraisseLimit_of_extensionRich`, where the countability hypotheses of Mathlib's `IsFraisseLimit` enter) and direct limits of embeddings that need not be inclusions (`SequenceExtension`; `amalgamationRich_of_sequenceExtension`, from hereditary closure and amalgamation; `age_directLimit_eq`, from stage membership, hereditary closure, finite generation of the members, and coverage, without amalgamation; `countable_directLimit`, without countability of the signature; `isFraisseLimit_directLimit`). **The existence theorem is available at the pin** (signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; module `ModelTheory/FraisseExistence`, in the namespace `FirstOrder.Language`), in two forms. From representatives: `theorem exists_isFraisseLimit_representativeClass [Countable (Σ n, L.Functions n)] [Countable I] [Nonempty I] [∀ i, Countable (F i)] (hfg : ∀ i, Structure.FG L (F i)) (hsub : ∀ i (S : L.Substructure (F i)), S.FG → ∃ j, Nonempty (S ≃[L] F j)) (hjep : ∀ i j, ∃ k, Nonempty (F i ↪[L] F k) ∧ Nonempty (F j ↪[L] F k)) (hap : ∀ i j k (f : F i ↪[L] F j) (g : F i ↪[L] F k), ∃ (l : I) (a : F j ↪[L] F l) (b : F k ↪[L] F l), a.comp f = b.comp g) : ∃ (M : Bundled.{w} L.Structure) (_ : Countable M), IsFraisseLimit (representativeClass F) M`. For a Fraïssé class: `theorem exists_isFraisseLimit_of_isFraisse [Countable (Σ n, L.Functions n)] (K : Set (Bundled.{w} L.Structure)) [hK : IsFraisse K] : ∃ (M : Bundled.{w} L.Structure) (_ : Countable M), IsFraisseLimit K M`. The proof builds a Fraïssé sequence (`exists_fraisseSequence` (under `[Countable I] [Nonempty I]`): stages and chain maps such that every representative embeds in a stage and the whole-stage extension property `SequenceExtension` holds against the representative class, from finite generation, countable carriers, joint embedding, and amalgamation, without hereditary closure) and takes its direct limit. The limit's carrier is the direct limit itself, in the representatives' universe, not necessarily \(\mathbb N\); it may be finite or empty. Hereditary closure enters through `hsub`, as the closure of the representatives' finitely generated substructures under isomorphism; the amalgamation square is literal. It is classical [Fra54; Hod93, §7.1], uses choice, and supplies no computable enumeration. Its application to the age of top-free charts (step 3 of the section on the top-free witnesses) is `exists_isFraisseLimit_topFreeAge` (`ClassicalLimit/Amalgamation`), compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on the coatom extension property `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` at the stage (still to be proved), at a countable stage that is a nonzero limit; `isFraisse_representativeClass`, `representativeClass_hereditary`, and `representativeClass_countable_quotient` are applied there too (`isFraisse_topFreeAge`, `hereditary_topFreeAge`, `countable_quotient_topFreeAge`).
- *Factorization of finite tuples through the age* (ComputableModelTheory, available at the pin; beside the representative classes). For `F : I → Bundled.{w} L.Structure` and `M : Type w` (the bundled age and `F` in one carrier universe): `exists_factor_tuple_of_age_subset (h : L.age M ⊆ representativeClass F) (a : Fin n → M) : ∃ (i : I) (e : F i ↪[L] M) (b : Fin n → F i), e ∘ b = a`, repeated coordinates allowed, and `exists_factor_embedding_of_age_subset` for `a : Fin n ↪ M`, with `b : Fin n ↪ F i` and `b.trans e.toEmbedding = a`, the injectivity of `b` derived from the literal factorization. The equations are literal: that some representative embeds in `M` is not the statement. No homogeneity, receiving, amalgamation or joint embedding, finiteness of representatives, countability, or nonempty carrier or index is assumed. At `n = 0` the generated substructure contains the constants and need not be empty. Only this direction is asserted: coverage of tuples does not give equality of the age with the class without further hypotheses.
- *Orbit isolation, atomicity, and countable prime structures* (ComputableModelTheory, available at the pin; Mathlib only; no countability or relationality). For `a : Fin n → M`: `IsolatesTuple T φ a` (φ holds at `a`, and for every first-order `ψ`, `T ⊨ φ → ψ` exactly when `ψ` holds at `a`: entailment over all models of `T`, not only agreement inside `M`) and `IsAtomic T M` (every finite tuple has an isolating formula; `M ⊨ T` is a separate hypothesis). `isolatesTuple_of_orbit_formula`: under `[Nonempty M]`, if `φ.Realize b ↔ ∃ e : M ≃[L] M, ∀ i, e (a i) = b i` for every `b`, then `φ` isolates `a` over `L.completeTheory M`; `isAtomic_of_orbit_formulas` for every tuple; `IsolatesTuple.realize_iff`, transfer to any nonempty model of `T` in any universe; `IsolatesTuple.typesWith_eq_singleton`, under `[Nonempty M] [M ⊨ T]`, a singleton in the space of complete types, including types not realized in `M`. `exists_elementaryEmbedding_of_countable_atomic`: under `[Countable M] [Nonempty M] [N ⊨ L.completeTheory M]` and `IsAtomic (L.completeTheory M) M`, `Nonempty (M ↪ₑ[L] N)`, with separate universes for `M` and `N`, function symbols allowed, and no countability of `L` or `N`. The orbit formulas are ordinary first-order formulas of `L`, without constants naming the tuple, and may depend on the tuple; the empty tuple and repeated coordinates are included. "Atomic" means that complete types are isolated, not that formulas are syntactically atomic.
- *Rooted universality and uniqueness; isolation and primeness over named finite parameters* (ComputableModelTheory, available at the pin, signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; modules `ModelTheory/RootedExtension` and `ModelTheory/NamedParameters`). `ExtendsRepresentatives F N`: every embedding `g : F i ↪[L] N` extends along every `f : F i ↪[L] F j`, `∃ h : F j ↪[L] N, h.comp f = g`. `isExtensionPair_of_age_subset (hM : L.age M ⊆ representativeClass F) (hN : ExtendsRepresentatives F N) : L.IsExtensionPair M N`, with no hereditary closure, countability, or age hypothesis on `N`. Rooted universality, `exists_embedding_comp_eq_of_age_subset_of_countable [Countable M] (hM : L.age M ⊆ representativeClass F) (hN : ExtendsRepresentatives F N) (hA : Structure.FG L A) (aM : A ↪[L] M) (aN : A ↪[L] N) : ∃ e : M ↪[L] N, e.comp aM = aN`, and rooted uniqueness, `exists_equiv_comp_eq_of_age_subset_of_countable` (for `M` and `N : Type w` countable, each with age in the representative class and extending the representatives' embeddings: `∃ e : M ≃[L] N, e.toEmbedding.comp aM = aN`), are specializations of Mathlib's `embedding_from_cg` and `equiv_between_cg`. Over named parameters `c : Fin k → M`, read as the expansion `Named M c` in `L[[Fin k]]`: `isAtomic_named_of_orbit_formulas` (under `[Nonempty M]`) (formulas `ψ : L.Formula (Fin k ⊕ Fin n)` defining the orbits under the automorphisms fixing `c` make the expansion atomic over its own complete theory) and `exists_elementaryEmbedding_named` (a countable nonempty `M`, atomic over that theory, embeds `L`-elementarily into any `N` with parameters `d` whose expansion models it, sending `c` to `d`; no countability of `L` or `N`). Rooted uniqueness is the generic form of exact comparison for a prescribed age (the section "Reduction to full presentations"), over the empty root or a named core; the named-parameter statements are the generic forms of atomicity and primeness in the language naming a rigid core (Layer 4). No application of either is compiled here: their use for exact comparison and for the rigid-core case is expected, not elaborated.
- *Orbit formulas and the internal Scott rank; local automorphisms* (InfinitaryLogic, modules `Lomega1omega/QuantifierRank`, `Scott/OrbitFormulaThreshold`, and `Lomega1omega/LocalAutomorphism`). **Available at the pin:** these statements are available at the pin `e460cb6` (signatures checked) (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"); `SuggestedInterfaces.lean` `#check`s them, and the companion sketch applies them. The development does not reprove them. (i) `BoundedFormula.qrank_toLω_lt_omega0 (φ : L.BoundedFormula ι k) : φ.toLω.qrank < Ordinal.omega0`, for any first-order language, free-variable type, and arity, without relationality. (ii) Under `[L.IsRelational]`, for `M : Type w`, `a : Fin n → M`, and an orbit formula `φ : L.Formula (Fin n)` (`∀ b, φ.Realize b ↔ ∃ e : M ≃[L] M, ⇑e ∘ a = b`): `orbit_determined_of_orbitFormula` (back-and-forth equivalence to `a` at the lift of `φ.toLω.qrank` forces an automorphism carrying `a` to `b`), `exists_finite_orbit_threshold` (a finite threshold `β < ω`), `orbitRank_le_lift_qrank_of_orbitFormula`, and `orbitRank_lt_omega0_of_orbitFormula`, with binders `{L : Language.{u, v}} [L.IsRelational] {M : Type w} [L.Structure M]` and hypothesis `hφ : ∀ b : Fin n → M, φ.Realize b ↔ ∃ e : M ≃[L] M, ⇑e ∘ a = b`; no countability, nonemptiness, or infinitude of `M`. Relationality is a hypothesis of these statements, because the agreement theorem they use (`BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR`) requires it. (iii) Under `[L.IsRelational]`, `internalScottRank_le_omega0_of_orbitFormulas`: an orbit formula for every finite tuple gives `internalScottRank (L := L) M ≤ Ordinal.omega0`, in the library's convention `⨆ a, orbitRank a + 1`. Each tuple's formula has its own finite rank and no uniform bound is assumed, so the conclusion is `≤ ω`, not `< ω`, and not an equality. It is not deduced from atomicity: an isolating formula of a complete first-order type is not by itself an orbit formula. (iv) For any `L`, any `M`, and `f : M → M` with `∀ n (a : Fin n → M), ∃ e : M ≃[L] M, ⇑e ∘ a = f ∘ a`: `BoundedFormulaω.realize_comp_of_localAutomorphisms`, every `φ : L.BoundedFormulaω Empty n` satisfies `φ.Realize Empty.elim (f ∘ a) ↔ φ.Realize Empty.elim a`; its case of a self-embedding `g : M ↪[L] M`, `BoundedFormulaω.realize_embedding_comp_of_localAutomorphisms`; and the form with finite parameters, `BoundedFormulaω.realize_comp_append_of_localAutomorphisms`, for `φ : L.BoundedFormulaω (Fin m) n`, one automorphism chosen for the appended tuple moving both. No relationality, countability, infinitude, or nonemptiness. Injectivity and the embedding property of `f` are consequences of its hypothesis, not premises, and surjectivity is not implied; only finitely many free variables are assigned. Proving that an application has the local agreement property is not part of (iv). The construction supplies exactly two interfaces to these theorems: an orbit formula in the relational stage chart language for every finite tuple, and, for each relevant self-embedding and finite tuple, an automorphism agreeing with it on the tuple; everything after these interfaces is an application, compiled on abstract hypotheses in `SuggestedCompanions.lean`, section B (`exists_finite_threshold_of_orbitDefinedBy`, `orbitRank_lt_omega0_of_orbitDefinedBy`, `internalScottRank_le_omega0_of_orbitDefinedBy`, `realize_iff_realize_comp_of_agreesLocally`, `realize_comp_append_iff_of_agreesLocally`); their instantiation to the top-free witnesses awaits the two interfaces and is not elaborated (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Applications of library theorems"). The imports are the narrow modules `InfinitaryLogic.Scott.OrbitFormulaThreshold` and `InfinitaryLogic.Lomega1omega.LocalAutomorphism`, never `InfinitaryLogic.All`; they bring no López–Escobar or descriptive-set-theoretic machinery.
- *The rank of the Scott process* (InfinitaryLogic, available at the pin: merged at `a640bbb`, contained in the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; `Scott/OrbitRankStabilization` and `ScottProcess/RankComparison`). For any language and structure, `selfStabilizesCompletely_iff_orbitRank_le` and `bfStabilizationOrdinal_self_eq_iSup_orbitRank`. Under `[L.IsRelational] [Infinite M]`, for the process `P = scottProcessOf L M δ hδ`: `stabilizesAt_of_orbitRank_le (hα : α + 1 < δ) (h : ∀ n a, orbitRank a ≤ lift α) : P.StabilizesAt α`, so stabilization at `ω` needs `ω + 1 < δ`; `rank_le_of_orbitRank_le (h : P.Terminating)`, which keeps the termination hypothesis; and, each under `P.Terminating`, the comparison with the internal rank (`lift_rank_le_internalScottRank`, `internalScottRank_le_lift_rank_add_one`, with the attained and non-attained cases). Process levels are in `Ordinal.{0}` and orbit and internal ranks in the carrier universe, with explicit lifts. The internal rank is not identified with the rank of the process, and neither is compared with the separate convention of `Scott/Rank.lean`.
- *Uniform back-and-forth separation; the \(\Sigma^{\mathrm{in}}_\alpha\)/\(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_\alpha\) hierarchy* (InfinitaryLogic, available at the pin: `e460cb6`, signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; `Descriptive/BFSeparation` and `Lomega1omega/InHierarchy`). Under `[L.IsRelational]`, with no countability of the symbols: `exists_uniform_bfSeparation (hA : AnalyticSet A) (hA_noniso : ∀ p ∈ A, ¬ (structureIsoSetoid L).r p.1 p.2) : ∃ α < ω₁, ∀ p ∈ A, ¬ CodeBFEquiv α p.1 p.2`, for a set `A` of pairs of codes on \(\mathbb N\), with levels in `Ordinal.{0}` and no offset; also `CodeBFEquiv.monotone`, `exists_uniform_bfSeparation_forall_ge`, and `exists_uniform_bfSeparation_of_analyticSets` (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Upstream building blocks"). It enters this repository through InfinitaryLogic's `isThinOn_of_bfScattered` (`Descriptive/BFScattered`), whose proof applies it to the off-diagonal of the range of a Cantor antichain and which `isThinOn_of_countable_bfClasses` (`VaughtConjecture.MainTheorem.Scatteredness`, Layer 6) quotes; `exists_forall_not_codeBFEquiv_of_isClosed`, kept there for a closed antichain, quotes InfinitaryLogic's `exists_forall_not_codeBFEquiv_of_analyticSet` and is not used by the thinness theorems; Mathlib's `Set.offDiag` enters through the kept lemmas `analyticSet_offDiag` and `offDiag_noniso`. The hierarchy has two kinds of classes, kept apart: the **signed complexity classes** `IsSigmaIn α` and `IsPiIn α` (`inSigned`), syntactic classes read off the formula by a signed traversal (atoms in every class; an antecedent flips the sign; quantifiers and countable conjunctions and disjunctions raise the level from `1` on), and the **normal forms** `IsSigmaInNF α` and `IsPiInNF α` of [Mon], which lie in the signed classes of the same level (`NormalFormIn.inSigned`); the converse, up to logical equivalence, is not formalized. A complexity class stated in this roadmap is a signed class unless a normal form is named.
- *Montalbán's explicit Scott sentence* (InfinitaryLogic, available at the pin: `e460cb6`, signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; `Scott/MontalbanSentence` and `Scott/MontalbanComplexity`). For a countable `M` (`[Countable M]`) in a language with countably many relation symbols (`[Countable (Σ l, L.Relations l)]`) and a family `Φ : ∀ n, (Fin n → M) → L.Formulaω (Fin n)` indexed by all tuples, `montalbanSentence Φ : L.Formulaω (Fin 0)` is \(\Phi_{0,\langle\rangle}\wedge\bigwedge_{a}\forall\bar x\,(\Phi_a(\bar x)\to D_a(\bar x)\wedge\bigwedge_{m}\exists y\,\Phi_{am}(\bar x,y)\wedge\forall y\bigvee_{m}\Phi_{am}(\bar x,y))\), where \(D_a\) is the atomic diagram of \(a\) (`atomicDiagram a`), a countable conjunction of atoms and negated atoms (in \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_1\), not finitary, unlike the finitary diagram of [Mon]). If `IsOrbitFormulaFamily Φ` (`(Φ n a).Realize b ↔ ∃ e : M ≃[L] M, ⇑e ∘ a = b`), `M` satisfies the sentence (`montalbanSentence_self`). Under `[L.IsRelational]`, for any family, a countable `N : Type w` (the carrier universe of `M`) satisfying the sentence is isomorphic to `M` (`nonempty_equiv_of_realize_montalbanSentence`), so for an orbit-formula family `(montalbanSentence Φ).realize_as_sentence N ↔ Nonempty (M ≃[L] N)` (`montalbanSentence_characterizes`); the pointed form over parameters `c : Fin k → M`, with `Φ n a : L.Formulaω (Fin (k + n))`, gives `(montalbanSentencePointed c Φ).Realize d ↔ ∃ e : M ≃[L] N, ⇑e ∘ c = d` (`montalbanSentencePointed_characterizes`). Its complexity, in the signed classes `IsSigmaIn`/`IsPiIn` (syntactic membership, not Montalbán's normal forms), for `α : Ordinal.{0}`: `isPiIn_montalbanSentence (hα : 1 ≤ α) (hΦ : ∀ n a, IsSigmaIn α (Φ n a)) : IsPiIn (α + 1) (montalbanSentence Φ)`, with no orbit property and no relationality (pointed: `isPiIn_montalbanSentencePointed`); the Scott-sentence corollary `exists_isPiIn_scottSentence_of_sigmaIn_orbits` (under `[L.IsRelational]` and `1 ≤ α`: if the automorphism orbit of every tuple is defined by a formula in `IsSigmaIn α`, then there is `σ : L.Formulaω (Fin 0)` with `IsPiIn (α + 1) σ` and `σ.realize_as_sentence N ↔ Nonempty (M ≃[L] N)` for every countable `N : Type w`; pointed: `exists_isPiIn_pointed_of_sigmaIn_orbits`). The hypothesis `1 ≤ α` is part of the statement (at level \(0\) the forth clause is existential); level zero is a separate result, `exists_isPiIn_two_scottSentence_of_sigmaIn_zero_orbits`: orbit formulas in `IsSigmaIn 0` (finitary quantifier-free) give a Scott sentence in `IsPiIn 2`. It supplies *a* Scott sentence from suitable orbit formulas, not a particular one (Layer 4); no application to a structure of this roadmap is elaborated.

These theorems concern a structure in its full language and its own first-order theory. Applied to a top-free witness in the stage chart language, they say nothing by themselves about its base reduct or about the other models of the infinitary sentence. Orbit formulas and the rank bounds stay in the relational stage chart language \(L_\lambda\). Chart homogeneity in \(L_\lambda\) follows from ultrahomogeneity of the \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure \(M\) of the section on the top-free witnesses: the points of two actual occurrences of one type span substructures isomorphic to the finite structure of that type (step 4 there, by the factorization of tuples), the isomorphism between them extends to an automorphism of \(M\), and every automorphism of an \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure is an automorphism of its \(L_\lambda\)-reduct. Only this direction is used. The converse holds for a realization with its definitional expansion (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §5), and for \(M\) once the reconstruction roundtrip (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 11) identifies its operations with the definable hull operations of the reconstructed realization; it is not used. The finite age lives in the hull-function expansion \(L^h_\lambda\), and orbit formulas and infinitary rank bounds concern the relational stage chart language \(L_\lambda\); the passage between them is the explicit correspondence just described. Three notions are also kept apart. (a) *Uniqueness of two realizations of the same exact age:* two countable ultrahomogeneous structures with the same age, in a language with countably many function symbols and with carriers in one universe, are isomorphic (`IsFraisseLimit.nonempty_equiv`); this compares two limits of one age. (b) *Relative universality into a receiver:* a countable structure whose age is contained in the age of an ultrahomogeneous receiver embeds into it (the argument of `IsFraisseLimit.isExtensionPair` with `age M ⊆ age N`, then `embedding_from_cg`); the receiver may have additional chart types, and the conclusion is an embedding, not an isomorphism. (c) *Classification over a named rigid core* (the rigid-core comparison of Layer 4): models with the same actual globally rigid-core type are compared over that core. For (c), the class of charts involved is not "all types extending the core type" unless its admissibility conditions are proved: rigidity controls the actual containing charts in the model, not arbitrary extensions of the root type.

**Counting and observation.** Prove countability from a countable cover by subsingleton conditions; reuse the existing countable-loss theorem. Develop sentence splitting on a faithful class presentation without a measurable structure on its quotient.

**Summit 0:** these results are stated without reference to the construction, and their proofs do not import it. Do not state a general result in greater generality than its two or three actual applications require. The classical theorems quoted from Mathlib and the two libraries are applied with their stated hypotheses and are not reproved here.

### Layer 1 — Finite semantic kernel and closed charts

Define the recursive finite visible-face plans and prove their equivalence with the required subclass of finite convex geometries: every nonsingleton closed set has exactly two extreme points. Develop intersections, restriction, hulls, extremes, and the two-generator property. A two-generator hull need not have size two.

Define the actual graded cells, semantic rows, extended-ordinal labels, visibility replacement, lawful sections, and bountifulness at every cap self-visible at the target grade [Kni26, Definition 2.5.14] (these caps are not the permitted cutoffs of receiving; Layer 3). Retain the order, availability, and locality laws from the semantic contract (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`). Prove restriction, transport, pullback, zero/bottom cases, and the bounded transformations actually used by the construction. Guarded composition must retain its guards; do not declare a transitivity instance for a transformation relation that fails unrestricted composition. The lifting calculus does not rely on the following printed statements of [Kni26], which are not correct as stated: the transitivity of the transformation relation (Lemma 2.3.14); the floor formula, a maximum over the values below, extending the shifter in the proof of Lemma 2.3.10; Lemma 2.5.7 for an arbitrary transformation of a lawful section; Lemma 2.5.11, whose pointwise minimum of two lawful sections can lose availability; the offset bound of Lemma 2.5.13; the least active grade of Lemma 2.5.9; and the shifter (the map \(\sigma\) of a witness, [Kni26, Definition 2.3.9]) displayed in the proof of Lemma 3.1.3. Nor do the lifting calculus and the completion of the coatom amalgam (Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)) rely on the joint lifting of [Kni26, Lemma 4.3.19], whose conclusions 1–3 cannot hold together under the natural readings of efficiency ([Kni26, Definition 4.3.6], whose bindings the text leaves open): an instance with cap 2 and prescriptions 3 and 4 forces \(3=4\).

Define countable stage types and exact partial face maps, preserving undefined faces. Establish stage-reduction coherence and all countability results needed for the language and requests.

**Summit 1:** a transparent finite semantic category with lawful restriction and exact cap-ball lifting statements, but no model existence assumed.

### Layer 2 — Realizations, syntax, and finite charts as finite substructures

Define realizations with exact partial evaluation, consistency, covering, and the four unchanged extension families. Prove isomorphism transport and reduction of models. Derive canonical finite hulls from consistency and covering alone, and prove that actual finite supports are precisely the finite closed sets. Later comparisons should use these hulls where they only need a containing chart.

**Hull operations: finite charts as finite substructures.** This material is part of the core, because the classical limit uses it (the section on the top-free witnesses, after Layer 3). At every stage \(\lambda\), let the **stage chart language** have one relation symbol for each legal stage type at \(\lambda\) (the base-stage language below is the case \(\lambda=\omega\)); a realization at \(\lambda\) is read as a structure in it. Construct the parameter-free first-order definable total binary **hull operations** of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §1, indexed by the stage types at \(\lambda\) with a pair of coordinates containing the extreme points and a target coordinate (countably many for countable \(\lambda\)), and the **definitional expansion** of the stage chart language by them. Prove the five facts that the classical limit uses, and only these:

1. **finite hulls and their generators:** the canonical finite hull of a finite set, from consistency and covering, and its generation: a nonsingleton finite closed set is the hull of its two extreme points (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §3);
2. **definable total hull operations:** each operation is total on every exactly consistent covering realization, its graph is defined by a first-order formula of the stage chart language, and its value lies in the hull of its two arguments (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §§1–2);
3. **preservation by chart embeddings, including the default value:** an embedding of stage-chart-language structures between exactly consistent covering realizations, in particular a chart embedding (a face map along which restriction is literal), commutes with every hull operation, both where its defining formula has a chart witness and at the default value (the first argument) where it has none; the default case uses that a witness in the target reflects to one in the source (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §5);
4. **closed images of embeddings:** such an embedding carries finite closed sets to closed sets, the hull of a finite set to the hull of its image; hence it is an embedding of the definitional expansions, and every embedding of the expansions is one of the relational reducts (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §5);
5. **finite expanded substructures are actual charts:** the finitely generated substructures of the definitional expansion are exactly the finite closed sets, the supports of actual charts (the substructure generated by a finite set is its hull, by items 1–2), and each, with the induced structure, is isomorphic to the finite structure of the type of its actual chart; a chart on \(n\) points is read as the finite structure on its points whose relations are its visible faces with their types (its partial realization, in which a supported invisible face carries no relation) and whose operations are computed in the chart; chart embeddings are exactly the embeddings of these finite structures.

**Status for finite charts.** For a finite chart, read as the face realization of its stage type, the five facts are compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `Language/HullOperations` and `Language/HullDefinability`: item 1's generation of a nonsingleton closed set by its two extreme points by `StageType.hull_eq_univ_iff_extremes_subset`; item 2 by `StageType.realize_graphFormula` (the graph formula `HullIndex.graphFormula`, default value included), `StageType.definableFun_funMap_op`, and `StageType.hullOp_mem_hull`; item 3 for chart embeddings by `StageType.hullOp_map_of_restrictFace`, default value included; item 4 for chart embeddings by `StageType.hull_map_of_restrictFace`, and, when one of the two charts is legal, by `StageType.exists_eq_chartEmbedding` (every embedding of the charts is a chart embedding); item 5, for a legal finite chart, by `StageType.toChart_mem_closure_iff` and `StageType.exists_equiv_comap`, legality being essential there (`Language/HullOperationsExamples`: for a stage type on at least three points that is not legal, its two extreme points form a substructure of its chart that is not a closed face); and the two-charts theorem of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §1, for finite charts, by `StageType.eq_of_restrictFace_eq_some`.

**Status for realizations.** The two-charts theorem of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §1, for exactly consistent covering realizations is compiled in this repository (theorem named), `Realization.eq_of_eval_eq_some` (`Realization/TwoCharts`), with no legality hypothesis. With it, on every exactly consistent covering realization: `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §3 for pairs (every point of the canonical hull of a pair, `Realization.finiteHull`, is a value of a hull operation at the pair), by `Realization.exists_hullOp_eq_of_mem_finiteHull`, under the legality of the occurrence on the hull of the pair, which is necessary (`Realization.isLegal_of_hullOp_eq_right`), and by `Realization.mem_finiteHull_pair_iff` for a realization with legal types; item 2: totality, each hull operation being the only solution of its defining relation at every pair, repeated pairs and pairs with no chart witness included (`Realization.hullOp_eq_iff`, `Realization.existsUnique_chartWitness_or_default`), the definability of its graph by `HullIndex.graphFormula` and of the operation as a function of the stage chart language (`Realization.realize_graphFormula_iff`, `Realization.definableFun_hullOp`), and its value in the hull of its two arguments (`Realization.hullOp_mem_finiteHull`); item 3, default value included, for embeddings of realizations (`Realization.Embedding`, an injection carrying each occurrence to an occurrence of the same type: `Realization.Embedding.hullOp_map`, `Realization.Embedding.toHullEmbedding`). Every embedding of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §5, is an embedding of realizations (`Realization.Embedding.ofEvalTrans`), and conversely between exactly consistent realizations with covering source (`Realization.Embedding.eval_trans`), where it is also an embedding of stage-chart-language structures (`Realization.Embedding.toChartEmbedding`). For an arbitrary embedding of stage-chart-language structures, item 3 is compiled only when the source has legal types (`Realization.Embedding.ofChartEmbedding`): the stage chart language has no symbol for an occurrence whose type is not legal, so such an embedding need not carry that occurrence to an occurrence; without legal types it is still to be proved. Items 4 and 5 for realizations, and hull closure inside definable closure for realizations (below), are still to be proved. That stage reduction commutes with the reconstruction of partial evaluation from a structure in the hull language, with the carrier and the undefined tuples kept, is compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Reconstruction`: `reconstruct_reduce_toHullStructure_reconstruct`, for the reconstruction of any structure in the hull language and any stage that is zero or a limit, and `reconstruct_reduce_toHullStructure`, for a realization with legal types (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 11).

Item 3 does not follow from definability alone: an embedding that is not elementary need not preserve definable functions. Its proof uses that each operation, where its defining formula has a witness, is witnessed by an actual chart on the hull of its two arguments, and that such a chart is carried to a chart of the same type. The proofs of items 3–5 import neither the cardinality material (hull cardinality bounds, `TwoGeneratorCardinality`) nor the equality (1) of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md` for infinite sets. After the expansion, chart embeddings are structure embeddings and the amalgamation of charts over a common face is an ordinary amalgamation property. Cardinal bounds, uncountable maximality, the descriptive consequences, the inclusions in definable and algebraic closure, and the equality (1) for infinite sets stay downstream and optional (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §§4 and 6; `COMPANIONS.md`).

**Hull closure inside definable closure.** In the full stage chart language, the geometric hull closure of finite sets is contained in model-theoretic definable closure: each point of the hull of a finite set is defined over two of its points by the explicit unique-coordinate formula of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §1, whose solution is unique by the two-charts theorem. For finite charts it is compiled in this repository (theorem named), `StageType.definable₁_singleton_of_mem_hull` (`Language/HullDefinability`), under the legality of the restriction to the hull, a hypothesis that cannot be dropped (`Language/HullDefinabilityExamples`: a stage type on four points that is not legal, with a point of the hull of two points not definable over them); for realizations other than the face realizations of charts it is a Layer 2 statement still to be proved. It is not one of the five facts that the classical limit uses, and not a prerequisite of the main theorem. Equality with definable or algebraic closure is not asserted.

**A description of finite tuples (an interface only if a use is identified).** A finite tuple, the empty tuple and tuples with repeated coordinates included, is described by its finite hull, the chart type of that hull, and the coordinate map from the tuple into the hull's coordinates. The description is canonical only up to reindexing of the hull, not a canonical enumeration. It is stated as an interface only if a use for it is identified; it is not mandatory, and no step above requires it.

Construct the base-stage relational language and the literal infinitary sentence \(\Phi\): the density sentence, consisting of the structural clauses (injective tuples, unique labels, exact face coherence, covering, nonemptiness) and the one-point capped-extension clause. Prove its equivalence with modelhood in both directions, round trips, and isomorphism preservation/reflection. The density sentence is the preferred presentation of the sentence of the main theorem. Prove the required fidelity theorem: \(\Phi\) is equivalent to the four-family sentence, the sentence expressing the structural clauses and the four extension families of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1], clause 4 (generalized saturation (a), with the general family (a)i and the bottom patterns (a)ii; uniformity (b); high-arity dominance (c)); this equivalence is part of the completion criterion. Its direction from the four-family sentence to the density sentence uses the finite-cut receiving statement, (R1) of the table of Layer 3; that statement therefore remains a prerequisite of the completion criterion even though the main theorem is stated for the density sentence. Its direction from the density sentence to the four-family sentence uses the cap-to-model theorem of Layer 3 (3.4), which needs only the coatom extension construction; that theorem is proved with the fidelity theorem, before its second use in Layer 4. Encode bottom-pattern parameters through their finite observational quotient; do not quantify over an uncountable collection of labellings in the sentence. Prove there are no finite models.

Derive the partial realization of a finite chart from the chart rather than storing two synchronized copies, and distinguish a supported invisible face from an unsupported tuple; the finite structure of a chart (item 5) is this partial realization. The countable chain construction on finite master conditions is not the construction of the top-free witnesses; its place is described in the section on the top-free witnesses, and where it is used it absorbs roots before request classification and keeps its finite extension hypotheses explicit.

**The templates of [AFK26] and the stage types here.** The Lean convention is a scheme with fixed coded rows together with a separate lawful labelling of its cells: a `StageType` is its `Scheme` (cells, scopes, grades, and rows) and its `label`. Stage reduction (`StageType.reduce`: labels at or above the stage become top) acts on the labelling only and leaves the rows fixed. [AFK26] instead applies its stage operation (which it calls truncation) to every row entry of a template; that is a different operation unless a correspondence between the two is supplied. The translation keeps four notions apart: a **frame** (finitely many cells, with scopes and positive grades), a **template** (a frame with its fixed coded rows), a **labelled template** (a template with a lawful labelling \(p\)), and a type at stage \(\alpha\) (a labelled template whose labels are bottom, top, or below \(\alpha\)), with stage reduction acting by \((t,p)\mapsto(t,\operatorname{red}_\alpha\circ p)\). Three further points of the translation:

- **The finite cell set.** The cells of a scheme form a finite set (`Fin card`). Countably many cells would already allow continuum many labellings at stage \(\omega\), and the countability of each level uses the finiteness. Grades are positive; admitting grade zero would change the combinatorics.
- **Cap versus reduction to top.** A cap \(x\mapsto\min(x,\beta)\) takes the value \(\beta\), which is not in the alphabet \(\{\bot\}\cup\beta\cup\{\top\}\) of stage \(\beta\). Stage reduction sends the labels at or above the stage to top; a cap is an observation (\(\operatorname{obs}_c\), Layer 3) or a lawful capping at a self-visible cap, never a stage reduction.
- **The visibility map.** It has two parameters, a threshold \(k\) and a value \(i\): it sends \(\omega\cdot\alpha+n\) to \(\omega\cdot\alpha+i\) if \(n<k\) and to \(\omega\cdot\alpha+n\) if \(n\ge k\), and fixes bottom and top (`Ordinal.visibilityReplace k i`, `Label/OrdinalVisibility`, and `Label.visibilityReplace k i`, `Label/Visibility`). It keeps the block of its argument. Its case \(i=k\) sends \(\omega\cdot\alpha+n\) to \(\omega\cdot\alpha+\max(k,n)\), the least label at or above the given one that is self-visible at grade \(k\), in the same block; a label is self-visible at grade \(k\) exactly when this case fixes it. The display of the map in [AFK26] is not compared here; its comparison belongs to the concordance (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Manuscript concordance").

**Manuscript correspondence.** Items 1 (template correspondence, with the index translation) and 2 (the complete diagram round trip) of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)" belong to this layer.

**Summit 2:** an actual sentence, and the finite charts at every stage presented as the finite substructures of the definitional expansion by the hull operations, with the operations preserved by embeddings, default values included, and closed sets carried to closed sets. An abstract realization calculus is not a substitute for syntax correspondence.

### Layer 3 — Finite extension constructions, realization over the root, and recovery of donor labels

This layer is the specification of the finite constructions. It defines the vocabulary that the other layers, `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, and `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md` use for them, and it consists of four items, the checkpoints of this layer: the statements of the finite extension constructions, with the shared decoding lemma that supplies their lawful sections (3.1); one occurrence followed by labelled evaluation (3.2); `Correct`, LOW, and the recovery statements (3.3); and the table of extension statements with their first uses (3.4). `IMPLEMENTATION.md` places these items in its checkpoint order.

**Vocabulary.** Fix a limit stage \(\alpha\) and a realization \(R\) at \(\alpha\). In the applications \(R\) is a model in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1] or, in (R4) of the table in 3.4, the structural stable candidate at \(\alpha+\omega\) (Layer 4); stage types and face maps are those of [Kni26, Definitions 3.1.1 and 3.1.5]. A label is bottom \(\bot\), an ordinal, or the formal top \(\top\). For natural numbers \(i\le N\), the visibility replacement \(\operatorname{vr}_N(x,i)\) of [Kni26, Definition 2.2.3] is \(\mu+i\) when \(x=\mu+j\) with \(\mu\) zero or a limit and \(j<N\), and \(x\) otherwise; it fixes bottom and top. A label \(x\) is **self-visible at grade \(N\)** when \(\operatorname{vr}_N(x,N)=x\), that is, when \(x\) is bottom, top, or an \(N\)-times successor [Kni26, Lemma 2.2.4]. The observation of a labelling \(q\) at a cap \(c\) is \(\operatorname{obs}_c(q)=(x\mapsto\min(q(x),c))\), on every cell \(x\).

- **Permitted cutoffs and self-visible caps** are different notions and keep different names. A **permitted cutoff** at stage \(\alpha\) is a label \(c\) with \(\bot<c<\alpha\); ordinal zero is one. Receiving and the density sentence quantify over permitted cutoffs. A **self-visible cap** at grade \(j\) is a label self-visible at \(j\); bottom and top are self-visible caps at every grade, and ordinal zero is not one at any grade \(j\ge1\). Bountifulness [Kni26, Definition 2.5.14, clause 7] and lawful capping [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.8] concern self-visible caps. A permitted cutoff need not be self-visible, and each construction passes explicitly from the cutoff to an actual cut or to a self-visible cap above it (3.4).
- A **root** is an actual occurrence of \(R\): a literal injective tuple \(t\) of some length \(n\ge0\) together with its evaluated type \(p\). The **empty root** (\(n=0\)) is the base case of every statement below; a statement that needs a root of positive length says so and treats the empty root separately (3.4).
- A **donor type** over the root is a stage type \(d\) with a face embedding \(f\) of the root's coordinates into those of \(d\) along which \(d\) restricts literally to \(p\); in the table of 3.4 it is a **one-point coface** of \(p\), a stage type on \(n+1\) points whose restriction to the first \(n\) is \(p\), unless stated otherwise. The occurrence supplying these values, an actual occurrence of type \(d\) in \(R\) or in another realization, is the **donor occurrence**.
- **Receiving** over the root at a permitted cutoff \(c\) is a fresh point \(y\) such that \(t^\frown y\) is an actual occurrence of \(R\) whose type is on the donor's scheme and has the donor's observation at the cutoff, \(\operatorname{obs}_c(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y))=\operatorname{obs}_c(d)\); it is **exact** receiving when \(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y)=d\). \(R\) has the **finite-cut receiving property** when every root, every one-point donor type over it, and every permitted cutoff admit receiving; the density sentence asserts this. Receiving at a cutoff is weaker than exact receiving, and it never yields equality at the formal top (3.3). Two statements of this property are kept apart. For the top-free witnesses it is compiled in this repository (theorem named), for the reconstruction of an ultrahomogeneous structure whose age is the age of top-free charts (`hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct`, step 6 of the section on the top-free witnesses), from the age equality, ultrahomogeneity, and capping, with no model clause; such a structure, the classical limit, exists under `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` (step 3). For every model it is (R1) of the table in 3.4, still to be proved (compiled in this repository (theorem named) conditional on the gated pinned extension property, the table in 3.4); condition 3 of the reduction to expansion domains (through the one-block transfer of Layer 5), the direction from the four-family sentence to the density sentence at stage \(\omega\) (the fidelity theorem of Layer 2), and (R2), whose LOW construction takes its occurrence from (R1), need it. The first does not give the second: a model, or a model expansion, need not be a Fraïssé limit. The property descends along stage reduction, one permitted cutoff at a time, between stages that are zero or limits (`HasFiniteCutReceiving.reduce`, `Realization/Receiving`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); this is not exact projected receiving (3.3).
- **Receiving for finite extensions.** An all-finite-extension presentation of the finite-cut receiving property, compiled in this repository (`HasFiniteCutReceiving.hasFiniteExtensionReceiving`, `Realization/Receiving`): for every root, every donor type over it with any finite number of new points, and every permitted cutoff, there is an extension of the root by fresh points that is one actual occurrence with the donor's observation at the cutoff. It is equivalent to the one-point presentation (`hasFiniteExtensionReceiving_iff`). The many-point form implies the one-point form at once. The converse proceeds one point at a time along a chain of visible faces of the donor's plan at an auxiliary cap. The bounded-observation lifting of Layer 1 is available at caps self-visible at the target grade, not at every permitted cutoff, so for a requested cutoff \(c\) the chain is run at an auxiliary ordinal \(c'\) chosen strictly between the requested cutoff and the stage, \(c<c'<\alpha\) (a permitted cutoff, so never the formal top), self-visible at the donor's arity \(k\). Such a \(c'\) is given by `Label.exists_lt_lt_isSelfVisible (hβ : Order.IsSuccPrelimit β) (ho : o < β) (k : ℕ) : ∃ c : Ordinal, o < c ∧ c < β ∧ IsSelfVisible k (c : Label)` (`Label/Visibility`, namespace `Label`), applied with \(o:=c\), with witness \(c'=c+(k+1)\). Its hypotheses hold here only because (i) the requested cutoff \(c\) is an ordinal below the stage (it is a permitted cutoff, `isPermittedCutoff_coe`), and (ii) the stage is zero or a limit (`Order.IsSuccPrelimit`). Each step takes a lawful coface of the actual type with the donor's observation at \(c'\); this stronger agreement at \(c'\) is preserved throughout the finite chain of extensions, after which one passes down to \(c\) by capping. The stage hypothesis (ii) stays in the statement. At stage zero there are no permitted cutoffs, so that case is vacuous; it is not a source of a witness. No generalization to successor stages follows from this argument. The hypotheses of the converse are exact consistency of the realization and (ii); no countability is used, so it covers stage \(\omega\), where the cutoffs are natural numbers, and the stages \(\lambda_\xi\) (all limits). The quantifier order is kept: for each donor and each permitted cutoff there exists an extension, and different cutoffs may use different extensions.
- The **private context** of a receiving construction is an actual occurrence of \(R\) containing the root as a literal face. It is acquired in the model by uniformity, high-arity dominance, and generalized saturation ([Kni26, Definition 3.2.1], clauses 4(b), 4(c), and 4(a)i), as in [Kni26, Lemma 8.1.1], clauses 3 and 4, with its threshold above a requested floor; the proof of that lemma cites high-arity dominance as clause 4(a) of Definition 3.2.1, where it is clause 4(c). In the conditional form of (R1) compiled in this repository (theorem named; the table in 3.4), the private context is acquired by uniformity and high-arity dominance, clauses 4(b) and 4(c), with exact consistency, and nothing else: no generalized saturation, no marker, and no reference cell requested for the block of the cutoff, the private cap being labelled above the cutoff (`Realization.IsModel.exists_privateContext`, `Realization/PrivateContext`, and its anchored form `Realization.IsModel.exists_privateContext_isAnchored`, in the same module). Its type carries the cells the construction reads: the **private cap** \(C\), a cell of full scope whose grade \(N\), the **threshold**, exceeds the arity of the donor; the **marker**, a cell below \(C\); and the **reference cells**, cells below \(C\), one for each block \([\mu,\mu+\omega)\) (\(\mu\) zero or a limit) that contains a proper donor label, labelled \(\mu+k\) with \(k<N\) (in (R4) this is the stable value, and the reference of the block \(\alpha\) is the marker itself). Its **actual cut** is the largest of the ordinals \(\mu+N\) over these blocks; in (R1) the block of the requested cutoff is also requested, so that the actual cut lies above the cutoff. In (R1) the reference cells are the **anchors** of the row of the gate: a proper donor label \(\mu+i\) outside the root, with finite part \(i\le N\), is read by that row as the visibility replacement \(\operatorname{vr}_N(\cdot,i)\) of its entry at a private cell of the block \(\mu\) whose label has finite part strictly below \(N\) (so is not self-visible at \(N\)) and is at most the label of the private cap (the anchor of that label); a root or private cell already in the block serves as one. The marker is not used in (R1); it is read by `Correct` (3.3). The gate (below) is a cell of the constructed scheme, not of the private context.
- The **master chart** of a construction is the given finite chart that it extends: the private context, the chart of a root in the classical limit (R5), or, in the retained chain construction, the master chart of a condition. Every row, occurrence, and label of the master chart is retained literally.
- **Padding** enlarges the arity of the constructed chart by coordinates that carry prescribed labels and no request. **Freshness**: designated coordinates are new points, every cell whose support meets one of them is a new cell of the construction, and new cells are not identified with old cells merely because their roles or observations agree.
- The **bottom pattern** of a labelling records which cells are labelled bottom, as in the bottom-pattern family of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1], clause 4(a)ii. That clause tests the pattern only on the cells of grade at most \(n\), where \(n\) is the arity of the occurrence being extended (the set \(D^{\le n}\) of the clause); a cell that a construction needs to be non-bottom on the realized occurrence (the gate of (R1)) must have grade at most \(n\). A **literal-top prescription** requires given cells to carry the formal top itself, not a large proper value.
- A labelling is **orderly** when each value is self-visible at the grade of its cell [Kni26, Definition 2.3.4]. The rows \(E(\Sigma)\) of a scheme are orderly [Kni26, Definition 2.5.3], and a labelling is **lawful** when it is orderly and respects the rows [Kni26, Definition 2.5.4]: **locality**, the row \(E(\Sigma)\) transforms in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 2.3.9] to the labelling of the cells below \(\Sigma\) capped at its value at \(\Sigma\); and **availability**, clause 2 there.
- A **legal scheme** is a domain in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 2.6.1]: a scheme whose rows are consistent [Kni26, Definition 2.5.12], bountiful [Kni26, Definition 2.5.14], and complete [Kni26, Definition 2.5.15] (every graded face carries a cell; completeness is defined for bountiful rows), and coded. **Coding** is the range normalization of the rows: every row entry is bottom or an ordinal \(\omega\cdot i+j\) with \(i,j\) natural numbers, that is, an ordinal below \(\omega^2\). Legality imposes it as a condition, checked on the rows each construction builds; it is not derived from [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.13], whose offset bound is not correct as stated (Layer 1). A construction that also needs the bound \(j\le g+1\) on the entries of a row of grade \(g\) imposes that bound on its constructed rows; it is not part of legality. The range clause of [AFK26, Definition 4.27] (clause 4: entries \(\omega\cdot n+i\) with \(i\le g\)) is an offset bound of the same kind, one tighter, and is not part of legality either: the one-point scheme `CodingExamples.pointRow 2`, with row value \(2\) at a cell of grade \(1\), separates the two bounds, being legal, within \(j\le g+1\) (`Scheme.isStronglyCoded_pointRow_two`), and outside clause 4 (`Scheme.not_hasPrintedRange_pointRow_two`). No compiled construction uses that clause; it occurs only in the concordance (`Correspondence/Current/LegalTemplate`, concordance row 45), and legality is not strengthened to include it (checkpoint 2.2). The recoverability that Definition 2.6.1 also asks for, that each cell determines its graded face and the plan, cells, and rows below it, holds by representation: the rows are part of the data of a scheme. In Lean legality is the predicate `Scheme.IsLegal`, whose coding clause `Scheme.IsCoded` is exactly the range normalization. A **legal stage type** is a stage type on a legal scheme; the relation symbols of Layer 2 are indexed by legal stage types.
- The **fields** of a construction are the cells of the private scheme and of the donor scheme, together with one cutoff field in the LOW construction; a **state vector** assigns a label to each field. Each construction defines when a vector is **admitted** at grade \(j\): its private and donor parts are lawful on the cells of grade at most \(j\), they agree on the root, every field of grade greater than \(j\) is self-visible at grade 1, and the construction's own condition holds (`Correct` in the growth construction of (R3) and (R4), once \(j\ge N\) and the private part lies in the prescribed bottom class of 3.2; the LOW forcing in the LOW construction, 3.3). The **catalogue** at grade \(j\) is the finite set of canonical admitted vectors at grade \(j\): normal forms, without top, whose values lie in a finite set fixed by \(j\) and the number of fields. Every admitted vector at grade \(j\) is the image of a catalogue vector under a **decoder**, a witness bounded by grade \(j\) (3.1) that sends a catalogue value to bottom only if it is bottom. The **future fields** at grade \(j\) are the fields of grade greater than \(j\); every replacement of an admitted vector at grade \(j\) retains them literally.
- **Owners**, **short** and **long-row** owners, **ownerwise decoding**, **long-row locality**, and the **section theorem** are defined in 3.1.
- The vocabulary of checkpoint 2.4 (3.1, (R6)). The **grade cut** of a scheme at a grade \(g\) consists of its cells of grade at most \(g\), with their rows read literally. A **source prefix** of a scheme below a graded face \(Z\) is a lower embedding of a smaller scheme (its source) over the same points, keeping scopes, whose image contains every cell below \(Z\). The **splice** of two labellings at a grade \(j\) is the first on the cells of grade at most \(j\) and the second above; it is a different operation from the splice of two witnesses (3.1, the transformation lemma). The **lift at the lower grade** of a lift from \((C,j+1)\) to \((B,j+1)\) is the capped lift from \((C,j)\) to \((B,j)\). The **owner of a prescription** below a graded face \(X\) is an owner of graded index \(X\) whose label is the largest among the prescribed cells of the grade of \(X\). An **owner-capped lift** is a lawful labelling below the target pair that reads the prescription capped at the owner label below the source pair and keeps the observation of the ambient labelling at the cap at every cell. A **lawful companion** of a labelling \(\nu\circ r\) is a lawful labelling bottom exactly where \(\nu\circ r\) is; for \(r\) lawful and \(\nu\) a witness bounded by a grade bounding the cells, \(\nu\circ r\) is lawful exactly when it has a lawful companion, so its bottom pattern is the only obstruction. The **flattened source** of a labelling with values in a finite set \(V\), at a grade \(m\), is the flattening of finite parts at \(m\) of its strongly coded normal form at \(m\); it is the **coded source section** of the owner alignment, lawful, short at \(m\), and never the formal top. For the alignment of owners, fix an owner \(o\) of grade \(m\), maximal among the cells below it, a lawful prescription \(p\) below \(o\), and a cap \(\gamma\) self-visible at \(m\) with \(\bot<\gamma<p(o)\). A **source** below \(o\) is a lawful labelling \(s\) of the cells below \(o\), short at \(m\) (every finite part at most \(m\)), with \(s(o)\ne\top\), together with a witness \(\tau\) bounded by grade \(m\) with \(\tau\le\gamma\) and \(\tau\circ s=\min(p,\gamma)\); in the one-grade step it is the row of the serving cell (below). A cell \(e\) is **saturated** when \(\tau(s(e))=\gamma\). The **strip** of a label \(\omega b+i\) at grade \(m\) is the band \([\omega b,\omega b+m]\) at the start of its block. The **source cap** \(h\) is the least value of \(\operatorname{vr}_m(s(e),m)\) over the saturated cells. The owner-local alignment gives a **reading cap** \(\delta\), self-visible at \(m\) with \(\gamma\le\delta\le p(o)\), and a witness \(\rho\) bounded by grade \(m\), the **alignment decoder**, with \(\rho(h)=\delta\), \(\min(p(e),\delta)=\min(\rho(s(e)),\delta)\) at every cell, and \(h\le s(e)\) at every cell with \(\delta<\min(p(e),p(o))\); when some cell with \(\gamma<\min(p(e),p(o))\) has its source below \(h\), the alignment decoder reads the strip of \(h\) through the capped witness of the locality of \(p\) after the **block shift**, the translation of that strip onto the strip that the locality of \(s\) carries onto it. For \(\mu\) zero or a limit above \(h\) and a finite set \(V\) of labels, the **tail code** of a label \(y\) is \(\mu+\operatorname{strongEncode}_{V,m}(y)\), the translation by \(\mu\) of a strongly coded code, which need not be strongly coded itself; the **tail decoder** is bottom below \(\mu\) and decodes the rest after removing \(\mu\); the **high code** of \(y\) is bottom when \(y\le\delta\) and its tail code otherwise. The **aligned encoding** at a cell \(d\) is \(\max(\min(s(d),h),\text{the high code of }\min(p(d),p(o)))\), and the **extended decoder** \(x\mapsto\max(\min(\rho(x),\delta),\text{the tail decoder at }x)\) reads it literally as \(\min(p,p(o))\). In the one-grade step from \((C,j+1)\) to \((B,j+1)\), the **serving cell** of a lift is a cell of graded index \((B,j+1)\) at which the ambient labelling reaches the cap (availability), and its row is the source. The **lift at an ambient** \(S\) (`CappedLiftAt`) is the capped lift whose ambient is \(S\), at positive caps and for prescriptions never the formal top. The **boundary** is the set of cells below \((B,j+1)\) that lie below one of the two coatoms \(U\), \(V\) at grade \(j+1\), the cells of the amalgam; the rows **extend from the boundary** at a cap \(h\) along \(S\) (`ExtendsFromBoundary`) when every labelling lawful below \(U\) and below \(V\) that agrees with \(S\) capped at \(h\) on the boundary extends, unchanged there, to a labelling lawful below \((B,j+1)\) that agrees with \(S\) capped at \(h\) everywhere.
- A **display** is a lawful labelling of a constructed scheme, constructed as data, whose restrictions along the private and donor embeddings are literally the private labels and the donor labels. (R1) and (R2) use one (3.2); (R3) and (R4) use none.
- The **gate** of the ordinary construction (R1) is a cell of the constructed scheme of graded index \((\mathrm{univ},n)\), on the \(n+1\) points of that scheme, \(n\) the private arity; in (R1) the private cap has full scope and full grade, so the grade of the gate is the threshold \(N=n\); the other cells of that graded index are its **twins**. The display labels the gate non-bottom (not necessarily top) and its twins bottom; the **gate equation** records that the gate is not bottom on the realized occurrence. The recovery statement of (R1) reads the bottom pattern of the whole graded index of the gate, and the lower bound on the gate comes from availability against the private cap (3.3). `Correct` and LOW have no gate.
- The **coatom extension construction** takes stage types on \(A\setminus\{a\}\) and \(A\setminus\{b\}\) that agree on \(A\setminus\{a,b\}\) (a closed face of both) and returns a stage type on \(A\) with exactly these two faces and with a cell of full scope and full grade whose label is the maximum of its labels, the apex form (3.1, (R6)) of [Kni26, Corollary 4.3.22]. The bountifulness of its rows is proved for the construction chosen in 3.1 (R6), not taken from [Kni26, Lemma 4.3.20], whose statement concerns the completion of Definition 4.3.14 and is unproved (not refuted).
- The **amalgam** of two coatom types, the **coatom extension property** at a stage, its **apex form**, and a **seed** of its completion are defined in 3.1 (R6); the coatom extension construction is a proof of the apex form.
