- A **pinned extension** of a chart is an extension whose restriction to that chart is literally the chart, labels included; it is **top-free** when no label is the formal top. The **exact pinned extension** is (R6) of the table in 3.4.
- **Restriction-compatible labellings** are defined in 3.2; `Correct`, LOW, and the recovery statements in 3.3; the cap-to-model theorem and the comparisons named in the table in 3.4.

#### 3.1. Statements of the finite extension constructions, and the section theorem

For each finite extension construction, state separately:

- **input data:** the master chart and the root in it, the donor type with its face embedding, the permitted cutoff, the padding length, the designated fresh coordinates, the bottom pattern, the literal-top prescriptions, and the cells of the private context that the construction reads (private cap, marker, reference cells);
- **compatibility conditions:** the donor type restricts literally to the root's type; the private context contains the root as a literal face, and the two restrictions to the root agree cell by cell; the threshold exceeds the donor's arity and every requested offset; the bottom pattern and the literal-top prescriptions are consistent with the donor type;
- **constructed finite object:** a legal scheme (plan, cells, grades, rows) with embeddings of the master chart and of the donor scheme and, where the statement uses one, a display; all of these are data, not propositions asserting existence;
- **literal equations:** the private type and the donor type are the literal ordered face-map restrictions of the constructed scheme: its restriction along the private embedding is the master chart's scheme and its restriction along the donor embedding is the donor's scheme, with the same cells in the same order and the same rows, not merely the same lawful labellings; the root is their literal common face; the display restricts literally to the private labels and to the donor labels, and has the prescribed bottom pattern and literal tops; the designated fresh coordinates are new.

Each statement is proved:

1. for every root size, including the empty root, and every padding length, including zero; where a construction needs a root of positive length, its statement in 3.4 gives the base case of the empty root;
2. with designated freshness and literal retention of the root and of every row, occurrence, and label of the master chart;
3. with bottom patterns and literal-top prescriptions given separately: neither is derived from the other, and realizing a bottom pattern does not realize literal tops;
4. with legality of the constructed scheme, its bountifulness proved at every self-visible cap of the target grade, each lift preserving the observation at its cap on every cell of the target, including the auxiliary cells the construction introduces and the future fields, even where the recovery statement that uses the construction ignores them; and with the future fields retained literally by every replacement of an admitted vector.

**Per-cap lifting, not simultaneous preservation.** Per-cap lifting is bountifulness [Kni26, Definition 2.5.14]: for graded faces \(X\preceq Y\) of the scheme, with \(j\) the grade of \(Y\), every cap \(c\) self-visible at \(j\) (clause 7: \(c=\operatorname{vr}_j(c,j)\)), and every lawful labelling \(q\) of the cells below \(Y\), every lawful labelling \(p\) of the cells below \(X\) with \(\operatorname{obs}_c(p)=\operatorname{obs}_c(q|_X)\) extends to a lawful labelling \(q'\) below \(Y\) with \(q'|_X=p\) literally and \(\operatorname{obs}_c(q')=\operatorname{obs}_c(q)\). At a cap that is not self-visible at \(j\) the lift is not available in general: [Kni26, §2.5] notes that \(q\) may then provide extra information about \(p\). Simultaneous preservation, one extension of \(p\) with \(\operatorname{obs}_c(q')=\operatorname{obs}_c(q)\) at every compatible self-visible cap \(c\), is not proved and is not needed. It does not follow from per-cap lifting through a largest compatible cap. When \(p\) and \(q|_X\) differ, let \(m\) be the least value of \(\min(p(x),q(x))\) over the cells \(x\) where they differ; the compatible caps are the caps self-visible at \(j\) and at most \(m\), and they need not have a maximum. At target grade 3 with \(m=\omega\cdot2+1\) they are bottom and the ordinals \(i\) and \(\omega+i\) with \(3\le i<\omega\); with \(p(x)=\omega+1\) and \(q(x)=\omega+2\) they are bottom and the natural numbers \(i\ge3\). When the compatible caps do have a maximum, a lift at it preserves every compatible cap, because caps nest under \(\min\); when \(p=q|_X\), \(q\) itself preserves every cap. The only known route to simultaneous preservation in general is through injectivity of restriction (uniqueness of extension), which is not available (Layer 0). The main theorem needs only per-cutoff extension: density is \(\forall\) root, \(\forall\) donor, \(\forall\) cutoff, \(\exists\) extension, and different cutoffs may use different extensions.

**The attained least lift** (a target of the finite lifting statements of Layers 1–3; prospective here: no statement of this paragraph or of the next three is a theorem of this repository unless one is named). The finite lifting statements are organized around one target. Its cell-wise ingredients, cited below, are compiled in `Stage/Threshold`, a module of Layer 1; the paragraph stands here with the finite lifting statements (the section "Endpoints and shared foundations", "The finite lifting boundary"). Fix a limit stage \(\beta\) and a stage type \(q\) at \(\beta\); the target is stated for \(q\) legal (`StageType.IsLegal`). A **lift** of \(q\) is a stage type at \(\beta+\omega\) on the unchanged scheme and rows of \(q\) whose stage reduction to \(\beta\) is \(q\): a labelling of the cells of \(q\), lawful for its rows, with labels below \(\beta+\omega\) or the formal top, equal to the label of \(q\) at every cell where that label is below \(\beta\), and at least \(\beta\) at every cell labelled the formal top in \(q\). So the lifts of \(q\) are the lawful lifts of the capped prescription \(\operatorname{obs}_\beta(q)\), read at \(\beta\), a permitted cutoff at the stage \(\beta+\omega\) that is not a cap self-visible at any positive grade (so bountifulness supplies no lift at it). The labels below \(\beta\) are the same in every lift. At a cell labelled the formal top, the minimum over the lifts is attained, separately for each cell: for a rooted cover \((q,f)\) of a root \(p\) and a cell \(d\) labelled the formal top in \(p\), some lift of \(q\) carries on its face along \(f\), at \(d\), the label \(\beta+o\), where \(o\in\mathbb N_\infty\) is the provisional offset of \(d\) at \((q,f)\), the formal top when \(o=\top\) (`StageType.exists_lift_label_eq_ofOffset` with `Label.ofOffset`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), for \(\beta\) zero or a limit). *The target* (prospective): for a **legal** stage type \(q\) at \(\beta\), **one** lift \(q_{\min}\) of \(q\) attains these minima at all cells simultaneously, so that \(q_{\min}\le Q\) pointwise for every lift \(Q\) of \(q\): one lawful labelling of the lifted cover, the attained least lawful lift. Legality is an explicit hypothesis of the target, not one recorded in passing: the availability law is existential, so the cell-wise minima of the lifts of an arbitrary stage type need not be attained by one lawful labelling, and the target is not stated without it. The threshold forced by a rooted cover is then identified by testing against that one attained least lawful lift, not by combining witnesses chosen separately at the cells, and the threshold characterization and the limit-stage monotonicity below are derived from it. This does not validate an ordering of twins fixed in advance: the twin-ordering hypothesis is refuted (`Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_twinOrdering_blockStage`, compiled in this repository (theorem named); "Least, not unique" below). Its stage hypothesis, a limit stage \(\beta\) with the lifts at \(\beta+\omega\), is the one under which it is presently formulated, and it is recorded on every statement derived from it (the section "Endpoints and shared foundations", "The finite lifting boundary"). Two statements are derived from it, each with that stage hypothesis (prospective in their derived form), each in two forms: a per-cover form, at one rooted cover and its extensions, which belongs to Layers 1–3, and a supremum form, through the stable offset and the stable label of `Continuation/Normalization`, which belongs to Layer 4:

- **(a) The limit-stage monotonicity** (the monotonicity of thresholds along extensions of rooted covers, at a limit stage). *Per-cover form* (Layers 1–3): the least lifts, read on the face of the root, only increase along extensions of rooted covers (the provisional offsets do, `StageType.provisionalOffset_le_trans_face`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). *Supremum form* (Layer 4): the label at the next block stage is their supremum: the stable label of a cell (`Realization.stableLabel`) is \(\operatorname{ofOffset}_\beta\) of the supremum in \(\mathbb N_\infty\) (`Realization.stableOffset`), over the rooted covers, of the offsets of their least lifts; and the label of an exactly consistent realization at the next block stage, with legal types, whose reduction is the given realization, is its stable label (`Realization.label_eq_stableLabel`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on finite-extension receiving, from (R1), and on `ForcingDonors`, both still to be proved). The compiled definitions read provisional offsets, which are the offsets of the least lifts once the target is proved; no compiled statement mentions a least lift.
- **(b) The threshold characterization.** *Per-cover form* (Layers 1–3): for a rooted cover \((q,f)\) of a root \(p\), a cell \(d\) labelled the formal top in \(p\), and \(n\in\mathbb N\), three statements are equivalent: \((q,f)\) forces the threshold \(n\) at \(d\) (`StageType.ForcesThreshold`); every lift of \(q\) is at least \(\beta+n\) at \(d\) on its face along \(f\) (the definition of forcing); the least lift is. *Supremum form* (Layer 4): through the supremum, \(n\) is at most the stable offset exactly when the least lift of some rooted cover is at least \(\beta+n\) at \(d\), the form with least lifts of `Realization.natCast_le_stableOffset_iff` (compiled in this repository (theorem named), with forcing in place of the least lift). The case of one cell already follows from the attainment cell by cell above; the target adds that the thresholds of all cells of a cover are read off one lawful labelling.

**The monotonicity for an arbitrary second stage is kept.** The monotonicity of forcing along extensions (`StageType.ForcesThreshold.trans_face`) and of provisional offsets (`StageType.provisionalOffset_le_trans_face`), compiled in this repository (theorem named), hold for an arbitrary second stage \(\alpha\) in place of \(\beta+\omega\), with only the hypothesis that \(\beta\) is zero or a limit. They are not replaced by the forms derived from the least lift, which are stated at \(\alpha=\beta+\omega\) under the limit-stage hypothesis, until the cases of an arbitrary second stage are handled by the least lift as well.

**Exact lifting over a separately prescribed higher root is a separate statement, and the least lift is not simultaneous preservation.** The least lift lifts one lower type on its own scheme. The two-face lift `2FL(j)` (`Seed.TwoFaceLift`, defined in this repository) and its existential form `2FL∃(j)` (`Seed.TwoFaceLiftExists`, defined in this repository; 3.1, (R6), 2.6–2.7) lift a face prescription across one coatom while a labelling of the other coatom is prescribed separately; they are not derived from the least lift, and the least lift is not derived from them. The capped lift (`CellScheme.Rows.CappedLift`) gives existence separately for each ambient labelling and each self-visible cap, and the one-grade lift `CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundaries_short` (compiled in this repository (theorem named)) uses one boundary triple at the cap \(\bot\) and another at the positive caps; a single lift preserving every compatible cap at once is the stronger statement of the paragraph "Per-cap lifting, not simultaneous preservation" above (prospective, and not needed). The least lift is one labelling minimal at every cell among the lifts of one prescribed observation, not one labelling serving every cap.

**Least, not unique** (a caution on the finite lifts). Literal weakening (3.3, "Lifting over a root of small fixing rank": the lower type read at the higher stage, `StageType.castLE`, every label kept, the formal top included) is a lift (`StageType.reduce_castLE` with `StageType.reduce_self`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and it is the **greatest** lift, since every lift agrees with \(q\) below \(\beta\) and the formal top is the largest label (prospective as a named statement). Above a stage \(\beta\) that is zero or a limit, with the whole block \([\beta,\beta+\omega)\) of labels available, the set of lifts of \(q\) is a singleton exactly when \(q\) is top-free (`StageType.IsTopFree`) (prospective): a top-free type has only its literal weakening, and a type with a cell labelled the formal top also has the lift capped at \(\beta+K\), with \(K\) the largest grade of such a cell, which differs from literal weakening there (`StageType.capLift`, defined in this repository, a lift by `StageType.capLift_reduce`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). So "least lift" is never read as "unique lift". Nor is a pointwise minimum of lawful lifts lawful in general: on the legal type of the twin-ordering special case (`Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_twinOrdering_blockStage`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), the lifts \((\beta+2,\bot,\beta+2,\beta+1,\bot)\) and \((\beta+2,\bot,\beta+1,\beta+2,\bot)\) order the twins oppositely, and their pointwise minimum \((\beta+2,\bot,\beta+1,\beta+1,\bot)\) puts the cell of smaller scope above both twins, against availability (informal; not compiled as a separate statement), as for the printed [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.11], which is not relied on (Layer 1). On that type the least lift exists nonetheless, \((\beta+1,\bot,\beta+1,\beta+1,\bot)\) (informal): the target is an attained minimum, not the pointwise minimum of chosen lifts. So the least and the greatest lift of \(q\), where the least exists, agree exactly when \(q\) is top-free, and between them lie lifts that need not be comparable. Placement: the greatest lift and the singleton criterion belong to Layers 1–3 (`Stage/`); the negative special case on the twin-ordering type, whose scheme is the five-cell scheme defined in `Continuation/CandidateCounterexamples` (`fiveCells`, `fiveCellRows`, `fiveCellScheme`), is to be compiled in that module, beside the refutation of twin ordering, and belongs to Layer 4.

**The section theorem and the shared decoding lemma.** Ownerwise decoding is an explicit target, proved once. It belongs to the constructions: it supplies lawful sections of the constructed schemes in the bountifulness proofs of this item, and it is not a recovery statement.

A **witness** in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 2.3.9] is a pair \(\langle g,\sigma\rangle\) of a **suppressor** \(g\) (a non-increasing function from grades to labels, each value self-visible at its grade) and a **shifter** \(\sigma\) (a map on labels) satisfying clauses 3–5 there. Let \(g_K\) be the suppressor that is top at the grades at most \(K\) and bottom above. A value map \(\nu\) on labels is a **witness bounded by grade \(K\)** when \(\langle g_K,\nu\rangle\) is a witness. Spelled out: \(\nu(\bot)=\bot\); \(\nu\) is monotone; \(\nu(\operatorname{vr}_k(x,i))=\operatorname{vr}_k(\nu(x),i)\) for every label \(x\) and all \(i\le k\le K\); and, for every \(k>K\), if \(\nu(x)=\bot\) then \(\nu(\operatorname{vr}_k(x,i))=\bot\) for all \(i\le k\). The last condition is clause 5 of the definition at the grades \(k>K\), where it applies whenever \(\nu(x)\le g_K(k)=\bot\), and it does not follow from the others: for \(K=1\), the map that sends \(0\) and \(1\) to bottom and fixes every other label satisfies the first three, but \(\nu(\operatorname{vr}_2(0,2))=\nu(2)=2\neq\bot\). A label is **short** at grade \(g\) when it is bottom, top, or an ordinal whose finite part is at most \(g\). An **owner** is a cell together with its row; it is a **short owner** when every entry of its row is short at its grade, and a **long-row owner** otherwise.

**Statement.** Let \(r\) be a lawful labelling (the input) of the cells of a scheme below a graded face whose cells all have grade at most \(K\), and let \(\nu\) be a witness bounded by grade \(K\), applied cellwise. Suppose that every owner \(c\) is short or satisfies the **mapped locality** \(E(c)\Rightarrow\bigl(d\mapsto\min(\nu(r(d)),\nu(r(c)))\bigr)\) on the cells \(d\) below \(c\). Then \(\nu\circ r\) is lawful. Orderliness and availability are transported through \(\nu\) once, for all cells. Locality is proved owner by owner: at a short owner by composing the input's locality with \(\nu\), which is possible because short entries are unchanged by flattening of finite parts at the owner's grade; at any other owner it is the hypothesis. The lemma assumes neither lawfulness of the decoded section, nor bottom reflection of \(\nu\), nor shortness of the inherited rows. Shortness is not a consequence of legality: coding bounds the entries only below \(\omega^2\), and even the bound \(j\le g+1\), where a construction imposes it, allows an entry whose finite part exceeds the grade by one. The new rows of a construction are short by their support, not by their coding.

**The section theorem.** In a construction with fields and a catalogue, let \(S\) be admitted at grade \(j\), and let \(a\) be a catalogue vector and \(\nu\) a decoder with \(\nu\circ a=S\). The construction defines from \(a\) a lawful **source section** \(r_a\) of the constructed scheme below grade \(j\). Its owners are of two kinds. The new rows, of full scope and grade at least 2, are short, by their support. The inherited cells carry the rows of the original schemes literally, and \(r_a\) is determined on them cell by cell by the values of \(a\) (the **exact base table**); their rows may be long. For an inherited owner, **long-row locality** is its mapped locality, obtained by transporting the lawfulness of \(\nu\circ a=S\) on the original rows along the exact base table; no bottom reflection of the decoder is used. The lemma then makes \(\nu\circ r_a\) a lawful section of the constructed scheme whose values on the original cells are those of \(S\). This is the **section theorem** of the construction, the step of the bountifulness proof that realizes an admitted vector by a section; **ownerwise decoding** is its owner-by-owner passage from the lawfulness of \(r_a\) to that of \(\nu\circ r_a\).

Both the growth construction (the legal scheme shared by (R3) and (R4) of 3.4) and the LOW construction (R2) are required applications: each is shown to satisfy the hypothesis owner by owner, its new owners by shortness and each inherited long row by long-row locality. The lemma shares the owner-by-owner argument; it does not identify the rows of the two constructions.

**A transformation lemma still to be proved in this layer.** The band rule of Layer 1 (a witness bounded by grade \(K\), followed by the band map that sends the formal top into the band \([\alpha,\alpha+K]\)) covers only the top-witness row of the proof of [Kni26, Lemma 5.3.5]: the row \(E(\Theta)\) of a full-scope cell \(\Theta\) of grade \(K\) labelled top, the **top-witness cell**, whose witness that proof composes with the band map. The row \(E(\Sigma)\) of any other top-labelled cell \(\Sigma\), of grade \(J\le K\) and below \(\Theta\), needs a **splice of two witnesses** with the same source \(E(\Sigma)\): a witness \(E(\Sigma)\Rightarrow q\), where \(q\) is the labelling of the cells below \(\Sigma\) (the locality of the labels at \(\Sigma\); its suppressor is top at the grades up to \(J\), and the values of \(q\) are below \(\alpha\) or top), and a witness \(E(\Sigma)\Rightarrow r\), where \(r(d)=\min(E(\Theta)(d),E(\Theta)(\Sigma))\) for the cells \(d\) below \(\Sigma\), the row of \(\Theta\) on those cells capped at its entry at \(\Sigma\) (a witness given by the consistency of the rows). The spliced target is \(q\) at the cells where \(q\) is below \(\alpha\) and, at the cells where \(q\) is top, the band map of \(r\) with a base \(\lambda\) (zero or a limit) that \(r\) does not fall below on those cells, and the splice asserts \(E(\Sigma)\Rightarrow\) spliced target, on cells of grade at most \(J\). It is not a general composite: each of the two regions carries its own hypotheses. The printed proof of Lemma 5.3.5 obtains these rows through the transitivity of Lemma 2.3.14, which is not correct as stated (Layer 1); the splice replaces that step. It is a statement still to be proved here; the lawfulness of the stable candidate in Layer 4 uses it.

##### (R6): the coatom extension property and the completion of the amalgam

**What is established.** Let \(A\) be a set of \(m+2\) points with distinct points \(a,b\), and let \(t_a\) and \(t_b\) be legal stage types on the coatoms \(A\setminus\{a\}\) and \(A\setminus\{b\}\) that agree literally on their common face \(A\setminus\{a,b\}\) (a closed face of both). Their **amalgam** [Kni26, Definition 4.3.1] is constructed as data: its cells and rows are those of the two schemes, the cells of the common face counted once, and its labelling is the union of the two labellings. Its rows are consistent and bountiful [Kni26, Lemma 4.3.2], and its restrictions to the two coatoms are literally \(t_a\) and \(t_b\), with the same cells in the same order, rows and labels included. It is not complete: every graded face whose scope is not \(A\) carries a cell, but no cell has scope \(A\), so the amalgam is not a legal scheme. Its cells, with their rows and labels, are the **boundary** of its completion. The exact pinned extension reduces to one statement about stage types:

- The **coatom extension property** at a stage \(\alpha\): any two legal stage types at \(\alpha\) on the two coatoms of a set of \(m+2\) points that agree literally on their common face (a closed face of both) are the literal faces, labels included, of one legal stage type on the \(m+2\) points. Its **apex form** asks in addition for a cell of full scope and full grade \(m+2\) that carries the maximum of the labels, as in [Kni26, Corollary 4.3.22]; the apex is what high-arity dominance uses [Kni26, Lemma 4.4.3], while the nonemptiness of the uniformity instances [Kni26, Lemma 4.4.2] uses the plain form. The plain form follows from the apex form by forgetting the apex. In Lean the two forms are the hypotheses `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions`.

From the plain form follow the exact pinned extension (statement (R6): one coatom extension for each point of the chart outside the face, each point chosen so that the face enlarged by it is closed), the one-point extension of every legal stage type [Kni26, Proposition 4.3.23], and the amalgamation of two legal stage types over a common face. The last, capped at a proper cap self-visible at the arity of the amalgam and above every label of the two charts, is the amalgamation of top-free charts (step 2 of the classical limit, in the section on the top-free witnesses; `StageType.exists_isTopFree_amalgam`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` at a nonzero limit stage); (R6), capped in the same way, is (R5), which, used only over a whole chart (where no coatom extension is needed), gives receiving in the classical limit (step 6 there). [Kni26, Proposition 4.3.24], for the domains that are legal schemes, needs no hypothesis: the bottom labelling of a legal scheme is a legal stage type at every stage. The exact pinned extension uses nothing about the stage; the stage enters only in the proof of the coatom extension property. What remains for (R6) is that proof, in the apex form: the completion of the amalgam by cells of full scope. Status: a completion below the full grade is compiled in this repository (theorem named) for every seed at the arities \(m\le2\), with no hypothesis on the stage (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_zero`, `Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_le_one`, `Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_le_two`), and at every arity for a seed each of whose grades \(2\le j<m\) has the two-face lift `2FL(j)` or dead old cells of grade \(j+1\) (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`; 2.6 below), a sufficient condition that does not cover every seed (refuted: `CaseSplitCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`), and for a seed each of whose grades \(2\le j<m\) has the existential two-face lift `2FL∃(j)`, the step of the tower stated exactly (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_twoFaceLiftExists`; 2.7 below). The arity-general step is open at \(m\ge3\): `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` are still to be proved, and they are not refuted. What is refuted, each at a legal seed on five points (negative special cases named), is the universal two-face lift (`TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLift`), at a seed whose completion nevertheless exists (`TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seed4`), and the universal existential two-face lift (`TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLiftExists`); at the refuting seed the invariant of the tower fails at the top grade (`TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.not_towerInvariant_top_seedL`). The identified obstruction survives the redesigns examined (argued, not formalized). The refuting seed nevertheless has a completion below the full grade outside the tower, the thin completion (`ThinCompletion.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seedL`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and with the apex added the coatom extension of its two coatom types with apex at every stage (`ThinCompletionExamples.exists_coatomExtension_seedL`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); so it is not a counterexample to `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at \(m=3\).

**Forcing donors from the plain form.** Forcing donors at \(\eta\) (`ForcingDonors`, Layer 4, output 2) follow at every input from the plain form at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\), compiled in this repository (theorem named): `forcingDonors_of_hasCoatomExtensions` (`Extension/ForcingDonorsCoatom`); hence from the apex form at every countable block stage (`forcingDonors_of_forall_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), the hypothesis under which the cap-to-model theorem and nonempty losses are compiled. The construction uses no (R1) and no completion. A legal stage type with its cells of full grade removed is legal below the full grade, with the same faces along proper faces (`StageType.IsLegal.isLegalBelowFullGrade_partBelowFullGrade`, `Extension/PartBelowFullGrade`). For an input \(t\), a cell \(d\) reducing to the formal top, and a threshold \(n\) above the grade of \(d\): a legal type \(U\) on \(n\) points with a cell \(x\) of grade \(n\) labelled \(\lambda_\eta+n\) (a one-point type with that label, extended by one-point extensions over the empty face, cut below the full grade, and closed by a tied apex at that label); the amalgam of \(t\) and \(U\) over the empty face, extended by one point and cut below the full grade; and the apex over it. The row of the apex is the coded copy of the labels, so locality at the apex gives \(\min(Q(x),Q(\mathrm{apex}))\le\min(Q(d),Q(\mathrm{apex}))\) in every lift \(Q\) (`StageType.forcesThreshold_of_row_le_of_grade_le`), and the order law at \(x\) and at the apex forces \(n\) at \(d\). The cell \(x\) need not lie above \(d\), so the inputs that no tie of full grade serves (\(\operatorname{grade}d<n\le j\le|\operatorname{scope}d|\) for the label \(\lambda_\eta+j\) of \(d\)) need no tie at an intermediate grade. Unconditionally, donors are compiled for one- and two-point inputs up to the threshold \(4\) (`forcingDonorsUpTo_one_four`, `forcingDonorsUpTo_two_four`; the two-point inputs at the threshold \(2\) through the apex row over completions at the arities \(0\) and \(1\), `exists_forcingDonor_twoPoint_le_two`); beyond, forcing donors wait for the plain form at \(m\ge3\).

**The completion: what remains, and its checkpoints.** The completion is proved in seven checkpoints, 2.1–2.7, numbered within checkpoint 2 of `IMPLEMENTATION.md`. They are checkpoints of the mathematics, and the larger ones split into several steps.

- **2.1. The seed.** Define the finite input of the completion from the amalgam: the two coatom types, their common face, and the amalgamated rows. A **seed** is that input together with the conditions it must satisfy, each stated explicitly, among them the legality of the two coatom types, the lifting properties of the amalgamated rows, and the literal restrictions to the two coatoms; the recursion of 2.6 starts from a seed. That the amalgam of two legal coatom types is a seed is proved here, from the consistency and bountifulness of its rows and its literal restrictions to the two coatoms (the library's `Coatom.isConsistent_amalgamType`, `Coatom.isBountiful_amalgamType`, `Coatom.restrictFace_left_amalgamType`, and `Coatom.restrictFace_right_amalgamType`). The **apex theorem** is stated with the conclusion of the recursion of 2.6 as a hypothesis and proved from it: the truncation to the stage (see the simplifications below), the apex, and the literal restrictions to both coatoms.
- **2.2. Coding.** The construction works under the coding of legality (row entries below \(\omega^2\), as in the definition of a legal scheme above), not under the offset bound of [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.13], the clause beginning "Indeed" (at a cell of grade \(k\), entries \(\omega\cdot i+j\) with \(j\le k+1\)), which is not correct as stated (Layer 1). Acceptance includes an input whose rows violate that bound, universe polymorphism, and the orderliness of the constructed rows derived from their consistency rather than assumed. No later checkpoint of the completion (2.3–2.7) begins until this one is settled. If the construction cannot be carried through under the weaker coding, the first indispensable use of the offset bound is identified; the legality predicate is not strengthened. The same holds for the range clause of [AFK26, Definition 4.27], one tighter than that bound (the definition of a legal scheme above; concordance row 45).
- **2.3. Transformation algebra.** Lawful transformations, normal forms, and coded encoders (defined with 2.3) and the decoders of the vocabulary above. The numerical decoding identities are stated and proved separately from lawfulness and from cap preservation.
- **2.4. Lifting and alignment.** Carrying lawful sections across grade cuts (the restrictions to the cells of grade at most a given grade) and source prefixes (defined with 2.4), the alignment of owners, and the restoration of lower prescriptions (the prescribed labels at the grades below the one being built). Each lift preserves its cap on every coordinate, and the locality of inherited long rows is explicit (3.1, the section theorem). Status: 2.4a and 2.4b-i are compiled in this repository (theorem named). 2.4a: grade cuts (`CellScheme.gradeCut`, `Extension/GradeCut`), source prefixes (`CellScheme.IsSourcePrefix`, `Extension/SourcePrefix`), the restoration of lower prescriptions and the one-grade lift (`CellScheme.Rows.exists_restoration`, `CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_ownerCappedLift`, `Extension/Restoration`), with the cap kept at every coordinate, and the locality of inherited long rows (`CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.map_of_isLowerEmbedding`, `Extension/InheritedLocality`). 2.4b-i: positive-cap transport of lawfulness through a lawful companion (`CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.map_of_bot_iff`, `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawfulBelow.map_of_min_eq`, `Extension/CapTransport`; the cap must not be bottom), and the flattened source of a prescription (`CellScheme.Rows.IsLawfulBelow.flattenedSource_prescription`, `Extension/FlattenedSource`), whose flattening keeps the prescribed face and every cap through the encoder's own decoder, at a coding grade at most the flattening grade. 2.4b-ii, the alignment of owners, is compiled in this repository (theorem named) as well: the owner-local alignment, for every lawful source short at the grade of the owner and on the flattened source (`Label.exists_ownerAlignment`, `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawfulBelow.exists_ownerAlignment`, `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawfulBelow.exists_ownerAlignment_flattenedSource`, `Extension/OwnerAlignment`); the aligned encoding, lawful, with its literal reading and the transfer of the cap observation to every cell (`CellScheme.Rows.IsLawfulBelow.exists_alignedEncoding`, `Label.alignedDecode_alignedEncode`, `Label.min_alignedDecode_eq`, `Extension/AlignedEncoding`); and the owner-capped lifts at every cap self-visible at the grade, the cap \(\bot\) with literal top included, through the serving cell, the boundary lift across the other coatom, and the extension from the boundary, which give the one-grade lift (`CellScheme.Rows.hasOwnerCappedLifts_of_boundary`, `CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundary`, `Extension/OwnerCappedLift`). So 2.4 is complete. The alignment needs a source short at the grade of the owner, which the normal forms of 2.3 are not (they are strongly coded, with finite parts up to the grade plus one); in the one-grade step that source is the row of the serving cell. The one-grade lift takes as hypotheses properties of the new rows of full scope: their consistency, their shortness at the grade, never the formal top, and the extension from the boundary at every positive cap and at \(\bot\). For the canonical field layer they are proved over a scheme whose cells all have grade 1 (\(m=0\), 2.5a below): its new rows are short at 1, never the formal top, and consistent, which needs no hypothesis on the grades of the old cells, and they extend from the boundary at \(\bot\) and at every positive self-visible cap when every old cell has grade 1; for 2.5b (\(m=1\)) and 2.6, where old cells of other grades occur, they remain to be proved (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, checkpoints 2.5 and 2.6).
- **2.5. The two small arities.** The cases \(m=0\) and \(m=1\) are two separately stated constructions on their actual rows, each proved for arbitrary lawful prescriptions, with literal top, with the boundary retained literally, and with the cap preserved on every auxiliary cell. The full-scope cells of each grade are indexed by the canonical (rank-normalized) lawful patterns of the cells below, not by all short coded patterns; the rows between them are agreement heights on a grid. Status: 2.5a, the canonical field layer and the case \(m=0\), is compiled in this repository (theorem named). The canonical code is idempotent and has relative room (`Label.min_canonicalCode_eq`: a labelling that agrees with a canonical pattern capped at a label \(h\) has a canonical code that agrees with it capped at \(h\)), and a literal-reading decoder reads it literally (`Label.literalDecoder_canonicalCode`). The canonical field layer (`Scheme.fieldLayer`, `Extension/FieldLayer`) is consistent, with new rows short at the grade and never the formal top, with no hypothesis on the grades of the old cells; over a scheme whose cells all have one grade, it extends every lawful section at the cap \(\bot\) and extends from the boundary at every positive self-visible cap along every new row, so over a scheme whose cells all have grade 1 the hypotheses of the one-grade lift `CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundary` hold as stated, at every positive self-visible cap and not only at the source cap of the owner alignment. Every seed on two points has a completion below the full grade (`Seed.exists_completionBelowFullGrade_zero`, `Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_zero`, `Extension/SmallArities`), with no hypothesis on the stage. The case \(m=1\) (2.5b) is compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis on the stage: the field layer at mixed grades read through orbit codes, with the short-cap one-grade lift (`CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundary_short`), the lifts from a coatom to the full face (`Seed.cappedLift_fieldLayerOne_one`, `Seed.cappedLift_fieldLayerOne_two`), and the completion (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_one`; at both small arities, `Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_le_one`, `Extension/SmallArityOne`). The cap on the layer of grade 1 during a lift at grade 2 is kept by using the lift at grade 1 as the boundary lift into \((\mathrm{univ},1)\) (`SmallArityOneExamples.exists_lift_fourCellPairSeed`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). So 2.5 is complete; the coatom extension property `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` still needs every arity (2.6–2.7). The **orbit code** of a labelling at a grade \(k\) (`Label.orbitCode`, `Extension/OrbitCode`) concerns label strips: the values of the labelling that share a key \(\operatorname{vr}_k(x,k)\), some with finite part below \(k\); orbit codes and orbit keys are unrelated to automorphism orbits and to orbit formulas.
- **2.6. Recursion on the grade.** First one grade step, from the predecessor grade already established; then the general step. Lawfulness, consistency, the prefix equations, and unrestricted lifting are kept as distinct statements; the last two are defined with 2.6. The grade step uses the canonical catalogue at every grade; its normalization must handle values that are not self-visible at the grade (the labels of cells of lower grade), and the cap on the layers of lower grade during the lifts at a higher grade is the design point to settle with 2.5b. Status, compiled in this repository (theorem named) unless marked otherwise: the **tower** of field layers over the amalgam (`Seed.tower`, `Extension/Tower`) and its **lifting invariant** at a grade \(j\) (`Seed.TowerInvariant`: from each coatom to the full face, at every grade \(j'\le j\), every self-visible cap, and every lawful ambient). The step from \(j\) to \(j+1\le m\) (`Seed.towerInvariant_succ`) uses the amalgam boundary at every cap and, at the short positive caps, the **two-face lift** `2FL(j)` (`Seed.TwoFaceLift`: one labelling of the lower layers that respects both coatoms, extended through the layer at \(j+1\)); the step to the top grade \(m+1\) needs nothing beyond the invariant at \(m\) (`Seed.towerInvariant_top`). From the invariant at the top grade the tower is bountiful and legal below the full grade, and gives a completion below the full grade (`Seed.isBountiful_tower`, `Seed.isLegalBelowFullGrade_tower`, `Seed.completionBelowFullGradeOfTowerInvariant`, `Extension/TwoFaceLift`). `2FL(0)` and `2FL(1)` hold at every arity (`Seed.twoFaceLift_zero`, `Seed.twoFaceLift_one`), so the completion holds with no hypothesis at \(m\le2\) (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_le_two`). Two universal statements are refuted, each for a legal seed (negative special cases named): the **union fill**, the extension of the other coatom at the grade \(j+1\) from the union of the common face at \(j+1\) and the other coatom at \(j\) (`UnionFillCounterexample.not_unionFill_seed`; its case within the face holds for every seed, `Seed.exists_lift_union_of_lt_grade`), and `2FL(2)` (`TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.not_twoFaceLift_two`, for the seed `TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.seed4` on five points), so that `2FL(j)` at the grades \(2\le j<m\) for every seed is false at every stage (`TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLift`). The failure of `2FL(2)` is a non-existence of the lift, not a limitation of an encoding. The completion of that seed exists nevertheless (`TwoFaceLiftCounterexample.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seed4`), by the **dead-cell step** (`Seed.towerInvariant_succ_of_dead`, `Extension/DeadCellStep`): when the old cells of grade \(j+1\) are **dead** (`Seed.DeadAt j`: bottom in every labelling of the amalgam lawful below a coatom), the boundary triples of the step to the top grade (a **boundary triple** for the one-grade lift from \((C,j+1)\) to \((B,j+1)\), `Extension/Tower`: pairs \(U,V\le(B,j+1)\) and \(O\le U,V\) with \((C,j+1)\le U\), every cell below both \(U\) and \(V\) lying below \(O\), together with capped lifts from \((C,j+1)\) to \(U\) and from \(O\) to \(V\); it is the input of the one-grade lift `CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundary_short`) give the invariant at \(j+1\) with no two-face lift. Per seed, the completion therefore holds at every arity when each grade \(2\le j<m\) has `2FL(j)` or `Seed.DeadAt j` (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`); this is a sufficient condition, and it does not cover every seed (refuted: `CaseSplitCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`, at the seed `CaseSplitCounterexample.seed5`, which nevertheless has a completion, `CaseSplitCounterexample.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seed5`; 2.7).
- **2.7. The theorem.** At a stage that is zero or a limit, the apex form of the coatom extension property holds; hence the plain form and (R6). The improvement from limit stages to stages that are zero or limits is a separate lemma, with the zero stage treated explicitly. The truncation to the stage fails at successor stages (stage reduction of a lawful section to a successor stage need not be lawful), but that failure is not a proof that the coatom extension property fails there; a refutation would need its own counterexample. Whether the property holds at successor stages is open, and it is not needed. Status: still to be proved (the statement is `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at the stages that are zero or a limit), and not refuted. The completion below the full grade gives the apex form at a stage that is zero or a limit (`StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions.of_completionBelowFullGrade`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), which quantifies over the seeds of every arity. The theorem is not conditioned on the universal two-face lift, which is refuted (2.6), and no theorem is stated under it. Neither boundary triple of the library for the step to a grade \(j+1\le m\) serves every seed: the triple through the other coatom at \(j+1\) uses `2FL(j)`, refuted at \(j=2\), and the triple through the full face at \(j\) uses the union fill, refuted at the grade 2; each serves some seeds (2.6). The **existential two-face lift** `2FL∃(j)` (`Seed.TwoFaceLiftExists`, `Extension/TwoFaceLiftExists`), compiled in this repository (theorem named): for every catalogue entry \(a\) at the grade \(j+1\), every cap \(h\) self-visible and short at \(j+1\) with \(\bot<h\), and every labelling \(w_C\) lawful below \((C,j+1)\) that agrees with \(a\) capped at \(h\), some labelling \(w_D\) lawful below \((D,j+1)\), equal to \(w_C\) at the cells of the common face of grade at most \(j+1\) and agreeing with \(a\) capped at \(h\), is such that the labelling glued from \(w_C\) and \(w_D\) satisfies the conclusion of `2FL(j)`. It is the step of the tower, stated exactly (compiled in this repository (theorem named)): for \(j\le m\) and under the invariant at \(j\), the invariant at \(j+1\) holds exactly when `2FL∃(j)` does (`Seed.towerInvariant_succ_iff_twoFaceLiftExists`), and the invariant at the top grade holds exactly when `2FL∃(j)` holds at the grades \(2\le j<m\) (`Seed.towerInvariant_top_iff`). Sufficiency (`Seed.towerInvariant_succ_of_twoFaceLiftExists`) uses the one-grade lift with one boundary triple at \(\bot\) and another at the positive caps (`CellScheme.Rows.cappedLift_of_boundaries_short`), the second degenerate, so no separate choosing variant of the one-grade lift is needed. Both per-seed hypotheses imply it (`Seed.twoFaceLiftExists_of_twoFaceLift`, for \(j\le m\); `Seed.twoFaceLiftExists_of_deadAt`, under the invariant at \(j\)), and a seed satisfying it at the grades \(2\le j<m\) has a completion below the full grade (`Seed.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_of_twoFaceLiftExists`). On the seed `CaseSplitCounterexample.seed5` on five points, `2FL∃(2)` holds while `2FL(2)` and deadness both fail (`CaseSplitCounterexample.twoFaceLiftExists_and_not_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`), so the case split does not cover every seed (refuted: `CaseSplitCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLift_or_deadAt`), and that seed has a completion (`CaseSplitCounterexample.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seed5`). `2FL∃(2)` fails for a legal seed (refuted, negative special case named): for the seed `TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.seedL` on five points, of the coatom types `TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.TL` and `CaseSplitCounterexample.T5` (`TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.not_twoFaceLiftExists_two_seedL`). So `2FL∃(j)` at the grades \(2\le j<m\) for every seed is false at every stage (`TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.not_forall_twoFaceLiftExists`), and for `seedL` the invariant of the tower fails at the top grade (`TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample.not_towerInvariant_top_seedL`). No theorem is stated under the universal form of `2FL∃(j)`, and the theorem is not conditioned on it. For `seedL`, the identified obstruction (every new cell of the tower at \((\mathrm{univ},2)\) where the catalogue entry of the failure reaches its cap reads the cells at \((\{3\},1)\) and \((\{4\},1)\) at one value) survives the redesigns examined (argued, not formalized; the module docstring of `Extension/TwoFaceLiftExistsCounterexample`). `seedL` itself has a completion below the full grade outside the tower, compiled in this repository (theorem named): the **thin completion** (`ThinCompletion.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seedL`, `Extension/ThinCompletion`), the amalgam with one new cell at each graded face \((\mathrm{univ},k)\) of full scope below the full grade, labelled \(\top\) at the cells of grade 4 and \(\bot\) elsewhere. The identified obstruction does not apply to it: its only cell at \((\mathrm{univ},2)\) reads \((\{3\},1)\) strictly below \((\{4\},1)\) (`ThinCompletionExamples.row_newCell_two_lt`). With the apex added it gives, at every stage, a legal stage type on five points whose faces along the two coatoms are `TL` and `T5` with a cell of full scope and full grade carrying the largest label (`ThinCompletionExamples.exists_coatomExtension_seedL`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), so `seedL` is not a counterexample to `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at \(m=3\). **The ordered-layer step at \(m=3\)** (`Extension/OrderedLayerStep`, `Extension/OrderedLayerTop`, `Extension/OrderedLayerExamples`, `Extension/OrderedLayerObstruction`; compiled in this repository (theorem named) unless marked otherwise). For a seed on five points and layer rows \(\rho_k\) read off graded indices, the layer scheme (`OrderedLayer.layerScheme`) is the amalgam followed by one new cell at each \((\mathrm{univ},k)\), \(k=1,\dots,4\), whose row reads the cells below it through their graded indices by \(\rho_k\); the thin completion of `seedL` is the layer scheme of its ordered rows, by definition (`OrderedLayer.layerScheme_thinRows`). The **ordered-layer step** `Seed.OrderedLayerStep I ρ` is a named hypothesis with four fields: the rows are coded; each new row is consistent (the labelling read off by \(\rho_k\) is lawful below \((\mathrm{univ},k)\)); the rows lift capped from \((C,k)\) and from \((D,k)\) into \((\mathrm{univ},k)\), \(1\le k\le4\), the only lifts that need capping (every other lift is one of the amalgam or a composite with one of these, `OrderedLayer.isBountiful_layerScheme`); and a lawful labelling of the layer scheme extends the glued one. It gives a completion below the full grade for every seed on five points that has it (`Seed.OrderedLayerStep.completion`; for a seed with an ordered-layer step for some rows, `Seed.HasOrderedLayerStep.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade`), and it is exactly legality below the full grade of the layer scheme with a lawful extension of the glued labelling (`Seed.orderedLayerStep_iff`): it is the exact content of a completion with one new cell per graded face of full scope and rows read off graded indices, a restriction of the completion, and strictly stronger than it: `CrossedCouplingCounterexample.seedHG` has a completion below the full grade and no ordered-layer step (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.not_forall_hasOrderedLayerStep_of_nonempty`, compiled in this repository (theorem named); the multi-layer step below). For a seed with bottom apexes (`Seed.HasBottomApexes`, which holds when both coatom types are the apex added to \(\bot\) labels, `Seed.hasBottomApexes_of_addApex`, as for `T4`, `T5`, `TL`), with the top row at \((\mathrm{univ},4)\) the top grade is automatic: the step follows from its fields at the grades \(k\le3\) (`Seed.OrderedLayerStepBelowTop`, `Seed.OrderedLayerStepBelowTop.orderedLayerStep`, through the lift at the grade 4 from the grade 3, `OrderedLayer.cappedLift_four`), and those lifts reduce to lifts at the grade 3 of labellings vanishing above \(k\) (`OrderedLayer.cappedLift_of_lift_three`). Instances: `seed4`, `seed5` and `seedL` have ordered-layer steps (`Seed.orderedLayerStep_seed4`, `Seed.orderedLayerStep_seed5`, `Seed.orderedLayerStep_seedL`; the first two are completions of a shape other than the tower's, which already completes them); for `seed5` the row at \((\mathrm{univ},3)\) reads every live kind at \(\omega+3\), since the row of `seedL` there, which reads \(A_C\) at 1, is not consistent when the first coatom type couples \(G\le A_C\) (argued, not formalized). **Which separating rows exist**: every ordered-layer step of a seed of the coatom types `TL` and `T5`, for any rows, reads \((\{3\},1)\) strictly below \((\{4\},1)\) at the grades 1–3 (`Seed.OrderedLayerStep.thinRow_lt_of_TL_T5`). More generally, **forced separations** hold in every completion of every seed (`CompletionBelowFullGrade.exists_separating_of_forcesTop`): if a labelling lawful below \((B_1,k)\) is below \(\top\) at a cell \(d_1\) and forces \(\top\) at a cell \(d_2\) below \((B_2,k)\) of grade at least that of \(d_1\) (`Seed.ForcesTop`: every labelling lawful below \((B_2,k)\) equal to it on the common cells is \(\top\) at \(d_2\)), some cell at \((\mathrm{univ},\operatorname{grade}d_2)\) reads \(d_1\) strictly below \(d_2\). Two opposite forcings at one grade give two different cells there (`CompletionBelowFullGrade.exists_ne_of_forcesTop`), so a seed on five points with two opposite forcings has no ordered-layer step, for any rows (`Seed.not_hasOrderedLayerStep_of_forcesTop`). The orientation depends on the seed: for the mirror seed `seedLM` (`T5` on the first coatom, `TL` on the second) the ordered-layer step holds with rows oriented \(D\) before \(C\) (`Seed.orderedLayerStep_seedLM`), every ordered-layer step of it reads \((\{4\},1)\) strictly below \((\{3\},1)\) at the grades 1–3 (`Seed.OrderedLayerStep.rowsLM_lt_of_T5_TL`), and no layer rows serve both `seedL` and `seedLM` (`not_exists_orderedLayerStep_seedL_seedLM`); the two forcings behind the orientations are compiled (`Seed.forcesTop_of_TL_T5`, `Seed.forcesTop_of_T5_TL`). For the seed `seedLL` of `TL` with itself, where neither coatom type couples its parameter of grade 3 to its parameter of grade 1 and no separation is forced, the ordered-layer step holds with rows that read the cells of grade 1 of both coatoms at one value (`Seed.orderedLayerStep_seedLL`): its lawful labellings below \((\mathrm{univ},3)\) repeat one labelling of `TL` on both coatoms, and every lift is that repetition. **Refuted** (negative special case named): the ordered-layer step for every seed on five points, at every stage (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.not_forall_hasOrderedLayerStep`, `Extension/CrossedCouplingCounterexample`). The legal seed `CrossedCouplingCounterexample.seedHG` has the coatom types `TH` and `TG` (`Extension/CrossedCouplingTypes`) on the nineteen cells of `T4`, whose common face carries two parameters, \(H\) at \((\{0,1,2\},2)\) and \(G\) at \((\{0,1,2\},3)\); `TH` couples the parameter of its cells of grade 1 through the point 3 to \(H\) and not to \(G\), and `TG` couples the corresponding parameter to \(G\) and not to \(H\). These give two opposite forcings between \((\{3\},1)\) and \((\{4\},1)\) (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.forcesTop_left`, `CrossedCouplingCounterexample.forcesTop_right`), so every completion below the full grade of `seedHG` has two different cells at \((\mathrm{univ},1)\) (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.exists_ne_seedHG`) and `seedHG` has no ordered-layer step (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.not_hasOrderedLayerStep_seedHG`). No theorem is stated under the universal form. This refutes the step, not the completion. **The multi-layer step at \(m=3\)** (`Extension/MultiLayerStep`, `Extension/CrossedCouplingScheme`, `Extension/CrossedCouplingCompletion`; compiled in this repository (theorem named) unless marked otherwise). For multiplicities \(M_k\) and rows \(r_{k,i}\) read off cells, not graded indices, the multi-layer scheme (`OrderedLayer.multiLayerScheme`) is the amalgam followed by \(M_k\) new cells at each \((\mathrm{univ},k)\), \(k=1,\dots,4\), so two new cells with one graded index may read each other and themselves at different values. The named hypothesis `Seed.MultiLayerStep I M r` has the fields of the ordered-layer step together with positive multiplicities; it gives a completion below the full grade (`Seed.MultiLayerStep.completion`), and it is exactly legality below the full grade of the multi-layer scheme with a lawful extension of the glued labelling (`Seed.multiLayerStep_iff`); since its rows are arbitrary labellings of the cells, it reformulates the completion at \(m=3\) rather than restricting it (argued, not formalized), and its content is the choice of multiplicities and rows. **`seedHG` has a completion below the full grade** (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.nonempty_completionBelowFullGrade_seedHG`), with every field of `CompletionBelowFullGrade` proved: the multi-layer step holds for every seed of the coatom types `TH` and `TG` (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.multiLayerStep_HG`) with two new cells at \((\mathrm{univ},1)\), one for each forced separation (the first reads \((\{3\},1)\) at 1 and \((\{4\},1)\) at \(\omega+2\), the second the reverse), and one at each \((\mathrm{univ},k)\), \(k=2,3,4\), whose rows merge those of the cells of full scope of `TH` and `TG` at the grades 2 and 3. Its lawful labellings below \((\mathrm{univ},k)\), \(k\le3\), are the labellings by kinds of four parameters \(A_C,A_D,H,G\) with the couplings of both types (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.isLawful_kindLabel`, `CrossedCouplingCounterexample.exists_of_isLawfulBelow_univ`); the capped lift from \((C,k)\) keeps \(A_C,H,G\) and takes \(A_D\) from the ambient below the cap and \(\top\) above it (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.cappedLift_C_of_le_three`), the lift from \((D,k)\) keeps \(A_D,H,G\) and takes \(A_C\) from the ambient below the cap and, above it, \(H\) when \(k=3\) and \(H<G\), and \(\top\) otherwise, so always \(\top\) at \(k\le2\) (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.cappedLift_D_of_le_three`), and the lifts at the grade 4 come from those at the grade 3 (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.cappedLift_four_HG`). The old cells keep their scopes, rows and labels, so with the apex added both coatom faces are literally `TH` and `TG`, at every stage (`CrossedCouplingCounterexample.exists_coatomExtension_seedHG`). A systematic choice of the new cells for every seed on five points (one new cell per forced separation at each graded face of full scope) is prospective. The open point of 2.7 is the completion below the full grade for every seed at \(m\ge3\).

The completion stays within the construction of lawful finite extensions. It imports only Layers 0–1, the stage types, the amalgam, and the section theorem of 3.1 (with the shared decoding lemma); it does not depend on realizations, on receiving (statements (R1)–(R4)), or on any later step toward the main theorem.

**Simplifications.** At a stage that is zero or a limit, the construction completes a lawful labelling whose values need not lie below the stage and truncates it to the stage once, at the end, as in the proof of [Kni26, Corollary 4.3.22], rather than truncating every intermediate decoded value; it proves that the truncation keeps both prescribed faces literally, since their labels already lie at the stage (the library's `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.reduce`, stage reduction to a stage that is zero or a limit preserves lawfulness, and `Label.AtStage.reduce_eq`, reduction fixes a label at the stage). The capped-lifting calculus of Layer 1 and the section theorem of this item are reused, not re-proved. No intermediate construction that the proof does not use is carried.

**The naive completion.** The completion of [Kni26, Definition 4.3.14] has cells of full scope indexed by all lawful coded patterns (the efficient stacks of Definitions 4.3.3, 4.3.5, 4.3.6, and 4.3.7), with the rows between full-scope cells of the same grade given by the heights of their meets (clause 3 there). Its bountifulness, the statement of [Kni26, Lemma 4.3.20], is unproved, not refuted; Definition 4.3.6 needs explicit bindings before that statement is definite. Ties do propagate between full-scope cells: a tie of a lawful section at a value below the cap that is not self-visible at the grade of a full-scope cell forces equal row entries there, hence the same tie in every lawful section. Bountifulness prescribes one face, and there such a tie is already a tie of the prescription, so this mechanism separates no two cells of the face (the instance of Lemma 4.3.19 separates cells of different coatoms). For the variant whose full-scope cells are indexed by all lawful short coded patterns over a fixed finite alphabet, with agreement-height rows, bountifulness fails already at \(m=0\) and for every block bound at least 3 (in particular \(2N+1\) for \(N\) cells): a pattern constant at the top block of the alphabet, as ambient, leaves no room above the cap for a prescription with four distinct values on one point, once two lower constant patterns are pinned by the cap (compiled in this repository (theorem named): `SmallArityExamples.not_isBountiful_flatRows`, `Extension/SmallArityExamples`). Whether this applies to Definition 4.3.14 depends on the bindings of Definition 4.3.6; it does not apply when the patterns are rank-normalized, as in the canonical field layer of 2.5. The printed proof of Lemma 4.3.20 is not relied on: Claim 4.3.5 of that proof extends the prescription first, the proof then applies the joint lifting of Lemma 4.3.19 to both coatoms, and conclusions 1–3 of Lemma 4.3.19 cannot hold together under the natural readings of efficiency (Layer 1). The construction chosen instead builds the completion grade by grade over the boundary, in one fixed order of its cells; it chooses the full-scope cell that serves each lift (defined with 2.6) before extending on the other coatom, and it tops out in a single apex cell. At \(m=1\) the other coatom's cells of grade 2 are filled within their face after the lift at grade 1 (2.5). At \(m\ge2\) they cannot in general be filled over the union of the common face at their grade and the other coatom at the grade below: that union fill is refuted for a legal seed (`UnionFillCounterexample.not_unionFill_seed`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). The step instead chooses one labelling of the lower layers that respects both coatoms, the two-face lift of 2.6, which holds at the grades 0 and 1 and fails for a legal seed at the grade 2. So the caveat at the arity 2 is closed (the completion at \(m\le2\), 2.6), and the caveat at \(m\ge3\) remains (2.7).

#### 3.2. One occurrence, then labelled evaluation

Keep three steps separate:

- **Construction:** the finite object of 3.1, as data, with its lifting properties at every self-visible cap and the conditions on every auxiliary row it introduces.
- **Extension of the realization by one occurrence** over the private context, by a model clause that depends on the statement:
  - *(R1), the ordinary construction.* The display shows that the parameter of the bottom-pattern clause 4(a)ii of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1] is admissible. Applied over the private tuple, the clause gives an actual one-point extension \(u\) on the constructed scheme with the display's bottom pattern on the cells of grade at most the private arity. The graded index of the gate has grade \(N\), at most that arity, so each of its cells is tested: the gate is not bottom in \(u\) (the gate equation), and its twins are bottom in \(u\). The recovery statement reads the display only through this bottom pattern, and not its values.
  - *(R2), the LOW construction.* The occurrence comes from (R1): finite-cut receiving over the private context, in its form for finite covers, with the display on the constructed LOW scheme as donor type and a cutoff \(\delta\) above the rounded non-top donor maximum \(\beta_0\) of 3.3. Its recovery statement reads the observations of the received occurrence below \(\delta\) and compares them with those of the display.
  - *(R3) and (R4), the growth construction.* Generalized saturation, clause 4(a)i, realizes the constructed legal scheme over the private tuple: an actual one-point extension \(u\) on exactly that scheme. No display, no bottom pattern, and no stage bound for a display is used.

  In each case, exact consistency makes the restriction of \(u\) to the private tuple the private context, labels included, and the restriction of \(u\) along the donor embedding (the root followed by the new point) an actual occurrence on the donor's scheme whose labels are those of the corresponding cells of \(u\); the new point is fresh over the whole private tuple, hence over the root.
- **Labelled evaluation.** A **restriction-compatible labelling** \(L\) of \(R\) assigns to every actual occurrence \(x\) a lawful labelling \(L(x)\) of the scheme of its type, commuting with face maps: for every actual occurrence \(x\) and every face of \(x\) along which its type restricts, \(L\) of the face is the restriction of \(L(x)\). It need not be the labelling of a model, and its values need not lie below the stage. The actual labels form one such labelling; the stable labels of Layer 4 form another. The recovery statement of (R3) and (R4) is proved for every restriction-compatible labelling \(L\) whose value on the private context lies in the **prescribed bottom class**, that is, is bottom exactly at those cells below the private cap at which the private context's labels are bottom: for every realized one-point extension \(u\) on the constructed scheme, the donor face of \(L(u)\) satisfies `Correct` (3.3) against the value of \(L\) on the private context. It is then applied to the labelling that the statement evaluates. (R3) evaluates the actual labels. (R4) evaluates the stable labelling, which does not restrict to the actual private labels; only its bottom class agrees with theirs. The recovery statement for every restriction-compatible labelling uses no stable labelling and is proved with (R1)–(R3); the acquisition of the calibrated data of (R4) (3.3), its occurrence, and its evaluation come after the structural candidate of Layer 4 has been built.

The root, the private context, the donor values, and the gate equation all refer to this one occurrence \(u\): the root and the private context are its literal restrictions, the gate equation is an equation about its type, and the recovered donor values are values of its type, or of a restriction-compatible labelling, at it. Equations recorded on different occurrences, even of the same type, are not combined.

(R3) and (R4) share the constructed legal scheme, its section theorem (3.1), and the recovery statement. What differs is the relative data acquired in the model and the labelling evaluated: in (R3) the private cap and the marker are labelled top; in (R4) non-hollowness supplies an attained proper stable marker, and the data are calibrated to it. Their existence hypotheses stay separate: (R3) assumes hollowness and unbounded top-grade growth, (R4) the structural stable candidate of a model with non-hollow unbounded top-grade growth. Hollowness here is the original no-anchor predicate (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 8); the compiled statements that take (R3) as a hypothesis are presently formulated with cover-hollowness (`Realization.IsCoverHollow`, Layer 4); (R4) is declared, as the hypothesis `StableCappedReceiving` (`Continuation/Continuation`), with its negation, not cover-hollowness, among its assumptions, and is not proved; and the equivalence of cover-hollowness with the original no-anchor predicate is still to be proved. They are not merged because their evaluation arguments coincide.

#### 3.3. `Correct`, LOW, and the recovery statements

**`Correct`.** Its data are the private cap \(C\) of grade \(N\); a marker offset \(R<N\); and three sets of donor cells: the **requested-bottom** cells \(Z\), the **exactly requested** cells \(F\), each \(f\in F\) with a reference cell \(\rho(f)\) below \(C\) and an offset \(k_f\le N\), and the **high** cells \(T\), read through the marker \(a\) below \(C\). Every donor cell outside the root lies in \(Z\cup F\cup T\); the three sets need not partition the donor's cells. For a labelling \(s\) of the cells below \(C\) (in the applications, the value of \(L\) on the private context) and a labelling \(v\) of the donor's cells (the donor face of \(L(u)\)), `Correct`\((s,v)\) holds when, everything capped at the value \(s(C)\) of the private cap:

1. **bottom:** \(\min(v(z),s(C))=\bot\) for every \(z\in Z\);
2. **reference:** \(\min(v(f),s(C))=\min(\operatorname{vr}_N(s(\rho(f)),k_f),s(C))\) for every \(f\in F\), the reference with its offset replacement, as in [Kni26, Definition 8.3.1], clause 2;
3. **marker:** \(\min(\operatorname{vr}_N(s(a),R),s(C))\le\min(v(y),s(C))\) for every \(y\in T\), a lower bound, not an equation.

`Correct` has no gate, and the cap value \(s(C)\) can be the formal top. In the hollow case (R3) the private cap and the marker are labelled top; \(Z\), \(F\), and \(T\) are the donor's bottom, proper, and top cells; the reference of a proper donor label \(\mu+i\) (\(\mu\) zero or a limit) is the reference cell of its block, labelled \(\mu+k\) with \(k<N\), with offset \(k_f=i\). Then `Correct` against the actual private labels holds exactly when \(v\) is the donor's labelling, tops included. In (R4) (the non-hollow case) the data are **calibrated** to the stable labelling of the private context, with a natural number \(\ell<N\) as the marker offset \(R\): the private cap is top in the actual labels but has a proper stable value above \(\alpha+\ell\); the reference cell of each block \(\mu\) containing a proper donor label has stable value \(\mu+o_\mu\), with an offset \(o_\mu<N\), below the stable value of the cap; the marker has stable value \(\alpha+i\) for some \(i<N\), below the stable value of the cap; and \(\ell\) is chosen so that every proper donor label is below \(\alpha+\ell\) and has finite part below \(\ell\), and so that \(\gamma<\alpha+\ell\) for the requested ordinal \(\gamma\) of (R4). `Correct` against the stable labels, capped at the stable value of the cap, then returns each bottom and each proper donor value exactly (the bottom clause and the reference clause), and the marker clause gives a stable value at least \(\alpha+\ell>\gamma\) at each donor top.

**LOW.** The setting is the residual case: a model with no rigid core and eventual top grade \(K>0\). There the acquisition supplies the private context, a donor over it that restricts literally to the requested one-point donor \(d\) (the donor of this paragraph), and the private gap below. LOW is a condition on state vectors and uses no gate, no reference cell, and no `Correct`. Its data are:

- the donor labelling \(p\), lawful, with every top cell of grade at most \(K\);
- a **private gap**: a cell \(c\) of the private scheme of grade \(K\) and a cell \(r\) below it with \(h=\operatorname{vr}_K(E_c(r),K)<E_c(c)\), such that for every root cell whose donor label is top, the entry of the row \(E_c\) at its private face exceeds \(h\): the private row's gap lies above the root tops.

A state vector consists of complete donor and private vectors \(u\) and \(v\), future fields included, and the cutoff field \(b\); there is no gate field. It is admitted at grade \(j\) when \(u\) and \(v\) are lawful on the cells of grade at most \(j\) and agree on the root, the future fields are self-visible at grade 1, \(b\) is self-visible at grade \(\min(j,K)\), and the **LOW forcing** holds: when \(j\ge K\) and \(b\) is strictly above the maximum \(M(u)\) of \(u\) over the cells whose donor label is not top, every cell \(x\) with \(p(x)=\top\) satisfies \(u(x)\ge\max(b,e(v))\), where \(e(v)\) is the value that the private gap row reads from \(v\).

**LOW recovery.** Let the admitted full-grade vector have donor part \(p\), private part the private labels with \(c\) and \(r\) labelled top, and cutoff above \(\beta_0=\operatorname{vr}_K(M(p),K)\), the grade-\(K\) rounding of the maximum of the non-top donor labels. The constructed legal LOW scheme then has a display \(w\) with donor face \(p\) and private face the private labels, and every lawful labelling \(q\) of that scheme with the literal private face and with \(\operatorname{obs}_\delta(q)=\operatorname{obs}_\delta(w)\) on every cell, for some \(\delta>\beta_0\), has donor face exactly \(p\), tops included. Restricting to \(d\) then gives exact residual receiving (R2).

**The recovery statements** have four different conclusions, with separate hypotheses:

1. **agreement below a cutoff:** in (R1), every lawful labelling of the constructed scheme with the literal private face and the bottom pattern of the whole graded index of the gate (the gate not bottom, its twins bottom) agrees with the donor below the label of the private cap, which is at least the actual cut of the private context, itself above the requested cutoff. A literal private face and a non-bottom gate do not imply this agreement from lawfulness alone when the gate has a twin: locality at the gate does not bound the gate's value from below (lowering that value to a smaller label self-visible at its grade keeps locality, by capping), and the lower bound comes only from availability against the private cap, a cell of the same grade whose scope lies in the gate's, which reaches the gate only when its twins are bottom. Gate recovery is a finite statement about rows: it uses one witness (the locality of the gate), availability against the private cap, and the orderliness of the cap, and no legality, completion, or composition of witnesses, and it recovers the donor face only (bottom and anchored proper donor labels exactly, donor tops as values at least the label of the private cap); it is compiled in this repository (theorem named), for rows (`CellScheme.Rows.IsGate.recover`, `Extension/Gate`) and for a gated extension (`StageType.GatedExtension.recover`, `StageType.GatedExtension.exists_restrictFace_mem_receivingFamily`, `Realization/GateRecovery`);
2. **exact recovery of the values that are not top:** a cell whose donor value is bottom or a proper ordinal has exactly that value. In (R3) and (R4) a bottom donor value is recovered by the bottom clause of `Correct`, since the value of the private cap is not bottom, and a proper donor value by the reference clause of `Correct` below a private cap of larger value (in (R3), where the cap is top; in (R4), for stable values, by the calibration above); in (R2) both come from LOW recovery;
3. **literal-top recovery:** a cell whose donor value is the formal top has the formal top as its value, from LOW recovery (R2), or from the marker clause of `Correct` when the private cap and the marker are labelled top (in (R3), the hollow case);
4. **above-threshold inequalities:** a cell whose donor value is the formal top has a value above a threshold, possibly a proper value, from the marker clause of `Correct` (in (R4): stable value above the requested \(\gamma\)).

The third does not follow from the others. Agreement below a single permitted cutoff never alone identifies a top: at the limit stage \(\alpha\), for a permitted cutoff \(c\), the formal top and every proper label between \(c\) and \(\alpha\) have the same observation \(c\).

Each recovery lemma lists the observations it reads (the private face; the bottom pattern of the graded index of the gate; the capped readings of `Correct`; in LOW, the observations below \(\delta\)) and concludes only the donor values its first use needs. It does not require recovery of the whole type of the constructed occurrence (its auxiliary cells, private fields, or future fields), and no first use may assume such a recovery.

**The density boundary.** Three notions are kept apart, each with its name. A **cutoff observation** is the observation \(\operatorname{obs}_c\) at a permitted cutoff \(c\): receiving and the density sentence supply, for each cutoff separately, an occurrence with the donor's cutoff observation. A **stage reduction** is the reduction \(\operatorname{red}_\beta\) of a label, a type, or a realization to a lower stage \(\beta\) (`reduce`: labels at or above \(\beta\) become top), coherent under composition (`StageType.reduce_reduce`); the level observations of the full-presentation route are of this kind. **Exact extension within a specified age** is exact receiving, over every actual root, of every one-point coface in a prescribed age, as in (R2), (R3), step 6 of the top-free witnesses for top-free donors, and fullness (the section "Reduction to full presentations"). Fix a realization \(R\) at a limit stage \(\alpha\), a stage \(\beta<\alpha\) that is zero or a limit (so that \(\operatorname{red}_\beta\) is defined), and, optionally, a **specified age** \(\mathcal A\) at \(\alpha\). Its hypotheses: \(\mathcal A\) is a set of legal stage types at \(\alpha\), closed under reindexing and under restriction to closed faces, that contains the actual type of every actual occurrence of \(R\) (in the pointed case, of every actual occurrence containing the core; in the applications, an exact age: top-free, residual with top grades at most \(K\), hollow, or pointed at a named rigid core, in which case only roots containing the core are considered, and reindexing and restriction are those fixing the core). The last hypothesis is needed and is not a consequence of the others: if the actual type \(p\) of a root were outside a face-closed \(\mathcal A\), no coface of \(p\) would lie in \(\mathcal A\), and the lifting below would fail trivially. When \(\mathcal A\) is specified, the lift \(d'\) is required to lie in \(\mathcal A\), and exact extension within \(\mathcal A\) is exact receiving, over every actual root, of every one-point coface of the root's actual type that lies in \(\mathcal A\). For an actual root \(t\) of \(R\) of actual type \(p\), a **projected donor** over \(t\) (the term of (AE), the section "Reduction to full presentations") is a one-point donor type \(d\) at \(\beta\) over \(\operatorname{red}_\beta(p)\), and **exact projected receiving** of \(d\) over \(t\) is a fresh point \(y\) such that \(t^\frown y\) is an actual occurrence with \(\operatorname{red}_\beta(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y))=d\). The acceptance criterion of this item is: *Cutoff-by-cutoff receiving gives exact projected receiving only after proving that each projected donor lifts over the particular actual root being extended.* It restricts what may be claimed and adds no prerequisite to the main theorem: the one-block transfer of Layer 5 receives a donor already given at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\), which is its own lift. The lifting is a named statement, to be proved, and is not derived from the coherence of stage reduction:

> **Projected-donor lifting** (a statement to be proved in each application). Data: \(R\), \(\alpha\), \(\beta\), and \(\mathcal A\) when specified, as above. *Lifting of \(d\) over \(t\)*, for one actual root \(t\) of \(R\) with actual type \(p\) and one projected donor \(d\) over \(t\): there exists a one-point coface \(d'\) of \(p\), a legal stage type at \(\alpha\), in \(\mathcal A\) when \(\mathcal A\) is specified, with \(\operatorname{red}_\beta(d')=d\). *Projected-donor lifting for \(R\) at \(\beta\)*: for every actual root \(t\), and then for every projected donor \(d\) over \(t\), lifting of \(d\) over \(t\) holds; the lift \(d'\) may depend on \(t\) (through \(p\)) and on \(d\). The single-pair form and the form for every root and every projected donor are different statements, and the acceptance criterion asks for the second wherever exact projected receiving is claimed for all roots and donors.

*Exact projected receiving from lifting* (a statement to be proved, with this argument): if \(R\) has the finite-cut receiving property, then for every actual root \(t\) and every projected donor \(d\) over \(t\) such that \(d\) lifts over \(t\), there is a fresh point \(y\) with exact projected receiving of \(d\): receive a lift \(d'\) over \(t\) at the permitted cutoff \(\beta\); the cutoff observation at \(\beta\) determines the stage reduction to \(\beta\) (a label below \(\beta\) is kept by both, and every label at or above \(\beta\), top included, is observed as \(\beta\) and reduced to top). Coherence of stage reduction is not accepted as a proof of the lifting: the identities of stage reduction relate the types of one tuple at different stages, and say nothing about which donors over the reduced type of a root lift to donors over its actual type. A specified age of legal stage types in which some projected donor over the reduced type of a root has no lift within the age is expected to exist; such an example is a separate target, not claimed here. For abstract systems of observations with coherent projections commuting with face restriction (not legal stage types), a four-point example (informal; not formalized here) has a projected donor that lifts over one actual root and has no lift over another root with the same projection: lifting depends on the root, not only on its projection, which is why the statement quantifies \(\forall\) root, \(\forall\) donor, \(\exists\) lift; it is not a counterexample involving legal stage types. The projected finite-extension rule of (AE) (the section "Reduction to full presentations") is this lifting for presentations, and the second source of (AE) there is to obtain it from its hypothesis that the two roots have equal types at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\), one level above the comparison. Accordingly a projected structure is not called (or used as) a Fraïssé limit, or full, for an age at the lower stage unless exact extension within that age is proved for it. More generally, coherent projection of finite labels and base diagrams, receiving of every legal donor up to a permitted cutoff, and the existence of sufficiently high presentations with bounds on the levels of the presentations of one base structure are three separate assertions, none of which supplies another (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 13).

**Lifting over a root of small fixing rank** (a statement still to be proved; the fixed-root case only).
