The **fixing rank** of a stage type at \(\lambda_\eta\) is the least \(\xi\le\eta\) such that its stage reduction to \(\lambda_\xi\) changes no label (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"); it is a property of finite labels and is not identified with a Scott rank. Let \(\alpha=\lambda_\eta\), \(\beta=\lambda_\xi\) with \(\xi\le\eta\), and let \(t\) be an actual root of \(R\) whose actual type \(p\) has fixing rank at most \(\xi\), so that its stage reduction to \(\beta\) changes no label. Then every projected donor \(d\) over \(t\) lifts over \(t\) by literal weakening: \(d\), read at the stage \(\alpha\), is a one-point coface \(d'\) of \(p\) with \(\operatorname{red}_\beta(d')=d\), and this one lift carries both the exact root (its restriction along the root is \(p\), labels included) and the exact reduction. With a specified age \(\mathcal A\), membership of \(d'\) in \(\mathcal A\) is a further hypothesis. It covers only the fixed-root case, for roots whose fixing rank is at most the lower level: it is not projected-donor lifting for every actual root, and the acceptance criterion above is unchanged for the other roots.

**Manuscript correspondence.** Item 3 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)", the two-index density theorem with its boundaries and its witness-bounded form, belongs to this item.

#### 3.4. Extension statements and their first uses

The **residual comparison** and the **hollow comparison** are the second and third terminal comparisons of Layer 4 (no rigid core and the same positive eventual top grade; hollow top-grade growth). The **cap-to-model theorem** states that a realization with a nonempty carrier, exact consistency, covering, and the finite-cut receiving property is a model in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1]. Its proof supplies the cofaces requested by the four families from the coatom extension construction and uses no model. It gives the direction from the density sentence to the four-family sentence in the fidelity theorem of Layer 2 and the last step of stable modelhood in Layer 4, and it is proved with the first of these.

The table labels its six statements (R1)–(R6), and the roadmap cites them by these labels; the word row is kept for the rows \(E(\Sigma)\) of cells. (R1)–(R4) are receiving statements: each conclusion is an equation, or a list of equations, on one actual occurrence over the literal root, and each has a recovery theorem (3.3). (R5) and (R6) are extension statements about charts: their conclusions are charts, and they have a construction and a first use but no occurrence and no recovery theorem.

| Statement | Hypotheses | Conclusion | First use | Import boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (R1) Finite-cut receiving (the ordinary construction) | \(R\) a model at \(\alpha\); any root, the empty root included; any one-point donor type \(d\) over it; any permitted cutoff \(\delta\) | a fresh point \(y\) with \(t^\frown y\) actual, of type on the donor's scheme, and \(\operatorname{obs}_\delta(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y))=\operatorname{obs}_\delta(d)\); for a finite cover of the root, apply this one point at a time along a chain of visible faces of the donor's plan, at one cap self-visible at the donor's arity and above \(\delta\), repairing the donor to the received face by the bountifulness of its scheme (this many-point form is derived from the one-point form, `HasFiniteCutReceiving.hasFiniteExtensionReceiving` in `Realization/Receiving`; `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` is the form in which (R1), still to be proved, is to be supplied; it is compiled in this repository (theorem named) conditional on the coupled gated pinned extension property `StageType.HasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`, a named hypothesis that is open (`Realization.IsModel.hasFiniteCutReceiving_of_hasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`, `Expansion.finiteCutReceiving_of_hasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`), one permitted cutoff at a time, with agreement below the label of the private cap, which is above the cutoff, and with generalized saturation over the private context, witnessed by the display, with no hypothesis on the stage; the first form of that property, the gated pinned extension property `StageType.HasGatedPinnedExtensions`, whose displays label the twins of the gate \(\bot\), is refuted at every stage (`GatedExtensionCounterexample.not_hasGatedPinnedExtensions`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and the coupled form, which replaces that clause by the condition on rows `CellScheme.Rows.TwinsReadGate` (every twin reads the gate at least as the private cap), holds at the refuting input (`CoupledGateExamples.exists_coupledGatedExtension_comap_g₁`, compiled in this repository (theorem named))); for the reconstruction of an ultrahomogeneous structure whose age is the age of top-free charts the conclusion is compiled (step 6 of the section on the top-free witnesses, `hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct`; its constructed scheme must be legal (the bottom-pattern clause asks for a legal scheme), and completeness forces a chain of closed faces from the root with the new point to all points, so the construction contains exact pinned extensions (restricted to its last coatom it is an exact pinned extension of a face of the private type over the root, an instance of (R6)) and a final layer of full scope controlled at the grade of the gate, which the abstract coatom extension property does not supply; general (R1) therefore proceeds conditionally on a named gated-extension property, the coupled gated pinned extension property (`StageType.HasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`), which is open; its open point is cap lowering (CL), stated in its docstring, the uniform form of what the construction needs, a strengthening not shown necessary, so that a failure of (CL) refutes the coupled design only at a pair that a forcing prescription from an anchored legal donor actually realizes (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, checkpoint 4);
