| (R2) Exact residual receiving (the LOW construction) | \(R\) a model at \(\alpha\) with no rigid core and eventual top grade \(K\) (positive, since eventual top grade zero makes the empty tuple a rigid core); any root, the empty root included; any one-point coface \(d\) of the root's type whose top cells have grade at most \(K\) | a fresh point \(y\) with \(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y)=d\) exactly | the residual comparison (Layer 4; compiled in this repository (theorem named) conditional on (R2) as the named hypothesis `Realization.ResidualReceiving`: `Realization.nonempty_equiv_of_residual`) | imports (R1) and the coatom extension construction (R6), not any comparison or the chain construction; the comparison imports (R2) |
| (R3) Exact hollow-growth receiving (the growth construction, actual labels) | \(R\) a hollow model (presently formulated as cover-hollow, `Realization.IsCoverHollow`) with unbounded top-grade growth; any root, the empty root included; any one-point coface \(d\) of the root's type | a fresh point \(y\) with \(\operatorname{eval}(t^\frown y)=d\) exactly, tops included | the hollow comparison (Layer 4; compiled in this repository (theorem named) conditional on (R3) as the named hypothesis `Realization.HollowReceiving H`, with the hollowness predicate `H` a parameter: `Realization.nonempty_equiv_of_hollow`; the count takes `H` to be cover-hollowness at a block stage, `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock`; with the hollow property restricted to models without a globally rigid core, `Realization.HasRestrictedTerminalProperty`, the count takes instead `H` = `Realization.IsCoverHollowWithoutRigidCoreAtBlock`, cover-hollowness at a block stage without a globally rigid core, and the models with a globally rigid core go through the rigid-core comparison, which uses (R1) only; (R3) for `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock` implies (R3) for the restricted predicate, `Realization.HollowReceiving.withoutRigidCore`: weaker or equal, strictly weaker not shown) | imports the growth construction shared with (R4) and, for padding, the coatom extension construction (R6), not any comparison or the chain construction; the comparison imports (R3) |
| (R4) Capped receiving for the stable candidate (the growth construction, stable labels) | \(R\) a model at \(\alpha\) with non-hollow unbounded top-grade growth (presently formulated as not cover-hollow); a root of positive length; a one-point coface \(d\), at stage \(\alpha+\omega\), of the stable type of the root (its type in the structural stable candidate); an ordinal \(\gamma<\alpha+\omega\) | a fresh point \(y\) with \(t^\frown y\) actual, of type on the donor's scheme, whose stable value equals \(d\) at every cell where \(d\) is not top (bottom included) and exceeds \(\gamma\) at every cell where \(d\) is top; hence finite-cut receiving of the candidate over positive roots | stable modelhood (Layer 4), with the empty-root base case below and the cap-to-model theorem; the acquisition of its calibrated data, its occurrence, and its evaluation come after the structural candidate of Layer 4 | the construction imports the structural candidate (consistency and covering only), not its modelhood, and shares the growth construction with (R3); modelhood imports (R4) |
| (R5) Top-free pinned extension | a top-free chart \(P\) at a nonzero limit stage \(\beta\) (in the classical limit, the chart of a root; the stage is a nonzero limit so that a proper cap self-visible at the arity of \(Q\) exists above \(\delta\) and every label of \(P\)); a face of \(P\) with its literal restriction \(p\); any lawful one-point coface \(d\) of \(p\), not required to be top-free; any label \(\delta<\beta\), bottom included | a top-free one-point pinned extension \(Q\) of \(P\) whose restriction along the face extended by the new point is on the exact scheme of \(d\) and agrees with \(d\) below \(\delta\): the exact pinned extension (R6), capped at a proper cap self-visible at the arity of \(Q\) and above \(\delta\) and every label of \(P\), which is lawful [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.8] and leaves \(P\) unchanged | receiving in the classical limit (step 6 in the section on the top-free witnesses), which gives the top-free witnesses of the lower bound of Layer 6; step 6 uses only the case of a face onto the whole chart, where the pinned extension is the coface itself, capped, and no coatom extension is needed | imports the coatom extension construction (R6) and lawful capping; its case over a whole chart needs only lawful capping; the classical limit uses it only in step 6 and imports none of (R1)–(R4) |
| (R6) Exact pinned extension (the coatom extension construction) | any chart \(P\) at a limit stage; a face of \(P\) with its literal restriction \(p\); any one-point coface \(d\) of \(p\) | a one-point pinned extension \(Q\) of \(P\) whose restriction along the face extended by the new point is exactly \(d\), by one coatom extension for each point of \(P\) outside the face (reduced to the coatom extension property, whose proof is the completion of the amalgam in 3.1) | the amalgamation of top-free charts (step 2 in the section on the top-free witnesses, through the plain form of the coatom extension property) and (R5); also used in (R2) and (R3), in the empty-root base case of (R4), and in the cap-to-model theorem; outside the main theorem, the saturated model (through the plain form, reconstructed from the classical limit of the uncapped age) | imports only Layers 0–1, the stage types, the amalgam, and the section theorem of 3.1 (with the shared decoding lemma) |

**Base cases.** (R1) and (R2) hold for every root directly: the private context is acquired over any root, the empty root included. In (R3) the acquisition of the relative data needs a root of positive length; every root \(t\) is handled by padding one actual point \(z\), given by high-arity dominance, enlarging the donor by the exact pinned extension (R6) to a donor over \(t^\frown z\) that restricts literally to \(d\), receiving it exactly over \(t^\frown z\), and restricting. (R4) is stated for positive roots only. For the empty root, finite-cut receiving of the stable candidate uses its nonempty carrier, covering, and exact consistency, and the coatom extension construction: covering gives an actual chart of positive arity, the coatom extension amalgamates it with the one-point donor over the empty face, the positive-root statement receives the amalgam over that chart, and restriction by exact consistency gives the one-point occurrence. The cap-to-model theorem then gives stable modelhood. (R5) and (R6) include the empty chart and the empty face. In (R6) a face onto the whole chart, the empty chart in particular, needs no coatom extension: the coface itself, reindexed, is the extension; the empty face is the general statement over a face with no points.

**Status of (R2) and (R3): reformulations and the reduction to determination** (`Continuation/ExactReceiving`; compiled in this repository (theorem named)). Both are still to be proved; what is compiled is the following.

- *Exact reformulations.* (R2) is exact receiving, on any number of new points, of the legal stage types of top grade at most \(K\) in every model with no rigid core and top-grade supremum \(K\) (`Realization.residualReceiving_iff`); (R3) is exact receiving of all legal stage types in every model satisfying the hollowness predicate with unbounded growth (`Realization.hollowReceiving_iff`). The one-point form gives the multi-point form by the chain of closed faces (`Realization.ExactReceivingWithin.of_one_point`).
- *(R3) over a rigid core.* (R3) has no hypothesis excluding a globally rigid core, and the definitions do not exclude a cover-hollow model with unbounded growth that has one. Under (R3) such a core is rigid in every legal donor over its type (`Realization.HollowReceiving.isRigidCoreIn`). With (R3) for cover-hollowness (item 6 of the hypotheses of the main theorem below), the count uses (R3) at every cover-hollow model with unbounded growth, a globally rigid core included: the cover of the terminal models (`Realization.exists_hasTerminalProperty`) checks unbounded growth first and assigns the hollow property whatever the cores are, and the hollow property (`Realization.HasTerminalProperty`) does not exclude a globally rigid core. The restricted alternative (item 6′ below; `Continuation/RestrictedHollow`) excludes a globally rigid core from the hollow property and from the predicate given to (R3) (`Realization.HasRestrictedTerminalProperty`, `Realization.IsCoverHollowWithoutRigidCoreAtBlock`): the cover of the terminal models survives (`Realization.exists_hasRestrictedTerminalProperty`), models with a globally rigid core go through the rigid-core comparison, and the count needs (R3) only for that stronger predicate, which (R3) for cover-hollowness implies (`Realization.HollowReceiving.withoutRigidCore`, an instance of `Realization.HollowReceiving.mono`). It is a separate hypothesis set, not shown equivalent to item 6.
- *The rigid part is (R1).* Under finite-cut receiving at a limit stage, a one-point coface in which the root is a rigid core (a top-free coface in particular) is received exactly (`Realization.exists_covers_snoc_of_isRigidCoreIn`, the one-point case of the rigid-core receiving `Realization.exists_covers_of_isRigidCoreIn` of the rigid-core comparison). So (R2) and (R3) follow from (R1) for every model at every limit stage, together with their restrictions to the cofaces in which the root is not a rigid core (`Realization.ResidualReceiving.of_not_isRigidCoreIn`, `Realization.HollowReceiving.of_not_isRigidCoreIn`). This receiving hypothesis ranges over every limit stage at fixed universe levels and is not supplied by the countable-stage `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving`, the library's (R1) for models (item 2 of the hypotheses of the main theorem below; limit stages below \(\omega_1\), universe 0); the mismatch comes from (R2) and (R3), which are themselves stated at every limit stage. Neither the apex coatom extension property nor forcing donors is used there, and, informally (no non-implication is compiled), neither gives literal-top recovery: forcing donors bound labels at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\), not the label of a received cell at \(\lambda_\xi\).
- *Determination.* For a stage type \(t'\) on \(k\) points, \(h:\mathrm{Fin}\,n\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}\,k\), and a set \(U\) of stage types on \(k+1\) points, a one-point type \(d\) is *determined over \(t'\) along \(h\) within \(U\)* (`StageType.IsDeterminedWithin`) when every member of \(U\) with face \(t'\) along the initial segment has face \(d\) along \(h\) followed by the new point. A cover of \(t'\) realizing a member of \(U\) then receives \(d\) exactly over its restriction along \(h\) (`Realization.exists_covers_snoc_of_isDeterminedWithin`). The realized member comes from (R1) for the receiving family of a coface at a permitted cutoff, and from generalized saturation for the stage types on the scheme of a coface. The rigid-core case is the instance with \(h\) the identity (`StageType.isDeterminedWithin_receivingFamily_of_isRigidCoreIn`).
- *The reductions.* For a predicate \(P\) on acquired contexts \((t',h)\): (R2) follows from (R1) for every model at every limit stage (a receiving hypothesis that ranges over every limit stage at fixed universe levels and is not supplied by the countable-stage `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving`, as above), **residual acquisition** (`Realization.ResidualAcquisition P`: in every residual model every cover extends to a cover of an acquired context) and **cutoff determination** (`Realization.CutoffDetermination P`, a statement about stage types: over every legal acquired context every one-point coface of top grade at most \(K\) of the face along \(h\) is determined within the receiving family of a coface at a permitted cutoff) (`Realization.residualReceiving_of_cutoffDetermination`). (R3) follows from **hollow acquisition** (`Realization.HollowAcquisition H P`) and **scheme determination** (`Realization.SchemeDetermination P`, determination within the stage types on the scheme of a coface), with no receiving (`Realization.hollowReceiving_of_schemeDetermination`). In 3.3, the residual context is the private context with a private gap, \(D'\) the display on the LOW scheme, and LOW recovery is cutoff determination; the hollow context has the private cap and the marker labelled top, and the marker clause of `Correct` gives scheme determination. No predicate \(P\) is defined in the library, and neither acquisition nor determination is proved beyond the rigid-core instance.
- *Determination needs an acquired context.* Over a top-free root along the identity (the only case compiled), a coface that is not top-free is determined neither at a cutoff nor by a scheme: a cap of it is a top-free member of both families with the same root (`StageType.not_isDeterminedWithin_receivingFamily_of_isTopFree`, `StageType.not_isDeterminedWithin_saturationFamily_of_isTopFree`). So both determination statements fail for the predicate that is always true, at the empty root and the one-point stage type whose only cell is an apex labelled top (`Continuation/ExactReceivingExamples`). The library has no model with no rigid core and positive eventual top grade, and no model with unbounded growth. An ultrahomogeneous structure whose age is the age of legal charts has exact receiving of all legal donors (`exactReceivingWithin_reconstruct_of_legalAge`, `ClassicalLimit/LegalAge`), so the conclusion of (R3) holds for it; such a structure exists at a countable block stage, with a reconstruction that is a model, conditional on the coatom extension property with apex there (`exists_saturated_reconstruct`, `MainTheorem/SameLevelMaximal`), which is not proved, and its unbounded growth is not proved.

**The common core of the receiving routes (prescribed rows).** The open finite hypotheses of the routes to (R1)–(R4), and the completion of (R6), ask for objects over a context whose cells of full scope (graded index \((\mathrm{univ},g)\)) read prescribed cells in a prescribed way: (R2) and (R3) for a legal one-point extension whose cells of full scope read each new top of the donor at least as a private top; (R1) and (R4) for schemes (per block, a scheme on which every coface reads the labels of one block in the reading's own block; a scheme carrying a coface of the stage reduction that reads each new cell of the donor through the cap); the completion for a coatom extension, with no condition. `Extension/PrescribedFullRows` states one hypothesis on stage types, `StageType.HasPrescribedFullRows α`: every full-row prescription compatible with the faces (admissible rows — coded, lawful below \((\mathrm{univ},g)\) in both faces, transforming to the faces' labelling — at every lawful labelling of the context, `StageType.IsFaceCompatible`) has a prescribed extension, a legal one-point extension carrying the donor whose scheme meets it (so generalized saturation reaches it). Compatibility is necessary (`StageType.IsPrescribedExtension.isFaceCompatible`). The form that asks compatibility only at the labels of the context (with some lawful labelling of the donor) is false at every stage, at `GatedExtensionCounterexample.P α` (`PrescribedFullRowsCounterexample.not_hasPrescribedFullRowsAtLabels`): a reading of one cell at least as another at every cell of a graded index forces that order on every lawful labelling, and bountifulness at the cap \(\bot\) extends every lawful labelling of the context. The uniform form is not tested by that input (its compatibility premise fails there) and is open. `Extension/PrescribedFullRowsRoutes` proves, from the uniform form and the compatibility of each route's prescription, the reading context of (R2)/(R3) (`StageType.HasPrescribedFullRows.isReadingContext`; conversely a reading context makes some reading prescription compatible, `PrescribedFullRows.IsReadingContext.exists_isFaceCompatible`, so under the uniform form, at a legal input, the reading-context property is equivalent to a choice of private tops with a compatible reading prescription; obtaining a reading context from a graded context with a top is open and not derived from the uniform form; the reading context is a condition on stage types of the route to determination with a private top, not on `main`, and the reductions of (R2) and (R3) on `main` are those of the preceding paragraph) and the part of the (R4) condition concerning the scheme (the cap-reading scheme, `StageType.HasPrescribedFullRows.exists_isCapReadingScheme`; the composition to a stable recovery scheme is not compiled). For (R1) the arrow to block-tight saturations is not a reduction: the block prescription is not compatible wherever the context has a lawful labelling \(\bot\) at a block cell and not \(\bot\) at a cell of the top grade (`PrescribedFullRows.not_isFaceCompatible_block`), which holds at the per-block route's refuting context `CoupledGatedExtensionCounterexample.P α` for every \(\alpha>1\) (argued from compiled pieces of that route, not on `main`; the joining lemma is to be compiled once that route lands). At the empty prescription, under the uniform form the coatom extension property is equivalent to the compatibility of the empty prescription (`StageType.HasPrescribedFullRows.hasCoatomExtensions_iff`), itself open, so a general proof meets the open completion first. The canonical multi-layer step of a seed is not an instance (argued; it fixes the shape of the completion). These implications hold only at inputs where the compatibility hypothesis holds; the four routes' gaps are otherwise unchanged and separate, and none of (R1)–(R4) is refuted (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "The common core of the receiving routes").

(R1)–(R4) are the receiving statements used in the rest of the construction. (R5) and (R6) are used by the classical limit: step 6 uses (R5) only over a whole chart, where no coatom extension is needed, and step 2 uses the plain form of the coatom extension property, to which (R6) is reduced; (R6) is also used inside (R2), (R3), and (R5) and the cap-to-model theorem. The saturated model, reconstructed from the classical limit of the uncapped age, uses the plain form of the coatom extension property without capping. Share row calculations and occurrences over the root between the statements only where their complete hypotheses and conclusions agree. Receiving at a cutoff (R1) is weaker than literal-top recovery and is not a substitute for it.

The countable models that the main theorem needs are the top-free witnesses, obtained in the section after this layer. The finite work ends before any infinite construction: the finite extension constructions and the extensions of realizations by one occurrence import neither the classical limit nor the chain construction.

Direct limits of structures and the classical existence theorem stay outside this layer: they belong to the two libraries (Layer 0), and they replace neither a finite extension construction nor a decoding or recovery statement.

**Summit 3:** each of (R1)–(R4) of the table has an actual finite construction with its section theorem (3.1), a proof that the realization extends by one occurrence of it over the literal root (3.2), a recovery theorem for the observations its first use reads (3.2–3.3), and a proved first use; (R5) and (R6) have an actual finite construction (3.1) and a proved first use. An abstract form of one of these statements, a predicate asserting admissibility, or an assumed realization of a new auxiliary diagram is not a substitute for any of these. A statement whose first use lies in a later layer reaches this summit when that first use is proved; its construction still does not import the first use.

### The top-free witnesses: the finite age and its classical limit

Fix a countable block \(\eta\) and the stage \(\lambda=\lambda_\eta\), a nonzero countable limit. Let \(L_\lambda\) be the stage chart language at \(\lambda\) (relational, one symbol for each legal stage type at \(\lambda\)) and \(L^h_\lambda\) its definitional expansion by the hull operations (Layer 2); both have countably many symbols. The **age of top-free charts** at \(\lambda\) is the representative class (Layer 0) of the family indexed by the top-free legal stage types at \(\lambda\) of every finite arity, each read as a finite \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure (Layer 2, item 5). The top-free witness at \(\eta\) is obtained in seven steps, in this order.

1. **Finite top-free charts.** Construct the family: for each top-free legal stage type at \(\lambda\), its finite \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure, whose relations are literally its visible faces with their types. The index is countable and inhabited (the empty chart), and the members are finitely generated. Compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis beyond the countability of the stage: `topFreeAge`, `countable_topFreeIndex`, `fg_topFreeChart`, `nonempty_topFreeAge` (`ClassicalLimit/Age`).
2. **Hereditary closure, and amalgamation and joint embedding proved directly for finite charts.** The finitely generated substructures of a member are its closed faces with their literal restrictions, which are top-free legal stage types (Layer 2, items 3–5). Amalgamation: two top-free charts with face embeddings of a common chart are amalgamated over that common face by the plain form of the coatom extension property (Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)), and the amalgam is capped at a proper cap self-visible at its arity and above every label of the two charts, which is lawful [Kni26, Lemma 2.5.8], top-free, and restricts literally to both; the square of face embeddings commutes literally, as `Amalgamation` and `isFraisse_representativeClass` require. Joint embedding is amalgamation over the empty chart. Strong amalgamation is not claimed or needed: the property does not ask the two images to be disjoint outside the common chart. These are finite theorems, proved before and without any infinite model: amalgamation is proved before any infinite model is constructed, and is not established by embedding charts into the model whose existence the Fraïssé theorem is to supply. Compiled in this repository (theorem named): hereditary closure with no hypothesis (`exists_equiv_topFreeChart`, `hereditary_topFreeAge`, `ClassicalLimit/Age`); amalgamation and joint embedding conditional on the coatom extension property `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` at the stage (still to be proved) and on a stage that is a nonzero limit (`exists_amalgam_topFreeChart`, `exists_jointEmbedding_topFreeChart`, `amalgamation_topFreeAge`, `jointEmbedding_topFreeAge`, and, at a countable stage, `isFraisse_topFreeAge`; `ClassicalLimit/Amalgamation`). The capping step needs no hypothesis on the labels of the amalgam: it may carry top on its new cells, and the cap removes it.
3. **Classical existence.** `isFraisse_representativeClass` makes the age a Fraïssé class, and the classical existence theorem (Layer 0; available at the pin; `exists_isFraisseLimit_representativeClass`, or `exists_isFraisseLimit_of_isFraisse` once the age is a Fraïssé class) gives a countable \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure \(M\) that is a Fraïssé limit of the age. Compiled in this repository (theorem named), under the hypotheses of step 2 and the countability of the stage, from which the countability of the function symbols is derived: `exists_isFraisseLimit_topFreeAge` (`ClassicalLimit/Amalgamation`).
4. **Reconstruction of partial evaluation.** On an injective tuple \(t\) of \(M\), the evaluation is the stage type \(p\) with \(M\models P_p(t)\), and it is undefined when no chart relation holds at \(t\). The factorization of \(t\) through a representative (Layer 0) shows that the relations at \(t\) are literally those of one top-free chart at one tuple of its points, so the evaluation is well defined. Compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Reconstruction`, for a structure \(M\) whose age is contained in the age of top-free charts (top-free chart coverage), the limit being a hypothesis: the evaluation `reconstruct`, defined with no hypothesis on \(M\); the factorization of a tuple through one top-free chart (`exists_eq_trans_topFreeChart`), along which the evaluation is that of the face realization of the chart (`reconstruct_eval_trans_chart`, with `StageType.faceRealization`); literal recovery (`reconstruct_eval_eq_some_iff`, `relMap_rel_iff_reconstruct_eval`); uniqueness of the chart relation at a tuple (`eq_of_relMap_rel`); and the description of an evaluated tuple as the image of the points of a chart under an embedding of the chart (`reconstruct_eval_eq_some_iff_exists_embedding`).
5. **Consistency, covering, and top-freeness, each proved locally.** Each is a statement about one finite tuple and follows from its factorization through one representative: exact consistency (the evaluation of a face of an evaluated tuple is the literal restriction of its type, and undefined at an invisible face), covering (every finite tuple, the empty tuple and tuples with repeated coordinates included, lies in the points of an actual occurrence), top-freeness (every evaluated type is a member of the age), and a nonempty carrier (the one-point chart is in the age). Compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Reconstruction`: under top-free chart coverage, exact consistency (`isConsistent_reconstruct`), covering, for arbitrary tuples as well (`isCovering_reconstruct`, `exists_comp_eq_reconstruct_eval`), and top-freeness with every evaluated type in the age (`isTopFree_of_reconstruct_eval`, `mem_topFreeAge_of_reconstruct_eval`); a nonempty carrier when the age of top-free charts is contained in the age of \(M\) (`nonempty_of_topFreeAge_subset`); all of these, with legal types and the identification of the functions of \(M\) with the hull operations of the reconstruction, for a structure whose age is the age of top-free charts (`reconstruct_of_age_eq`), in particular for a Fraïssé limit of it (`reconstruct_of_isFraisseLimit`).
6. **Receiving, from finite pinned extension and ultrahomogeneity.** Let \(t\) be a root of type \(p\), \(d\) a one-point donor type over it, and \(\delta\) a permitted cutoff. (R5) of the table in 3.4 over the whole chart of \(p\) (the root is a whole actual occurrence, so the exact pinned extension of (R6) is the coface \(d\) itself and no coatom extension is needed), capped at a proper cap above \(\delta\) and every label of \(p\), self-visible at the arity of \(d\), gives a top-free one-point pinned extension \(Q\) of \(p\), a member of the age, that restricts to \(p\) literally along the root and agrees with \(d\) below \(\delta\) along the root followed by the new point. The chart embedding of \(p\) into \(Q\) is an embedding of finite \(L^h_\lambda\)-structures (Layer 2, items 3–5). `IsUltrahomogeneous.extend_embedding`, applied with \(S\) the finite \(L^h_\lambda\)-structure of \(p\), \(f\colon S\hookrightarrow M\) its embedding onto the points of \(t\) (the factorization of step 4: the points of an actual occurrence span a substructure isomorphic to the structure of its type), \(g\colon S\hookrightarrow Q\) the chart embedding, and `Nonempty (Q ↪[L] M)` because \(Q\) is in the age of \(M\), gives an embedding of \(Q\) into \(M\) that is literally the given one on \(t\). The image of the points of \(Q\) is one actual occurrence, and every equation of receiving concerns it: its restriction along the root is \(t\) with type \(p\), its new point is distinct from every point of the root (the embedding of \(Q\) is injective and is literally the given one on the root), and its type agrees with \(d\) below \(\delta\). For a top-free donor, a cutoff above every label of \(d\) makes agreement below the cutoff equality, and receiving is exact. For a donor containing top labels, receiving is approximation at each permitted cutoff: for every \(\delta\) there is an extension, depending on \(\delta\), serving the request at \(\delta\); there is not one extension for all cutoffs, and no top is recovered. Compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Receiving`, for a structure \(M\) whose age is the age of top-free charts and which is ultrahomogeneous, at a stage that is zero or a limit: `hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct`, with its form for a Fraïssé limit (`hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct_of_isFraisseLimit`), its form for donors on several new points (`hasFiniteExtensionReceiving_reconstruct`, through `HasFiniteCutReceiving.hasFiniteExtensionReceiving`), and exact extension of top-free donors within the age, with no stage hypothesis (`exists_reconstruct_eval_eq_of_isTopFree`). It uses the age equality (both inclusions), ultrahomogeneity, and capping, and neither the coatom extension property nor modelhood; only the existence of the limit (step 3, `exists_isFraisseLimit_topFreeAge`) needs `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions`.
7. **Modelhood, infinitude, and terminality.** The reconstructed realization has a nonempty carrier, exact consistency, covering, and the finite-cut receiving property, so it is a model in the sense of [Kni26, Definition 3.2.1] by the cap-to-model theorem (3.4). It is infinite, because a received point is fresh over a whole finite chart, not merely a new coordinate of a longer tuple. [Kni26, Proposition 4.3.23] supplies only the one-point coface: a stage type on one more point whose restriction is the given type; it says nothing about points of a realization. Freshness comes from the receiving criterion, all equations on one occurrence whose new point is distinct from every point of the root: given a finite set \(F\) of points, covering gives an actual occurrence \(t\) whose points contain \(F\); take \(t\) itself as the root and a one-point coface \(d\) of its type (Proposition 4.3.23); receiving gives one occurrence extending \(t\) literally, injective, so its new point lies outside the whole chart \(t\), hence outside \(F\). It is terminal: a model expansion to the next block changes only top labels, and there are none, whereas a model at the next block has labels in the new block \([\lambda,\lambda+\omega)\) (uniformity). Its base class lies in the loss at \(\eta\) by uniqueness of expansion and same-carrier transport, which exclude another, higher expansion of its base reduct (Layer 6). The base-reduct part is compiled in this repository (theorem named): for a structure whose age is the age of top-free charts and which is ultrahomogeneous, at a limit stage at least \(\omega\), the base-language structure of the reduction of the reconstruction to \(\omega\) satisfies the density sentence (`realize_densitySentence_reconstruct_reduce`, `ClassicalLimit/Receiving`), by legal types, exact consistency, and covering of the reduction and the descent of receiving along stage reduction (`hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct_reduce`, through `HasFiniteCutReceiving.reduce`); modelhood is not used and not claimed. Modelhood at \(\lambda\), infinitude, and terminality are compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Modelhood`, for a structure whose age is the age of top-free charts and which is ultrahomogeneous, at a nonzero limit stage: modelhood by the cap-to-model theorem at a limit stage (`Realization.isModel_of_hasFiniteCutReceiving`, `Realization/CapToModel`), whose instances of uniformity and dominance are nonempty under the coatom extension property with apex (`isModel_reconstruct`, with those instances as hypotheses, and `isModel_reconstruct_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`); infinitude under the plain property (`infinite_of_age_eq_of_hasCoatomExtensions`); and terminality, with no hypothesis beyond top-free chart coverage: no model at a higher stage reduces to the reconstruction (`reduce_ne_reconstruct`). The placement of the base class in the loss at \(\eta\) is compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `MainTheorem/LowerBound`, conditional on uniqueness of the model expansions at \(\lambda_\eta\) only (`nonempty_loss_of_topFreeWitness`), which follows from next-block uniqueness (`eq_reconstruct_of_nextBlockUniqueness`) and holds with no hypothesis at \(\eta=0\) (`eq_reconstruct_of_blockStage_zero`); per block, `nonempty_loss_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, conditional on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at the block stage, which also gives the existence of the limit (step 3). So step 7 is compiled conditionally on the coatom extension property with apex at the block stage, still to be proved, and on uniqueness at \(\lambda_\eta\), derived from `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness` (none at \(\eta=0\)); it is derived from these hypotheses, not proved.

**How the coatom completion feeds the limit.** The apex form of the coatom extension property at a stage that is zero or a limit (checkpoints 2.1–2.7 of Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)) gives the plain form. The plain form gives the amalgamation of legal stage types over a common face, which capped is step 2, and the exact pinned extension of (R6), which capped is (R5). Step 6 uses only the case of (R5) over a whole chart, the capped coface, which needs no coatom extension, with ultrahomogeneity and the age equality; the completion reaches step 6 only through the existence of the limit (step 3). The completion is used only through these finite statements.

**Complementary global routes** (separate statements, with their own hypotheses; none is a consequence of the finite lifting statements of Layer 3, 3.1, or of structural successor leastness, Layer 4). Classical Fraïssé existence (ComputableModelTheory, available at the pin; step 3) gives terminal examples at one level: the top-free witness of each countable block is terminal at that block (compiled conditionally (step 7), with the hypotheses recorded there). Scott isolation for one class, with countable-limit existence (closure of the expansion domains under countable limits, condition 1), gives maximal presentations of a prescribed base (the Scott route to maximal presentations, item 5 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)"; concordance rows 40 and 31–36; prospective). Terminal presentations give countability: through the countable cover of the terminal classes at each countable stage (terminal countability, condition 2, Layer 4), the classes with a terminal presentation at a given countable index are countably many (prospective). Global termination remains a companion route (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Further companion results", terminal refinement), even where the spectrum theorem avoids it: the count of the expansion-domain route bounds the persistent core by one class and does not assume eventual departure (Layer 6).

**Acceptance criterion of finite-age reconstruction.** An abstract Fraïssé limit is not by itself a realization. Finite-age reconstruction is complete only when its steps 4–6 prove, for the structure \(M\) of step 3:

- **literal recovery of chart relations:** \(M\models P_p(t)\) exactly when \(t\) is injective and its evaluation is \(p\);
- **uniqueness and injectivity of labelled tuples:** at most one chart relation holds at a tuple, and a tuple at which one holds is injective;
- **exact partial restriction:** the evaluation of a face of an evaluated tuple is the literal restriction of its type, invisible faces returning `none`;
- **covering:** every finite tuple, the empty tuple and tuples with repeated coordinates included, lies in the points of an actual occurrence;
- **receiving with all equations attached to one occurrence:** the root, the fresh point, and the agreement with the donor below the cutoff all concern the one occurrence of step 6;
- **the literal roundtrip, in both directions:** reconstructing from a canonical expanded realization (a realization with its definitional expansion) returns that realization; expanding a reconstructed, locally charted structure (every finite tuple in a finitely generated substructure isomorphic to a chart) by the definable hull operations returns its original relations and its original functions (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §5). Local chart coverage suffices for the identification of the functions; homogeneity is needed later, for receiving, and not for the roundtrip. Special cases: empty carriers where the language allows them, repeated inputs, and the default values where no chart witness exists.

Receiving itself uses only the literal recovery of the chart relations. Status: compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `ClassicalLimit/Reconstruction`, under top-free chart coverage: literal recovery, uniqueness and injectivity (`reconstruct_eval_eq_some_iff`, `relMap_rel_iff_reconstruct_eval`, `eq_of_relMap_rel`, `injective_of_relMap_rel`), exact partial restriction (`isConsistent_reconstruct`), and covering (`isCovering_reconstruct`); the roundtrip in direction (a) for every realization with legal types (`reconstruct_toHullStructure`) and in direction (b) for structures covered by top-free charts (`toHullStructure_reconstruct`), for the operations `Realization.hullOp`, which on an exactly consistent covering realization are those defined by the graph formula (`Realization.realize_graphFormula_iff`); the special cases in `ClassicalLimit/ReconstructionExamples`. Receiving with all its equations on one occurrence is compiled in `ClassicalLimit/Receiving`, for a structure whose age is the age of top-free charts and which is ultrahomogeneous (`hasFiniteCutReceiving_reconstruct`, step 6). Direction (b) of the roundtrip under local chart coverage by arbitrary charts is still to be proved. This criterion is item 11 of `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`.

**The chain construction.** The countable chain construction on finite master conditions (the dense-set chain theorem of `Construction/DenseChain.lean`, the chain unions of partial realizations, and the conditional chain construction of models from finite extension statements taken as hypotheses) is needed neither for top-free existence nor for saturated existence, and no step toward the main theorem uses it. The countable saturated model of [Kni26, Definition 4.1.1 and Proposition 4.4.5], which realizes every one-point coface of every occurrence exactly, is, at a stage that is zero or a limit, reconstructed from the classical limit of the uncapped age of all legal stage types at that stage: that class is hereditary, amalgamates by the plain form of the coatom extension property (without capping), and has countably many isomorphism types, and its limit is saturated by `IsUltrahomogeneous.extend_embedding` and [Kni26, Proposition 4.3.23]. Compiled in this repository (theorem named): the uncapped age and its limit (`isFraisse_legalAge`, `exists_isFraisseLimit_legalAge`, conditional on `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions`), in `ClassicalLimit/LegalAge`, and the saturated model reconstructed from it at every countable block stage \(\lambda_\beta\), \(\beta<\omega_1\) (stage zero is not covered), a model with exact receiving of every legal donor (`exists_saturated_reconstruct`, conditional on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions`), which is acceptance lemma 1 ("Reduction to full presentations") with terminality added under `ForcingDonors` (`exists_sameLevelMaximal`, `MainTheorem/SameLevelMaximal`). The chain construction is retained for an effective presentation only, by an explicit enumeration of requirements (optional developments below); this route is conditional on effective input data, an effective enumeration of the age and an effective amalgamation procedure, since the classical Fraïssé construction uses choice and supplies no computable presentation. The partial-realization statements of the conditional chain development (`StageType.chartRealization`, `StageType.isConsistent_chartRealization`, `StageType.chartRealization_eval_eq_none_iff`, in `Realization/Partial.lean`, outside `Construction/`, in the library) are not used by steps 1–5 as compiled: there the finite structure of a chart is the face realization of its stage type (`StageType.faceRealization`, `StageType.faceRealization_eval`, in `Realization/Partial.lean`; `StageType.relMap_chart`), of which the partial realization is the placement on \(\mathbb N\), and the relations at a tuple of the limit are those of the face realization of a chart through which the tuple factors (`reconstruct_eval_trans_chart`). The import boundary below holds either way. The chain-union statements are not used by steps 1–7.

**Import boundary.** Steps 1–7 import Mathlib, the two libraries of Layer 0, Layers 0–2, and the finite kernel: Layer 1, the coatom extension construction with (R5) and (R6), and the cap-to-model theorem of 3.4. No module of this argument imports a `Construction/` module, and the classical theorems it quotes import no module of this repository.

**Summit (top-free witnesses):** at every countable block, the top-free witness is obtained by finite-age reconstruction from a Fraïssé limit of the age of top-free charts, meeting the acceptance criterion above, with steps 1–7 proved in this order. Receiving for donors containing top labels is proved at each permitted cutoff separately.

### Layer 4 — Structural continuation and terminal comparisons

**Higher-stage reconstruction** (a milestone of Layers 4–5; the second of the two reconstruction problems of the section "The intended construction"). From a base realization, recover the canonical, partially defined tower of its expansions: its expansion to each stage \(\lambda_\xi\) to which it expands, on an initial segment of the stages. The milestone has five outputs, each a statement still to be proved in full (the status paragraphs of Layers 4 and 5 record what is compiled, and under which hypotheses); Layers 4 and 5 state them, and this list maps that text onto them:

1. **the structural candidate:** a realization at the next block whose labels are recovered from finite observations, the stable values of the provisional observations on actual rooted covers, built from consistency and covering, with lawfulness, exact partial evaluation, and literal reduct (the first paragraph below; `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 7);
2. **normalization:** every actual next-block model expansion agrees with that candidate, pointwise, undefined tuples included (the second paragraph below), refined by structural successor leastness (the status paragraph below: the candidate is to be at most every coherent next-block assignment, and among the structural successors it is to be the least, literal weakening being the greatest);
3. **a separate modelhood criterion:** non-hollow unbounded top-grade growth makes the candidate a model, by (R4) of the table of Layer 3 with its empty-root base case and the cap-to-model theorem (the second paragraph below);
4. **partial expansions:** reduction of a model expansion to a lower stage (Layer 2), uniqueness of the expansion at each stage, coherence of the expansions at different stages under reduction, and transport under isomorphism with same-carrier normalization (Layer 5, first paragraph; `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 9);
5. **limit existence:** for a nonzero countable limit ordinal \(\delta\), a base model with model expansions to \(\lambda_\xi\) for every \(\xi<\delta\) has a model expansion to \(\lambda_\delta\), the coherence of the lower expansions being derived from uniqueness (output 4), not assumed (Layer 5, first paragraph; condition 1 of the reduction to expansion domains).

That the candidate is uniquely determined (outputs 1 and 2) never substitutes for its existence as a model (output 3): normalization says that every expansion is the candidate, not that one exists. Finite-age reconstruction is not a case of this milestone: it constructs the top-free witnesses from a finite age, and output 4 then places their base classes in the losses (Layer 6). Coherent assignments are not models either: a family of stage types of one tuple, one at each level up to \(\alpha\) and coherent under stage reduction, is finite data at each level, and that it is the family of chart types of that tuple in model expansions of one base model is a statement about actual expansions (outputs 3–5), not a consequence of coherence. If the levels are encoded in one ambient set, the fixed-point sets of the projections are nested, and a least fixing index is spoken of only for a point that has a fixing index (the section "Reduction to full presentations", "Level observations").

Construct the provisional observations on actual rooted covers. Prove the geometric directedness and transported-cell equations before applying the numerical limit lemma. Build the stable candidate from consistency and covering; prove lawfulness, its exact partial evaluation, and literal reduct before proving modelhood.

Show that every genuine next-block model expansion equals the structural candidate by pointwise normalization. Keep this uniqueness direction separate from existence. For non-hollow unbounded top-grade growth, construct finite cap receiving of the candidate (statement (R4) of the table of Layer 3, with its empty-root base case) and apply the cap-to-model theorem (Layer 3, 3.4). The selected-occurrence construction is not a required intermediate step.

Retain the original no-anchor predicate as the definition of hollowness (the meaning of "hollow" in `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 8), and prove its equivalent stable-label fixedness formulation for models. The compiled statements are presently formulated with **cover-hollowness** (`Realization.IsCoverHollow`, `Continuation/Hollow`), a distinct named predicate: a realization at \(\lambda_\xi\) is cover-hollow when it has no **anchor at the top**, that is, no occurrence with a cell labelled the formal top and a bound \(N\) such that no rooted cover compatible with the occurrence forces the threshold \(N\) at that cell (forcing of thresholds, `StageType.ForcesThreshold`; the status paragraph below). Its stable-label fixedness form, that every cell labelled the formal top has the formal top as its stable label, holds for every realization at a block stage, with no hypothesis (`Realization.isCoverHollow_iff_forall_stableLabel_eq_top`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). Output 3, the count of terminal classes, (R3), (R4), acceptance lemma 1 ("Reduction to full presentations"), and the terminality of cover-hollow realizations (`Realization.IsCoverHollow.isTerminalAt`) are presently stated with cover-hollowness. The equivalence of cover-hollowness with the original no-anchor predicate is still to be proved; until it is proved, the compiled statements concern cover-hollowness, not the hollowness of the semantic contract. Define top-grade growth directly through covers, not through characteristic arity (`Realization.topGradeSup`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). Prove the three comparisons:

- the **rigid-core comparison**: same actual globally rigid-core type implies isomorphism (the roadmap's working definition, to be made precise with this comparison: a finite core is **rigid** in a stage type containing it, through a face embedding, when the only admissible top support of that type containing the top cells supported on the core is the whole set of top cells of the type; it is **globally rigid** in a model when it is rigid in the type of every actual chart containing it. Its intended content: a set \(H\) of top cells of a lawful labelling \(p\) is an **admissible top support** when some lawful labelling of the same scheme agrees with \(p\) on every non-top label, keeps exactly the cells of \(H\) at top, and gives the other top cells of \(p\) proper labels below the stage; so a core is rigid when every lawful relabelling that fixes the non-top labels and keeps the core's top cells at top, lowering the others to proper labels below the stage, keeps all top cells at top. In the slogan of `EXPOSITIONS.md`, §3, rigidity concerns admissible top supports, not the absence of automorphisms);
- the **residual comparison**: no rigid core and the same positive eventual top grade imply isomorphism;
- the **hollow comparison**: hollow top-grade growth implies mutual isomorphism.

**Terminal classification by exact ages** (the generic comparison is compiled in this repository (theorem named), the status paragraph below; the named-core statements and the exact-age Scott sentences are still to be proved). The two coreless comparisons, residual and hollow, are instances of one exact-age comparison theorem: two countable exactly consistent covering realizations with the same exact age (the set of types of their actual occurrences) and exact receiving, over every root, of every one-point coface in that age are isomorphic, by back-and-forth over literal roots (one point at a time along a chain of closed sets from the root's support to an actual occurrence containing the new point); (R2) and (R3) of the table in Layer 3 supply the exact receiving. The rigid-core case is treated in the language naming the supplied finite core by constants: the comparison, and the analogues of B2 and B3.1–B3.2, B3.4 of `COMPANIONS.md` (local automorphisms, orbit formulas, atomicity, primeness), are stated and proved in that language, from the exact receiving over roots containing the core (not from top-freeness); the rank bound B3.3 needs a relational language, so for it the core is named by one unary relation per core point. The class of charts there is not "all types extending the core type" unless its admissibility conditions are proved (Layer 0): rigidity controls the actual containing charts, not arbitrary extensions of the root type. **Exact-age Scott sentences**, in the full stage chart language (they characterize the realization among countable structures of that language, not its base reduct). For each of the three coreless exact ages (top-free, residual, hollow), an explicit sentence: the structural clauses (exact consistency and covering), the exclusion of every chart type outside the age, and exact receiving, over every actual root, of every one-point coface in the age, retaining the root. For the rigid-core case, the same with the core named: a formula whose free variables are the core, requesting exact receiving over every root containing the core only for those one-point cofaces in which the core is still a rigid core (rigid in the coface through the embedding of the core, in the sense of the rigid-core comparison above), and excluding the actual one-point extensions of core-containing roots whose types are not of this kind (a property of the rigid-core terminal model, still to be proved). Existentially quantifying the core gives a Scott sentence of the realization: any two countable structures, each with some core satisfying the formula, are isomorphic by the named-core comparison, still to be proved. The syntactic-complexity target is a \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) sentence in the coreless cases and after naming the core, and a \(\Sigma^{\mathrm{in}}_3\) sentence after existentially quantifying those constants away: the \(\exists\)-atomic case in the language naming the core, and Scott rank 1 over parameters, in the correspondence of [Mon, Theorem II.23 and Proposition II.26 (§II.5), and Table 1 of §II.7] (Theorem II.23 for the parameterless \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) case). The hierarchy itself is InfinitaryLogic's `Lomega1omega/InHierarchy` (available at the pin; Layer 0). The target is stated in its signed complexity classes (`IsPiIn`, `IsSigmaIn`), which are syntactic and contain [Mon]'s normal forms (`IsPiInNF`, `IsSigmaInNF`) without being identified with them; a normal-form statement would be a separate claim. The general theorem behind Theorem II.23's (U1) ⇒ (U2), Montalbán's explicit sentence of a family of orbit formulas (Layer 0), is available at the pin (signatures checked): it supplies a Scott sentence from suitable orbit formulas, in `IsPiIn (α + 1)` for orbit formulas in `IsSigmaIn α` with `1 ≤ α` (level zero has a separate \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) result), and the displayed sentence includes the atomic diagram \(D_a\) of each tuple. It says nothing about the particular exact-age sentence above or its literal complexity: that this sentence characterizes the realization, and that it lies in \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) (coreless, or with the core named) or in \(\Sigma^{\mathrm{in}}_3\) (after quantifying the core), remain construction-specific statements still to be proved. Its application to the top-free witness, which gives the existence of some \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) Scott sentence once the orbit formulas are shown to be in `IsSigmaIn 1`, is `COMPANIONS.md`, B4. For the exact-age sentence, its characterization and its complexity are separate statements: the requests are to be written in finitary form, or normalized, before a syntactic \(\Pi^{\mathrm{in}}_2\) classification applies. This target is distinct from the internal Scott-rank bound \(\le\omega\) of Layer 0: it is not derived from that bound, and no implication between them is asserted.

**The literal form of the exact-age sentences** (statements still to be proved; full stage chart language only). A request is the clause asking for exact receiving of one one-point coface over every actual root of one type: a universal quantifier over the root, the root's chart relation as antecedent, and an existential block over the donor occurrence. In the normalized sentence:

- the root equations, identifying the root with coordinates of the donor occurrence, are a finite first-order conjunction inside the donor's existential block; a countable conjunction over a finite or empty index is still infinitary syntax, and its signed level is not \(0\);
- when the existential block of a request is moved out of its implication, the request keeps an alternative stating that the antecedent fails: moving a positive existential block out of an implication changes truth on the empty carrier;
- the signed classes (`IsSigmaIn`, `IsPiIn`) are kept apart from the normal forms (`IsSigmaInNF`, `IsPiInNF`): the target of this layer is a signed class, and membership in a signed class gives no bound on quantifier rank;
- optional further targets are literal normal forms, a conjunction over the requests of \(\forall\bar x\bigvee_j\exists\bar y_j\) with a finite quantifier-free matrix, obtained by flattening the request indices rather than by conjoining normal forms: `IsPiInNF 2` for the coreless sentences and for the formula with the core named, and `IsSigmaInNF 3` after existentially quantifying the core. These carry the quantifier-rank bounds \(\omega\cdot2\) and \(\omega\cdot3\) by `IsPiInNF.qrank_le` and `IsSigmaInNF.qrank_le` (InfinitaryLogic, available at the pin (signatures checked)); the bounds concern the normalized formulas, not the original syntax trees.

Qualifications for the pointed core formula: it asserts that the core's coordinates are distinct, so a non-injective parameter tuple satisfies it in no structure; and the empty carrier is handled explicitly (a core of positive arity has no realization there), not by convention. None of these targets is a statement about the base reduct or about the models of \(\Phi\), whose Scott ranks are unbounded (the paragraph "Standard definitions"). Base-language counterparts (definitions of the chart predicates at \(\lambda_\eta\), a sentence defining \(D_\eta\) relative to base models, and absolute Scott sentences of supplied base models, obtained by substituting those definitions into these sentences), with bounds on the quantifier rank of the constructed syntax, are the prospective quantitative-reconstruction pathway of `COMPANIONS.md`, "Further companion results", used by neither route to the main theorem; its bounds are syntax bounds, not Scott ranks of base classes, and it keeps the generic existence of a Scott sentence apart from the literal complexity of the exact-age sentences, as above.

Count terminal classes using the countable family of conditions, where \(S^\alpha_n\) is the set of stage types at \(\alpha\) on \(n\) points (the types of rigid cores),
\[
\left(\sum_n S_n^\alpha\right)\sqcup\mathbb N\sqcup\{*\}.
\]
Derive positivity rather than excluding grade zero by assumption. Non-hollow growth prolongation is enough to cover terminal models; a full biconditional characterization of all possible continuation is not a required strengthening (for the main theorem; the domain guard of the quantitative-reconstruction pathway, `COMPANIONS.md`, "Further companion results", needs the converse of output 3 for models, that a model at \(\lambda_\eta\) with a model expansion to \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\) is non-hollow with unbounded top-grade growth; its cover-hollow half, that a realization at \(\lambda_\eta\) with a model expansion to \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\) is not cover-hollow, is compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis, as the contrapositive of `Realization.IsCoverHollow.isTerminalAt` (`Continuation/Hollow`); the growth half, and the hollow half for the original no-anchor predicate (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 8), are still to be proved).

These conditions, and the stage types that index the sentences of this layer, are countably many at each countable stage, and \(\aleph_1\) many over all countable stages. Every sentence of this layer, and of the pathway of `COMPANIONS.md`, is written at one countable stage; their disjunction over all countable stages, or over all stage types of all stages, is not a sentence of \(L_{\omega_1,\omega}\) (a disjunction over uncountably many formulas is not a formula), and no statement here uses one.

**Status.** Each item carries its marker. A conditional theorem is listed with its named hypotheses; each hypothesis has its own status (`DASHBOARD.md`).

- *Output 1, the structural candidate.* Compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `Continuation/Candidate`, for a realization \(R\) at \(\lambda_\xi\): the stable section of a typed tuple (`Realization.stableSection`: the stable label at the cells labelled the formal top, the actual label elsewhere); the stable candidate at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\), defined given the lawfulness of the stable sections (`Realization.stableCandidate`, with the hypothesis `Realization.IsStablyLawful`), with literal reduct (`Realization.stableCandidate_reduce`) and with legal types, covering, and exact consistency from those of \(R\) (`Realization.hasLegalTypes_stableCandidate`, `Realization.isCovering_stableCandidate`, `Realization.isConsistent_stableCandidate`); and lawfulness law by law: the order law with no hypothesis (`Realization.orderly_stableSection`), and locality from exact consistency and covering (`Realization.locality_stableSection`, through the face compatibility of stable offsets, `Realization.stableOffset_comap`). Availability is compiled when no type of \(R\) has two cells labelled the formal top at one graded index (such cells are **twins**; `Realization.availability_stableSection_of_injOn`, `Realization.isStablyLawful_of_injOn_gradedIndex`), for a cover-hollow realization (`Realization.isStablyLawful_of_isCoverHollow`), and for the reduction of an exactly consistent realization at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) with legal types and finite-extension receiving (for a model, from (R1)), under forcing donors (`Realization.isStablyLawful_of_reduce_eq`). **Availability at twins is compiled for every realization with legal types** (`Realization.availability_stableSection_of_hasLegalTypes`): a legal rooted cover that forces a threshold at a cell labelled the formal top forces it, over the same cover, at a cell labelled the formal top at the graded index of the second cell (`StageType.exists_forcesThreshold_twin_face`, `Stage/Threshold`). Hence every exactly consistent covering realization with legal types, in particular every model, is stably lawful (`Realization.isStablyLawful_of_hasLegalTypes`, `Realization.IsModel.isStablyLawful`). Two hypotheses on single stage types examined for it are refuted, each a negative special case named in `Continuation/CandidateCounterexamples`: synchronizing cofaces, in three forms (`Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_unrestrictedSynchronizingCofaces`, `Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_synchronizingCofaces_blockStage`, `Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_synchronizingCofacesOnLifts`), and twin ordering (`Continuation.CandidateCounterexamples.not_twinOrdering_blockStage`). On the type that refutes twin ordering every lift gives the cell of smaller scope the label of the larger twin, and both orders of the twins occur; the transfer that holds happens inside the forcing cover, from a forced level, which statements about every lift ignore.
- *Output 2, normalization.* Compiled in this repository (theorem named), in `Stage/Threshold` and `Continuation/Normalization`: the forcing of a threshold by a rooted cover and the provisional offset (`StageType.ForcesThreshold`, `StageType.provisionalOffset`); the stable offset and the stable label as a supremum over rooted covers (`Realization.stableOffset`, `Realization.stableLabel`), with the single-cover characterization unconditional (`Realization.coe_add_le_stableLabel_iff`); soundness, from exact consistency alone (`Realization.Covers.le_label_of_forcesThreshold`); and the threshold lemma, the normalization of labels, and the determination of a realization at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\) by its reduction (`Realization.le_label_iff_exists_forcesThreshold`, `Realization.label_eq_stableLabel`, `Realization.eq_of_reduce_eq_of_forcingDonors`), conditional on finite-extension receiving (from (R1)) and on **forcing donors** (`ForcingDonors`: at a legal stage type at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\), every threshold \(n\) with \(\lambda_\eta+n\) at most the label of a cell whose reduction is the formal top is forced by the reduction of a legal extension of the type), both still to be proved. Forcing donors is a finite statement about legal stage types; it follows from the coatom extension property at \(\lambda_{\eta+1}\), by a finite construction of Layer 3 without (R1) (`forcingDonors_of_hasCoatomExtensions`, `Extension/ForcingDonorsCoatom`; Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)), and is compiled unconditionally for one- and two-point inputs up to the threshold \(4\). Next-block uniqueness follows (Layer 5, "Status").
- *Output 2, refined: structural successor leastness* (the "least" property a target, prospective; the other statements as marked). A **coherent next-block assignment** for a realization \(R\) at \(\lambda_\xi\) is a lawful next-block assignment of its cells: a restriction-compatible labelling of \(R\) (Layer 3, 3.2) whose value at each actual occurrence is a lift of its type to \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) (Layer 3, 3.1, "The attained least lift"). The **finite-cover minimum** of a cell at a rooted cover is the label there, on the face of the root, of the least lift of the cover's type; until the least lift is proved, the label \(\operatorname{ofOffset}\) of the provisional offset, which is attained cell by cell (`StageType.exists_lift_label_eq_ofOffset`). The target: every coherent next-block assignment dominates every finite-cover minimum, since its value at the cover is a lift whose face along the root is its value at the root; taking suprema in \(\mathbb N_\infty\) (`Realization.stableOffset`) and decoding the next block (`Label.ofOffset`), it is at least the stable section (`Realization.stableSection`) at every cell (these three defined in this repository), so the stable candidate (`Realization.stableCandidate`, defined in this repository), where it is defined, is the least coherent next-block assignment. Compiled in this repository (theorem named): the candidate, defined given its lawfulness, with literal reduct, legal types, covering, and exact consistency (output 1 above); soundness (`Realization.Covers.le_label_of_forcesThreshold`), which is the domination, one forced threshold at a time, for a coherent next-block assignment that is an exactly consistent realization at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) reducing to \(R\); and, conditional on finite-extension receiving (from (R1)) and on `ForcingDonors`, the equality of such a realization, with legal types, with the candidate (`Realization.label_eq_stableLabel`). The minimum is attained, the candidate being itself a coherent next-block assignment, when the candidate is lawful: compiled in this repository (theorem named) without twins and for a cover-hollow realization (output 1 above); for every exactly consistent covering realization with legal types, in particular for every model (`Realization.isStablyLawful_of_hasLegalTypes`, `Realization.IsModel.isStablyLawful`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), by a proof that uses exact consistency, covering, and legal types only: no receiving, and no clause of modelhood beyond these. The "least" property itself, that the stable section is at most every coherent next-block assignment at every cell, is not yet stated: a separate target (prospective). Stage hypothesis: \(\lambda_\xi\) a block stage, a limit; a form derived from the attained least lift carries its stage hypothesis.
- *The least and the greatest structural successor* (prospective; Layer 4; no statement of this item is a theorem of this repository unless one is named). Let \(R\) be a consistent realization at \(\lambda_\xi\), a nonzero limit stage. A **structural successor** of \(R\) is a consistent realization at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) whose reduct to \(\lambda_\xi\) is \(R\): a coherent next-block assignment that is itself a realization, whose value at each occurrence is a lift of its type (Layer 3, 3.1); it is not assumed to be a model. The target: the assignment of labels to the finite occurrences of \(R\) has, among the structural successors, a least and a greatest, each attained, each one coherent realization, each least (greatest) at every coordinate at once, and every structural successor lies between them, cell by cell. The **greatest structural successor** is literal weakening: \(R\) read at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\), each type by `StageType.castLE`, the formal top kept; it is a structural successor and the greatest from the consistency of \(R\) alone. The **least structural successor** is the stable candidate (`Realization.stableCandidate`, defined in this repository), when \(R\) is also covering and the candidate is lawful (output 1 above); covering is used for the least only. Neither uses the countability of the carrier or a nonempty carrier. Not claimed: that a labelling between the two is lawful, or that the structural successors are closed under pointwise minima (Layer 3, 3.1, "Least, not unique"). The rows do not change: the static semantic rows of each scheme stay fixed, and stage projection changes only the labels; any other representation, such as the variable rows of the templates of [AFK26] (item 1 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)"), is used here only through a transport checked to preserve lawfulness, cells, faces, and reduction, not through a substitution of notation.
- *Cover-hollowness as the equality of the least and the greatest structural successor* (prospective; Layer 4; a statement about models, beside the classification of this layer). For a **model** \(R\) at \(\lambda_\xi\), the least and the greatest structural successor are equal exactly when \(R\) is cover-hollow (`Realization.IsCoverHollow`). For a cover-hollow \(R\) the candidate is lawful (`Realization.isStablyLawful_of_isCoverHollow`, with no hypothesis) and is literal weakening (`Realization.stableCandidate_eval_of_isCoverHollow`), both compiled in this repository (theorem named). Conversely, a model is stably lawful (`Realization.IsModel.isStablyLawful`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), so its candidate is defined, and it is literal weakening only if every cell labelled the formal top has the formal top as its stable label, which is cover-hollowness (`Realization.isCoverHollow_iff_forall_stableLabel_eq_top`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). The equality of the candidate with literal weakening is equivalent to cover-hollowness for every stably lawful realization; it is stated here for models, where the candidate is a structural successor (the least one, by the preceding item). Then the structural successors of \(R\) form a subsingleton, and every structural successor of a cover-hollow model is the stable candidate itself. The predicate is cover-hollowness, the one of the compiled statements; `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 8, is unchanged. This is not a continuation theorem: a structural successor need not be a model, and this uniqueness supplies no expansion (for a cover-hollow realization the candidate is never a model, `Realization.not_isModel_stableCandidate_of_isCoverHollow`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). For a model that is not cover-hollow the two differ, which does not contradict the uniqueness of model expansions (output 2), since that uniqueness concerns model expansions and a structural successor need not be one. The equivalence is stated for models only and is not extended to arbitrary realizations. It is compatible with the nonuniqueness of finite lifts (Layer 3, 3.1, "Least, not unique"): one finite type with a cell labelled the formal top has distinct lawful lifts, while a coherent structural successor of a cover-hollow model, compatible with all its covers, keeps that top; lifting one observation and lifting the whole realization coherently are different statements.
- *Output 3, the modelhood criterion.* Compiled conditionally (theorem named), on (R4) and the coface instances at the next block: `Realization.isModel_stableCandidate` and `ContinuationCriterion.of_stableCappedReceiving` (`Continuation/Continuation`; with the coatom extension property with apex at every next block, `ContinuationCriterion.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, `MainTheorem/CapToModel`). It enters the count as the named hypothesis `ContinuationCriterion` (`Continuation/Classification`; sufficiency only: a model at \(\lambda_\xi\), \(\xi<\omega_1\), that is not cover-hollow and has unbounded top-grade growth has a model expansion to \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\)); its converse is not used. The cover-hollow half of the converse, that a realization at \(\lambda_\xi\) with a model expansion to \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) is not cover-hollow, is compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis (`Realization.IsCoverHollow.isTerminalAt`, `Continuation/Hollow`); the growth half, and the hollow half for the original no-anchor predicate, are still to be proved. Its intended proof, by `Realization.isModel_of_hasFiniteCutReceiving` at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) applied to the stable candidate, needs (R4) over positive roots (still to be proved), the empty root through the coatom extension over the empty face, `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) for the instances of uniformity and dominance (still to be proved), and the lawfulness of the candidate, compiled for every model (output 1). For a cover-hollow realization the candidate is never a model (`Realization.not_isModel_stableCandidate_of_isCoverHollow`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); more generally, no model at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) reduces to a cover-hollow realization at \(\lambda_\xi\) (`Realization.IsCoverHollow.isTerminalAt`). **Leastness is not modelhood.** That the stable candidate is lawful and least (output 1, output 2 refined, and the least structural successor above), or that it is the only structural successor (for a cover-hollow model), does not make it a model. Receiving, (R1)–(R4) of the table of Layer 3, stays its own statement. The receiving statement on one occurrence, (R4), and the instances of the coface families at the next block, from the coatom extension property with apex at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\), remain the content of output 3: output 3 from these two hypotheses (`StableCappedReceiving` and `StageType.HasNonemptyCofaceInstances`, both defined in this repository), is compiled conditionally as above (theorem named), with the two hypotheses still to be proved. Exact lifting over a separately prescribed higher root (the two-face lift, `Seed.TwoFaceLift`, defined in this repository, and the coatom extension property with apex, `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions`; Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)) still needs its own proof: leastness alone does not give it. **(R4) at one occurrence** (`Continuation/StableReceiving`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). (R4) at an occurrence \(x\) of the candidate, a coface \(D\) of its type and an ordinal \(\gamma\ge\lambda_\xi\) (`Realization.StablyReceivesAt`) is the conjunction, at one new point \(y\), of exact receiving in \(R\) of the reduction \(D{\downarrow}\lambda_\xi\), the formal top included, and the calibration of the stable labels of \(x^\frown y\) at the cells where that reduction is the formal top (`Realization.stablyReceivesAt_iff`); the two parts concern the same point and are not supplied separately, since two points of one type in \(R\) can have different stable labels. At the cells visible through the face of \(x\) the calibration is automatic (`Realization.stablyReceivesAt_iff_of_mem_cofaces`), and a donor with no new cell reducing to the top is received exactly from finite-cut receiving of \(R\), that is, from (R1) for \(R\) (`Realization.exists_stableCandidate_eval_eq_of_hasFiniteCutReceiving`); the content of (R4) is at the new cells reducing to the top, where no cutoff below \(\lambda_\xi\) separates the formal top from a large ordinal. (R4) at one occurrence fails at a cover-hollow realization for every donor with a label in the new block, and, when the stable labels are bounded by \(\lambda_\xi+K\), at \(\gamma=\lambda_\xi+K\) for every donor with a top cell (`Realization.not_stablyReceivesAt_of_isCoverHollow`, `Realization.not_stablyReceivesAt_of_stableLabel_le`); both hypotheses are excluded by those of `StableCappedReceiving` (non-hollowness, and top-grade supremum \(\top\), under which the stable labels are unbounded, `Realization.not_forall_stableLabel_le_of_topGradeSup_eq_top`), so neither is a refutation. **(R4) is the continuation criterion.** For a model, (R4) at every occurrence of positive arity is finite-cut receiving of the candidate, given the amalgam over the empty face (`Realization.forall_stablyReceivesAt_iff_hasFiniteCutReceiving`), and, under (R1) (`Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving`), forcing donors at \(\xi\) and the coface instances at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\), it is the existence of a model expansion (`Realization.forall_stablyReceivesAt_iff_exists_model`); globally, under the same three hypotheses, `StableCappedReceiving` is equivalent to `ContinuationCriterion` (`Expansion.stableCappedReceiving_iff_continuationCriterion`, `Expansion/StableReceiving`; the new direction, `Expansion.stableCappedReceiving_of_continuationCriterion`, uses (R1) and forcing donors only). So (R4) is an exact reformulation of output 3 modulo named hypotheses already in the list, not a weaker step. The models at which (R4) is proved here are those with a model expansion (`Realization.stablyReceivesAt_of_reduce_eq`, conditional on forcing donors and on finite-cut receiving of the expansion), a class derived from the expansion that cannot be used to construct it. **The reduction of (R4).** The evaluation step is compiled with no hypothesis beyond modelhood (`Realization.stablyReceivesAt_of_isStableRecoveryScheme`): generalized saturation realizes a stable recovery scheme (`StageType.IsStableRecoveryScheme`: a scheme on the points of an occurrence \(w\) containing \(x\) and one more, on which every stage type at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) with face the stable type of \(w\) has the conclusion of (R4) along the root followed by the new point) over \(w\), and stable lawfulness and exact consistency of the candidate evaluate it. With it, (R4) follows from the acquisition of calibrated contexts and stable recovery schemes for one calibration at each \(\xi\) (`StableCappedReceiving.of_stableRecoveryContexts`). That statement is **false** at every \(\xi\) (`Continuation.StableRecoveryCounterexample.not_hasStableRecoverySchemes_markerCap`, and `Continuation.StableRecoveryCounterexample.not_forall_hasStableRecoverySchemes_markerCap` for the hypothesis at every \(\xi<\omega_1\), compiled in this repository (theorem named)); this refutes that finite hypothesis, not (R4) and not output 3. Its cap is already the formal top at \(\lambda_\xi\) (a label at least \(\lambda_\xi\) reduces to \(\top\)); it asks for recovery from less calibration than the design of 3.3 and the coupled gate form of (R1) (`StageType.HasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`, whose form without the coupling is refuted): it lacks a private cap of full scope and full grade \(N\) with stable value above \(\lambda_\xi+\ell>\gamma\), a marker offset below \(N\) (in the design only; (R1) has no marker), reference cells (anchors, `StageType.IsAnchored`) for the blocks of the proper labels of the donor, and the arity bound (the root and the new point strictly fewer than the points of the occurrence); and the recovery clause covers every stage type on the scheme with the given face, so every proper label of the donor at a new cell must be determined by the rows of the scheme from that face alone. The instance is the smallest the definition allows: the root is one point with one cell labelled \(\lambda_\xi+2\) (both marker and cap), \(T^+\) is the root itself (no private point), and the donors are the two lifts \((\lambda_\xi+2,\bot,\lambda_\xi+2,\lambda_\xi+1,\bot)\) and \((\lambda_\xi+2,\bot,\lambda_\xi+1,\lambda_\xi+2,\bot)\) of the five-cell type of twin ordering, with the same face; over the whole occurrence a stable recovery scheme is the scheme of the donor and recovery is rigidity of the donor (`StageType.IsStableRecoveryScheme.eq_toScheme_of_refl`, `StageType.IsStableRecoveryScheme.label_eq_of_refl`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), which the twins violate. The calibration lacks a cap above the new cells (informal; not compiled): recovery at a new cell is read through a cell of the scheme whose label is held up by a cap of its scope and grade (availability) and whose row reads the new cell in the block of a reference cell, with the reference offset below the grade of the cap (the decoder at one reading cell, `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.label_eq_of_reading`, with `Label.TransformsTo.eq_coe_add_of_reading`, `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.le_label_of_reading` and `CellScheme.Rows.IsLawful.label_eq_bot_of_reading`, `Label/Transform` and `Scheme/Row`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). The refuting instance does not satisfy the graded cap calibration (`Continuation.StableRecoveryCounterexample.not_gradedCapCalibration_twinRoot`); no instance of the finite statement is compiled. The apex coatom extension property does not enter (R4); it enters output 3 only through the coface instances.

**Summit 4:** constructed countable terminal fibres and non-hollow growth continuation. No characteristic theory or global termination theorem is a premise.

### Layer 5 — Expansion domains and logical agreement

Construct the expansion domain on actual isomorphism classes. Prove downward closure and literal same-carrier normalization. Establish coherent countable-limit expansions; decreasing sets alone do not prove continuity.

Map successor losses into fixed-stage terminal classes and obtain countability. Apply the generic countable-loss lemma.
