Prove whole-finite-cover transfer in one block, and derive from it, by InfinitaryLogic's graded-matching theorem `bfEquiv_of_gradedMatching` (Layer 0), with the match (ii) of Layer 0 and the common empty chart as initial match (on abstract hypotheses, `ExpansionMatchData.bfEquiv_of_expansionMatch`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), the back-and-forth equivalence at \(\eta\) of any two members of \(D_\eta\) ("Condition 3 from back-and-forth", in the reduction to expansion domains); the sharp sentence comparison at block \(\eta\), not merely at block \(\omega\cdot\eta\), is its corollary by `BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR`. Use the existing infinitary back-and-forth semantics, including empty and repeated parameter tuples.

**Status.** Compiled in this repository (theorem named), each with the hypotheses named:

- the expansion domains, their downward closure, and transport to codes on \(\mathbb N\) (`Expansion.expansionDomain`, `Expansion.expansionDomain_antitone`, `Expansion.mem_expansionDomain_iff`, `Expansion/Domains`), and the first domain, every class, conditional on the cap-to-model theorem at \(\omega\) (`Expansion.expansionDomain_zero`);
- uniqueness of model expansions and limit existence, with the coherence of the lower expansions derived from uniqueness (`ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, `ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_forall_lt`, `Expansion.biInter_expansionDomain_subset`, `Expansion/Uniqueness`), conditional on next-block uniqueness (`Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`); next-block uniqueness is derived from (R1) and forcing donors (`Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.of_forcingDonors`, `Expansion/UniquenessOfForcing`), both still to be proved, so it is not a hypothesis of the main theorem;
- condition 3, in back-and-forth form, and logical agreement (`Expansion.bfEquiv_of_modelExpansions`, `Expansion.realize_iff_of_modelExpansions`, `Expansion/Agreement`), conditional on (R1);
- condition 2, the countable successor losses (`Expansion.expansionDomain_loss_countable`, `Expansion/Losses`; checkpoint E of condition 2), conditional on (R1), `ContinuationCriterion` (output 3, sufficiency only), (R2) (`Realization.ResidualReceiving`), and (R3) for cover-hollowness at a block stage (`Realization.HollowReceiving` with `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock`): the expansions to \(\lambda_\xi\) of a class in the loss at \(\xi\) are terminal, with no hypothesis (`ModelExpansion.isTerminalAt_of_mem_loss`); two models with expansions sharing a terminal property are isomorphic (`ModelExpansion.nonempty_equiv_of_hasTerminalProperty`), so each property holds of at most one class (`Expansion.subsingleton_classes_of_property`); and a countable cover by subsingletons gives countability (`Counting.countable_of_subsingleton_cover`, `Counting/Domains`, Layer 0). So condition 2 is complete, conditional on output 3, (R1), (R2), and (R3); no hypothesis of countable losses remains. The equivalence of cover-hollowness with the original no-anchor predicate is still to be proved and is not used; the count is presently formulated with cover-hollowness.

**Manuscript correspondence.** Item 4 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)", the structural common-invariant comparison, belongs to this layer, with the density and invariance statements and the unique-reconstruction interface of item 5, the interface using the expansion domains.

**Summit 5:** every actual infinitary sentence has a countable truth side or false side on the concrete class presentation.

### Layer 6 — The upper and lower bounds

For thinness, apply descriptive separation, in the form of the library's thinness theorem for countable sentence splits, to the countable truth sides of Layer 5. For the upper bound, apply Scott separation on the persistent core: distinct classes are separated by a sentence, so by the agreement of Layer 5 the persistent core \(\bigcap_{\eta<\omega_1}D_\eta\) is a subsingleton, and its complement is covered by the \(\aleph_1\) many countable exceptions \(Q\setminus D_\eta\). Morley's dichotomy (the witnessed perfect-set alternative) is an alternative route to the upper bound that is not used. State thinness as absence of a perfect isomorphism antichain, not cardinality below the continuum. Measurability belongs on model codes; no quotient-Borel hypothesis is permitted.

**Thinness in scatteredness form** (no sentence minimality). If for every \(\eta<\omega_1\) the codes on \(\mathbb N\) of models fall into only countably many classes of back-and-forth equivalence at \(\eta\) (InfinitaryLogic's `CodeBFEquiv η`, restricted to the codes of models of the sentence: `bfEquivSetoid`), there is no perfect isomorphism antichain. The hypothesis counts classes of that relation, not codes. Proof (InfinitaryLogic's `isThinOn_of_bfScattered`, `Descriptive/BFScattered`, available at the pin): a perfect antichain in the completely metrizable space of codes yields a Cantor antichain; its range is analytic, and `exists_uniform_bfSeparation` (Layer 0, available at the pin), applied to the off-diagonal of the range (Mathlib's `Set.offDiag`), separates its distinct points at one level \(\eta\); the class map at \(\eta\) then injects Cantor space into the countable quotient, which is impossible. Neither sentence separation nor López–Escobar is used. The scatteredness-form composition is in `VaughtConjecture.MainTheorem.Scatteredness`: `isThinOn_of_countable_bfClasses` (for a set of codes) and `isThinOnNatModels_of_countable_bfClasses` (for the models of a sentence), quotations of InfinitaryLogic's `isThinOn_of_bfScattered` and `Sentenceω.isThinOnNatModels_of_bfScattered` (`Descriptive/BFScatteredSentence`); the module keeps, with their statements, the lemmas of the same argument for a closed perfect antichain (`analyticSet_offDiag`, `offDiag_noniso`, `exists_forall_not_codeBFEquiv_of_isClosed`, and `not_countable_of_perfect`, the last through `Perfect.mk_eq_continuum`), which the thinness theorems no longer use; for the full-presentation route, `MainTheorem/Assembly` has the hypothesis `FullPresentations.HasScatteredTails` (the codes of the models in the tail at \(\eta\) meet countably many classes at \(\eta\)) and the conditional theorems `densitySentence_isThinOnNatModels_of_scatteredTails`, `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_scatteredTails` (with uncountability), and `vaughtCounterexample_of_scatteredTails` (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "The full-presentation route"). These apply `exists_uniform_bfSeparation` through InfinitaryLogic's `isThinOn_of_bfScattered`; that the construction supplies `FullPresentations.HasScatteredTails` is a statement still to be proved. On the expansion-domain route, thinness also has this form (expected, not elaborated): by the back-and-forth form of condition 3, at each \(\eta<\omega_1\) the classes in \(D_\eta\) lie in one class of empty-tuple back-and-forth equivalence at \(\eta\), and the complement \(Q\setminus D_\eta\) is countable; since isomorphic models are back-and-forth equivalent at every level, the codes of models of \(\Phi\) meet only countably many classes of `bfEquivSetoid Φ η`, and `isThinOnNatModels_of_countable_bfClasses` (compiled in this repository (theorem named)) applies, with neither sentence separation nor López–Escobar. The working thinness route, from countable truth sides (the minimality form), is kept; both thinness proofs are retained.

For the lower bound, take the top-free witness at every countable block (the section on the top-free witnesses, after Layer 3). Prove that its base class is in that block's loss: it lies in the domain of the block, and uniqueness of expansion together with same-carrier transport excludes another, higher expansion of its base reduct; then choose from pairwise disjoint losses. The proof of the lower bound must not use terminal countability, a cardinality conclusion, or eventual departure of every model.

Combine the two bounds, then apply the reduction to \(\mathbb N\): there are no finite models, so every countable model is infinite and hence isomorphic to a coded model on \(\mathbb N\). Prove the exact \(\aleph_1\) spectra and perfect-set failures for both natural-number and all-countable carriers. The perfect-set failure on all countable carriers is InfinitaryLogic's `¬ PerfectSetDichotomyAllCountable`, which adds to the clause on \(\mathbb N\) only the finite carriers, and these carry no model when there are no finite models. The all-countable-carrier formulation is compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on the hypotheses of the compositions on \(\mathbb N\) and on the cap-to-model theorem `CapToModel` (Layer 3, 3.4; still to be proved), from which the absence of finite models is derived: `HasThinAlephOneSpectrumOnCountableCarriers` with `hasThinAlephOneSpectrumOnCountableCarriers_iff`, `densitySentence_not_perfectSetDichotomyAllCountable`, and `vaughtCounterexample_allCarriers_of_expansionDomains` with its two counterparts for full presentations (`MainTheorem/AllCarriers`). The absence of finite models also follows from the coatom extension property at \(\omega\) alone (`infinite_of_realize_densitySentence_of_hasCoatomExtensions`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), with hypothesis `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions` at \(\omega\), still to be proved); once that property is proved, the reduction to \(\mathbb N\) for the density sentence no longer needs `CapToModel`.

**Status: the hypotheses of the main theorem.** The thin \(\aleph_1\) spectrum of the density sentence on \(\mathbb N\) is compiled in this repository (theorem named), `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification` (`MainTheorem/ModelExpansionDomains`; thinness alone, `densitySentence_isThinOnNatModels_of_terminalClassification`), conditional on the following named hypotheses. Each is a separate statement with its own status (`DASHBOARD.md`). None of them is derived from another in the library, except items 1 and 7, which are derived from the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage (with next-block uniqueness, from items 2 and 3, for item 7); item 3, which is derived from that property (`forcingDonors_of_forall_hasApexCoatomExtensions`; the six-hypothesis form below takes it, the five-hypothesis form derives it); and item 4, which is derived from that property together with (R4) (`ContinuationCriterion.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`); (R4) is not in the list. Items 1 and 7 are derived in the six-hypothesis form below, and items 1, 3 and 7 in the five-hypothesis form. The hypothesis count went from seven to six to five only by compiled derivations: seven in `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification`, six in `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, five in `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions'` (the first in `MainTheorem/ModelExpansionDomains`, the last two in `MainTheorem/Composition`); no hypothesis of the list is proved, and all three forms are kept.

1. The cap-to-model theorem at \(\omega\) (`CapToModel`; Layer 3, 3.4), for the first domain. Still to be proved; compiled in this repository (theorem named) conditional on the coatom extension property with apex at \(\omega\) (`CapToModel.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, `MainTheorem/CapToModel`), which is still to be proved.
2. (R1), finite-cut receiving of models (`Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving`), for next-block uniqueness, logical agreement, and the rigid-core comparison. Still to be proved; compiled conditional on the coupled gated pinned extension property (`Expansion.finiteCutReceiving_of_hasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`), which is open.
3. Forcing donors at every countable block (`ForcingDonors`, for every \(\xi<\omega_1\)), for next-block uniqueness, hence the limit clause. Still to be proved; compiled conditional on the coatom extension property at \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) (`forcingDonors_of_hasCoatomExtensions`, `Extension/ForcingDonorsCoatom`), hence on the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage (`forcingDonors_of_forall_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), which is still to be proved.
4. The continuation criterion (`ContinuationCriterion`, output 3 of Layer 4), for the cover of the terminal classes. Still to be proved.
5. (R2) (`Realization.ResidualReceiving`), for the residual comparison. Still to be proved. A reduction is compiled in this repository (theorem named): (R2) follows from (R1) for every model at every limit stage — a receiving hypothesis that ranges over every limit stage at fixed universe levels and is not supplied by the countable-stage `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` (item 2) — together with residual acquisition and cutoff determination (`Realization.residualReceiving_of_cutoffDetermination`), for a predicate \(P\) on acquired contexts not yet defined: the reduction is a template, and its hypotheses are not statements still to be proved (no predicate \(P\) is defined in the library, and neither acquisition nor determination is proved beyond the rigid-core instance); for \(P\) always true, acquisition is immediate and determination fails (compiled), which shows only that determination is not vacuous; the cofaces in which the root is a rigid core follow from (R1) in the same form alone (`Realization.ResidualReceiving.of_not_isRigidCoreIn`). See "Status of (R2) and (R3)" in Layer 3.
6. (R3) for cover-hollowness at a block stage (`Realization.HollowReceiving` with `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock`), for the hollow comparison. Still to be proved. A reduction is compiled in this repository (theorem named): (R3) follows from hollow acquisition and scheme determination with \(H\) `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock` (`Realization.hollowReceiving_of_schemeDetermination`), for a predicate \(P\) on acquired contexts not yet defined: the reduction is a template, and its hypotheses are not statements still to be proved (no predicate \(P\) is defined in the library, and neither acquisition nor determination is proved beyond the rigid-core instance); for \(P\) always true, acquisition is immediate and determination fails (compiled), which shows only that determination is not vacuous; under (R3) a globally rigid core of a cover-hollow model with unbounded growth is rigid in every legal donor over its type (`Realization.HollowReceiving.isRigidCoreIn`).
7. For the spectrum, nonempty losses (condition 4). Still to be proved; compiled conditional on the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage and on next-block uniqueness (`hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, `MainTheorem/LowerBound`, stated for the bundled domains, which also take `CapToModel`; per block, `nonempty_loss_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), which uses next-block uniqueness only at the successor blocks \(\xi+1\le\eta\) and none at \(\eta=0\); condition 4 is derived from these hypotheses, not proved, and this derivation is composed with items 2–6 in the six-hypothesis form below, and with items 2 and 4–6 (item 3 derived) in the five-hypothesis form.

**The six-hypothesis form.** `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions` (`MainTheorem/Composition`; on all countable carriers, `vaughtCounterexample_allCarriers_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`) is compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on items 2–6 above and on the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage (`StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at \(\lambda_\eta\) for every \(\eta<\omega_1\); Layer 3, 3.1, the open part of (R6)), still to be proved. Items 1 and 7 are derived in it, not assumed: the cap-to-model theorem from the coatom extension property with apex at \(\lambda_0=\omega\) (`CapToModel.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), and nonempty losses from that property at every countable block stage and next-block uniqueness (`hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), next-block uniqueness being derived from items 2 and 3 (`Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.of_forcingDonors`) and countable losses from items 2 and 4–6 (`Expansion.expansionDomain_loss_countable`). The seven-hypothesis theorem above is kept: the six-hypothesis form is obtained from it by the two derivations above, which, given items 2 and 3, give items 1 and 7 from the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage. No converse is known; the seven-hypothesis form is not derived from the six-hypothesis form. Item 4 stays a hypothesis: it is compiled conditional on (R4) and the coatom extension property with apex at the successor blocks (`ContinuationCriterion.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), and (R4) is still to be proved. Item 3 is still a hypothesis of this form (`hF`); it is derived in the five-hypothesis form below.

**The five-hypothesis form.** `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions'` (`MainTheorem/Composition`; on all countable carriers, `vaughtCounterexample_allCarriers_of_terminalClassification_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions'`) is compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on items 2, 4, 5 and 6 above and on the coatom extension property with apex at every countable block stage, still to be proved. It is the six-hypothesis form with item 3 derived: forcing donors at every \(\xi<\omega_1\) from the coatom extension property with apex at the next block stage \(\lambda_{\xi+1}\) (`forcingDonors_of_forall_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, `Extension/ForcingDonorsCoatom`). In it the cap-to-model theorem is derived from the coatom extension property with apex at \(\lambda_0=\omega\); next-block uniqueness from item 2 and forcing donors; countable losses from items 2 and 4–6; and nonempty losses from the coatom extension property with apex and next-block uniqueness. No compiled theorem derives next-block uniqueness or countable losses from the coatom extension property with apex alone; both derivations use (R1). The seven- and six-hypothesis forms are kept. The five-hypothesis restricted form, with item 6 restricted, is a separate statement, not part of this one (prospective).

**An alternative hypothesis set: (R3) restricted to models without a globally rigid core.** The thin \(\aleph_1\) spectrum is also compiled in this repository (theorem named), `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_restrictedTerminalClassification` (`MainTheorem/ModelExpansionDomains`; thinness alone, `densitySentence_isThinOnNatModels_of_restrictedTerminalClassification`), conditional on hypotheses 1–5 and 7 above and, in place of 6:

6′. (R3) for cover-hollowness without a globally rigid core at a block stage (`Realization.HollowReceiving` with `Realization.IsCoverHollowWithoutRigidCoreAtBlock`), for the hollow comparison of the restricted hollow property. Still to be proved.

The terminal properties are then the restricted ones (`Realization.HasRestrictedTerminalProperty`, `Continuation/RestrictedHollow`): the hollow property also excludes a globally rigid core, the cover survives with no further hypothesis (`Realization.exists_hasRestrictedTerminalProperty_of_hasTerminalProperty`, and under the continuation criterion `Realization.exists_hasRestrictedTerminalProperty`), and the models moved from the hollow property have a rigid-core property, compared by the rigid-core comparison on (R1) only. The count of the terminal classes at each countable level holds in the same form (`MainTheorem.countable_isoClasses_terminalAt_of_restrictedTerminalClassification`, `MainTheorem/TerminalClasses`). Hypothesis 6 implies 6′ (`Realization.HollowReceiving.withoutRigidCore`), so this hypothesis set is weaker or equal; strictly weaker is not shown. The six- and five-hypothesis forms with 6′ in place of 6 are not compiled (separate compositions, prospective). Cover-hollowness (`Realization.IsCoverHollow`), the hollow property of `Realization.HasTerminalProperty`, and the theorems with hypothesis 6 are unchanged. *A risk for hypothesis 6 (informal, not compiled beyond its first step):* under hypothesis 6, a globally rigid core of a cover-hollow model with unbounded growth receives every one-point coface of its type exactly and is therefore rigid in it (compiled for every legal donor over its type: `Realization.HollowReceiving.isRigidCoreIn`); so if every legal stage type had a legal one-point coface in which the root is not rigid, hypothesis 6 would force every cover-hollow model with unbounded growth to have no globally rigid core. Neither that property of the legal stage types nor its consequence is proved, and hypothesis 6 is not claimed to be false; hypothesis 6′ does not depend on this argument.

The compositions with next-block uniqueness or countable losses as hypotheses remain compiled (`densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_modelExpansions`, `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_forcingDonors`, `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`).

**Manuscript correspondence.** The two counting endpoints of item 5 of the section "Manuscript correspondence (required)" (expansion-domain counting, the default, and maximal-presentation counting, with its termination dependence explicit), the uniform fixing bounds from positive niceness with the strictness step, the equivalent criteria for maximal presentations with their literal uniqueness and the optimal all-presentation bound, the terminal refinement of a specified higher presentation, the two stopping proofs (the countable-slot argument and the Scott route to maximal presentations) with positive niceness from a terminal presentation, and the noncollapse statement belong to this layer.

**Summit 6:** the specified infinitary sentence has the four properties of the main theorem, with no hypotheses asserting that the finite constructions exist. There is no first-order counterpart of the main theorem in this roadmap.

## Manuscript correspondence (required)

This milestone spans Layers 2–6 and is required: the development is described as **matching the manuscript** only when it is complete. It concerns the correspondence with [Kni26] and [AFK26], two sources kept distinct: [Kni26] is the draft of the construction, and [AFK26] the draft of the full-presentation organization (`LITERATURE.md`, §9 and the bibliographic access record). **The manuscript** means these two drafts together, and every item below and every row of the concordance names the source of its notion. Both are cited only by the numbered statements the roadmap already cites (those of [Kni26] are listed in `LITERATURE.md`, "Bibliographic access record"); a notion with no numbered statement is named by its source and described in words.

Matching the manuscript means a **proved correspondence of definitions and hypotheses**: for each notion of the manuscript that the proof uses, either a comparison performed in this repository or a recorded correction. A comparison is performed by a theorem of this repository that compares the manuscript's notion, clause by clause, with a declaration here, or by a **definition-level identification**: a declaration with one clause for each clause of the manuscript's definition, the comparison of the clauses recorded with the declaration, every departure from a printed clause proved equivalent to it by a named theorem, and every notion its clauses use itself in proved correspondence or corrected. A correction replaces the manuscript's definition or statement by a corrected one, with the correction recorded; a theorem about the corrected notion is noted when one exists. Existing citation numbers alone do not establish a correspondence: a docstring that cites a numbered statement records which statement a declaration is meant to state, not that it states it. A compiled declaration with the same role as the manuscript's notion is not evidence of fidelity to the manuscript without the clause-by-clause comparison. Reaching the same counterexample theorem by another route is not a correspondence either. The declaration-level **concordance** (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Manuscript concordance") lists each notion, with its source, under one of three statuses: *proved correspondence* (the theorem or the definition-level identification that performs the comparison is named), *corrected manuscript definition or statement* (the correction recorded, with its place in the roadmap; a theorem about the corrected notion noted when one exists), and *still to be proved* (every other row, including a row whose only evidence is a compiled declaration with the same role, and a conditional theorem whose hypotheses are still to be proved). Every item below carries the markers of Layer 0: a statement is counted as proved here only when a theorem of this repository is named (*compiled in this repository (theorem named)*), and an argument with no theorem named in this repository is *prospective* here. The proof of the main theorem by the expansion-domain route does not use this milestone; items 4 and 5 say where their statements are also steps of the main theorem.

**The index translation** (source: [AFK26, Definitions 3.20 and 4.16]; a corrected indexing, concordance row 1, with theorems about it compiled in this repository (theorem named), `Correspondence/StageIndexing`). [AFK26] reads its projection of index \(\beta\) at the stage \(\omega\cdot\beta\). The observation index \(\xi\) here is read at the stage \(\lambda_\xi\) = `blockStage ξ`, defined in `Realization/Expansion` as `ω + ω * ξ`, that is \(\omega+\omega\cdot\xi\), with \(\omega\cdot\xi\) the ordinal product (the sum of \(\xi\) copies of \(\omega\), Mathlib's `ω * ξ`); equivalently `blockStage ξ = ω * (1 + ξ)` (`blockStage_eq_mul`), the printed stage of the index \(1+\xi\), with `blockStage 0 = ω` (`blockStage_zero`) and `blockStage (ξ + 1) = blockStage ξ + ω` (`blockStage_add_one`). So the two indexings agree from \(\omega\) on (`blockStage_eq_omega0_mul_of_omega0_le`), a finite index \(n\) here is the printed index \(n+1\) (`blockStage_natCast`), the printed indices of the block stages are the positive ordinals (`exists_blockStage_eq_omega0_mul_iff`), block stages are continuous at limits (`isNormal_blockStage`, `blockStage_eq_iSup_of_isSuccLimit`), and the block stages of countable index are the limit ordinals below \(\omega_1\) (`isSuccLimit_and_lt_omega_one_iff`); the printed truncation function of [AFK26, Definitions 3.12 and 4.9] is the stage reduction of a label (`Label.reduce_coe_eq_ite`, `Label/Basic`; a proved correspondence). The printed index \(0\), the reduction to the stage \(0\), is corrected and has no block index: with it the projections are not compatible with the base relations (`Correspondence.not_isCompatibleWith_omega0MulSystem`, item 2). Only the index \(0\) is forced: the printed system corrected at the index \(0\) alone is compatible and agrees with the printed one at every positive index (`Correspondence.isCompatibleWith_omega0MulMaxOneSystem`, `Correspondence.omega0MulMaxOneSystem_τ_of_ne_zero`); the shift by one at the other finite indices is the convention of `blockStage` (\(\lambda_0=\omega\)), not a consequence of compatibility. The indexing of [AFK26] is therefore corrected, not identified (concordance row 1: C; its truncation function: P). The observation at index \(\alpha\) of a closed tuple of a realization at a later stage is the stage reduction of its type to `blockStage α` (`StageType.reduce`, coherent by `StageType.reduce_reduce`).

1. **Template correspondence (Layer 2; source [AFK26]).** The stage types here keep the semantic rows fixed and vary the labels (Layer 2, "The templates of [AFK26] and the stage types here"), while the templates of [AFK26] carry variable rows [AFK26, Definitions 4.6 and 4.10]. The required representation (compiled in this repository (theorem named), `Correspondence/Template`): the finite coded scheme is fixed (`Scheme`, with cells `Fin card`, scopes, positive grades, and rows, coded by `Scheme.IsCoded` and legal by `Scheme.IsLegal`); a labelling \(p\) is represented by its coherent local rows \(r_d(e)=\min(p(e),p(d))\), for each cell \(d\) and the cells \(e\) of the row of \(d\); the labels are recovered from the diagonal, \(r_d(d)=p(d)\); and the following are theorems: both round trips between lawful labellings and coherent local row families (`Correspondence.lawfulRowsEquiv`, `Correspondence.diagonal_coherentRows`, `StageType.toTemplate_ofCoherentRows`, `StageType.ofCoherentRows_toTemplate`, `StageType.eq_of_toTemplate_eq`), compatibility with partial restriction (`StageType.coherentRows_comap`, with `StageType.restrictFace_eq_none_iff` for the undefined faces), and compatibility with projection (`StageType.toTemplate_reduce`: the template of the stage reduction is the entrywise reduction, the printed truncation of every row entry). *The fidelity question.* This represents the lawful stage types of this repository; it does not characterize the arbitrary frames and row families that the current definitions of [AFK26] allow. The roadmap requires one of two resolutions: (1) the manuscript adopts this restricted template class, or (2) a theorem identifies the intended legal templates of [AFK26] with that class. Either resolution preserves the finite coded cells, the positive grades, the static semantic information (scopes, grades, and coded rows), and lawfulness (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 3). Until one of them holds, the template notion is a corrected manuscript definition in the concordance, and the identification of the legal templates of [AFK26, Definition 4.27] (with the lawful local labellings and bountiful rows of [AFK26, Definition 4.26]) with the coherent local rows is still to be proved. The legal templates of the current draft of [AFK26] (its Definitions 4.24–4.27, with Lemma 4.28 and Theorem 4.29 as statements) are matched clause by clause in `VaughtConjecture/Correspondence/Current` (concordance rows 41–47), reading the row system of a template as the fixed semantic rows of a scheme, not as the coherent local rows above (the two readings make different objects legal; which one [AFK26] intends is part of the fidelity question): the visibility maps and the relation \(\Rightarrow\) are corrected (C), the lawful local labellings are identified with lawfulness below a graded face (P), and the bountiful rows, the legal templates (whose clause 1 is corrected, C), and the two statements are still to be proved (S). Agreement on lawful labellings is not correspondence of legal schemes. Clause 4 of [AFK26, Definition 4.27] (row entries \(\omega\cdot n+i\) with \(i\le g(d)\)) is an offset bound, which legality here deliberately omits (Layer 3, "legal scheme"), and the legal scheme `CodingExamples.pointRow 2` violates it (`Scheme.exists_isLegal_not_hasPrintedRange`); so there are legal stage types whose schemes are not legal templates in the printed sense (`StageType.exists_isLegal_not_hasPrintedRange`). The two classes are known to differ: with clause 1 corrected and clause 3 required at every stage together with the cap \(-\infty\), the legal templates of [AFK26] are exactly the legal schemes satisfying clause 4 (`Scheme.isLegal_and_hasPrintedRange_iff`), and that a legal template as printed (clause 3 at \(\omega_1\) only) is a legal scheme is not proved (row 44). Under this reading the first clause of [AFK26, Lemma 4.28] fails for the legal stage types here as they stand; a template carries no labelling, so \(\sigma[L]\) and `baseLanguage` are indexed by different kinds of object (templates, legal stage types), their underlying schemes differ by clause 4 (`pointRow 2`), and a comparison of the two languages needs the representation above (row 9) in any case. Under the coherent-rows reading the witness, whose labels are all \(\bot\), satisfies clause 4; clause 4 there bounds the labels (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, concordance note 43). Either of two results would resolve this, and neither is chosen here: a proof that the class restricted by clause 4 suffices for the main theorem, or a correction of clause 4 recorded as C. Until then, under this reading, resolution (2) above can hold only up to this bound. The lifts of a labelled template to the next block stage, their attained least lift, and the finite nonuniqueness of lifts (Layer 3, 3.1, "The attained least lift" and "Least, not unique"; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, §4, the finite lifting statements, roadmap targets related to this item and not rows of the concordance) are stated on the unchanged scheme and rows, as this representation requires: a lift changes labels only.
2. **The complete diagram round trip (Layer 2; source [AFK26, Definitions 2.4, 2.5, 2.6, and 2.8, and Lemma 4.19]).** The statement of [AFK26] that the invariant diagram and the invariant system are compatible becomes a milestone: at a fixed countable stage \(\lambda\), the structures of the stage chart language at \(\lambda\) (one relation symbol for each legal stage type at \(\lambda\), countably many) that satisfy the structural clauses, nonemptiness among them, correspond, by literal mutually inverse maps, to the fixed, exactly consistent, covering partial assignments of stage types at \(\lambda\) **with legal types on a nonempty carrier**, the realizations at \(\lambda\) with legal types on a nonempty carrier. (A realization with an illegal type has no counterpart, since the language has symbols only for legal types; the empty carrier carries an exactly consistent covering realization but no structure satisfying nonemptiness.) Acceptance: every relation and its negation; invisible faces, where the evaluation is `none`; repeated coordinates (which satisfy no relation) and empty tuples; and literal recovery in both directions. This is **structural reconstruction**: it is distinct from finite-age reconstruction (the section "The intended construction") and from modelhood (no extension clause enters). It is stated in countable fixed-stage languages, one stage at a time; the alphabet of all stage types at all countable stages, which has \(\aleph_1\) members (Layer 4), is not assumed countable. Compiled in this repository (theorem named): at the base stage \(\omega\), the round trips between realizations and structures of the base language (`baseLanguage.toStructure_toRealization`, `baseLanguage.toRealization_toStructure` for realizations with legal types, `baseLanguage.isTypeAssignment_iff_exists_toStructure_eq`, `Language/Structure`) and the satisfaction of the structural sentence (`baseLanguage.realize_structuralSentence_iff_toRealization`, `Language/Satisfaction`, whose right side includes `Nonempty M`); at every stage, the reading of a realization in the stage chart language (`Realization.toChartStructure`, `Language/HullOperations`) and, for a structure in the hull language covered by top-free charts, the reconstruction of partial evaluation with literal recovery and both round trips (`reconstruct_eval_eq_some_iff`, `reconstruct_toHullStructure`, `toHullStructure_reconstruct`, `ClassicalLimit/Reconstruction`); for model expansions (`Realization.IsExpansionOf`), the base relations read from covers (`Realization.IsExpansionOf.relMap_comp_iff`, `Realization/Expansion`) and, conditional on block determination (still to be proved), the chart relations at \(\lambda_\eta\) defined by base-language formulas (`ModelExpansion.relMap_toChartStructure_iff`, `Definability/BlockFormulas`, row 1 of quantitative reconstruction). These theorems compare two declarations of this repository. That the base language and the realizations form an invariance diagram and an invariant system in the sense of [AFK26, Definitions 2.4 and 2.6], compatible in the sense of [AFK26, Definition 2.8]: compiled in this repository (theorem named), in the language of [AFK26] with the invariants of all countable stages at once (`Correspondence/InvariantSystem`), with the diagram of the stage types supplied here in place of the printed diagram of templates [AFK26, Definition 4.18]; [AFK26, Definitions 4.17 and 4.18, and Lemma 4.19] are corrected (the concordance, row 14). The printed definitions are stated clause by clause, the invariance diagram of the stage types (`Correspondence.stageDiagram`) and the projections to the block stages (`Correspondence.blockSystem`) satisfy every clause and are compatible (`Correspondence.isCompatibleWith_blockSystem`), the structures compatible with the diagram are exactly the structures of the exactly consistent covering realizations with legal types at stage \(\omega_1\), by literal mutually inverse maps (`Correspondence.isCompatible_iff_exists_toInvariantStructure_eq`, `Correspondence.toRealization_toInvariantStructure`, `Correspondence.invariantLanguage.toInvariantStructure_toRealization`), whose base relations are those of the base language for the reduction to \(\omega\) (`Realization.relMap_base_iff`), and the closed tuples are the supported tuples (`Realization.relMap_closed_iff_isSupport`); since the relations of [AFK26] have positive arity, no nonemptiness clause enters there (the concordance, rows 14 and 29). Prospective: the round trip in the relational stage chart language at a general countable stage, for exactly consistent covering realizations with legal types on a nonempty carrier, with the whole acceptance list.
3. **The two-index density theorem (Layer 3; source [AFK26], no numbered statement), with its boundaries.** Here \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) are observation indices, read at the stages \(\lambda_\alpha\) and \(\lambda_\beta\); they are not the stages \(\alpha=\lambda_\eta\) and \(\beta=\lambda_\xi\) of Layer 3, 3.3. Write \(\mathrm{Dense}_\alpha(t)\) for density at observation \(\alpha\) over an actual root \(t\) of type \(p\): every one-point donor over \(t\) is realized by an actual occurrence that extends \(t\) literally and has the donor's observation at \(\alpha\); and \(\mathrm{Dense}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\) for the same with the donors restricted to those fixed at \(\beta\). The statement (prospective here): if \(\pi_\beta p=p\) (here: the stage reduction of \(p\) to \(\lambda_\beta\) changes no label, that is, the fixing rank of \(p\) is at most \(\beta\); Layer 3, 3.3) and \(\alpha\le\beta\), then \(\mathrm{Dense}_\alpha(t)\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{Dense}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\). Both sides retain the actual root exactly and request agreement at observation \(\alpha\) only. The proof projects an already legal donor downward: the projection to \(\lambda_\beta\) of a legal donor over \(t\) is a donor fixed at \(\beta\) over \(\pi_\beta p=p\) with the same observation at \(\alpha\) (`StageType.reduce_reduce`). It does not lift an arbitrary lower-stage donor over a prescribed higher-stage root; that is projected-donor lifting (Layer 3, 3.3), a different statement. Recorded separately:
   - the equivalence permits \(\alpha=\beta\); the construction is to provide the density only for \(\alpha<\beta\) (prospective);
   - different observations may use different realizing occurrences, as in the finite-cut receiving property `HasFiniteCutReceiving` (`Realization/Model`), whose occurrence is chosen after the cutoff, so that different cutoffs may use different occurrences (compiled in this repository (theorem named): the definition, which alone shows this unconditionally; and `Realization.IsModel.hasFiniteCutReceiving_of_hasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`, `Realization/CoupledFiniteCutReceiving`, conditional on `StageType.HasCoupledGatedPinnedExtensions`, a named hypothesis that is open, which acquires its private context for each cutoff separately);
   - this does not yet identify density at block observations with the scalar-cutoff predicate `HasFiniteCutReceiving`. One direction of the comparison of observations is compiled in this repository (theorem named): a cutoff observation at \(\lambda_\alpha\) determines the stage reduction to \(\lambda_\alpha\) (`StageType.reduce_eq_of_mem_receivingFamily`, `Realization/Expansion`). The scalar cutoffs inside the last block \([\lambda_\zeta,\lambda_\zeta+\omega)\) of a stage \(\lambda_{\zeta+1}\) are not block stages, and the identification is a statement still to be proved;
   - the special case (negative) of an actual model with a root over which some projected donor has no lift guards against the shortcut from density to projected Fraïssé homogeneity (Layer 3, 3.3, the density boundary; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Checkpoint order and acceptance", non-implication 4): prospective, not compiled here. It would be the first example of non-implication 4 with legal stage types; the only example recorded there, the four-point system, is informal and not of stage types.

   **Witness-bounded density: equivalent formulations** (a target; prospective here; concordance row 37). The predicates above bound the **donor**: \(\mathrm{Dense}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\) restricts the donors to those fixed at \(\beta\), and `HasFiniteCutReceiving` quantifies over the cofaces of the actual type and asks of the received occurrence only the donor's observation at the cutoff. The density of [AFK26] bounds the **returned invariant**, the type of the realizing occurrence. Write \(\mathrm{Dense}^{\mathrm w}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\) for: every one-point donor over \(t\) is realized by an actual occurrence that extends \(t\) literally, has the donor's observation at \(\alpha\), and has a type fixed at \(\beta\) (its stage reduction to \(\lambda_\beta\) changes no label). The target is to state \(\mathrm{Dense}^{\mathrm w}_{\alpha,\beta}\) as the predicate compared with [AFK26], and to prove, for \(\alpha\le\beta\) and a realization fixed by projection at \(\beta\) (every actual type fixed at \(\beta\); item 5, "Uniform fixing bounds from positive niceness"), that \(\mathrm{Dense}^{\mathrm w}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{Dense}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\) at every actual root \(t\): the donor-bounded and the returned-invariant-bounded predicates are equivalent formulations of density on such a realization. Neither predicate includes exact face preservation (that the type of the realizing occurrence restricts, on the face of the root, exactly to the type of the root): that is supplied separately by the exact consistency of the realization (`Realization.IsConsistent`, `Realization/Basic`, clause 2 of `Realization.IsModel`), and is not folded into the density predicate. On such a realization the bound on the returned invariant holds of every actual occurrence, so the left side is equivalent to \(\mathrm{Dense}_\alpha(t)\), and the two-index theorem above applies, the type of the actual root being fixed at \(\beta\). The fixation of the returned invariant comes from projection commuting with evaluation: the evaluation of a stage reduction is the reduction of the evaluation (`Realization.reduce_eval`, `Realization/Transport`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and stage reduction keeps typed and untyped tuples (`Realization.isSome_reduce_eval`, same module, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); so a realization equal, as an assignment, to its own projection to \(\beta\) has every actual type fixed at \(\beta\). Both sides keep the exact root and the realizing occurrence together: one occurrence extends the literal root, carries the donor's observation, and satisfies the bound. The equivalence supplies no lifting of a donor over an arbitrary projected root (projected-donor lifting, Layer 3, 3.3; the special case (negative) above; `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 12), and it does not identify density at block observations with `HasFiniteCutReceiving` (the third remark above).
4. **Structural common-invariant comparison (Layer 5; source [AFK26], the comparison of models with a common invariant, cf. [AFK26, Definition 2.16 and Theorem 2.17]).** The comparison theorem is to take, for two realizations with legal types at a common block stage \(\lambda_\eta\): exact consistency, covering, finite-cut receiving given as a hypothesis on these two realizations, and an explicit initial common chart (covers of one stage type in both, of any arity, the empty chart included). It is to derive back-and-forth equivalence at \(\eta\), in the base language, of the tuples selected from the two covers, and agreement on formulas of quantifier rank at most \(\eta\), through InfinitaryLogic's `bfEquiv_of_gradedMatching` and `BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR` (available at the pin (signatures checked)); lowering is stage reduction, along which finite-cut receiving descends (`Realization.HasFiniteCutReceiving.reduce`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). Empty and repeated parameter tuples are covered by the selection of the match (ii) of Layer 0. Modelhood provides the receiving hypothesis in a separate application ((R1) of the table in 3.4). Compiled in this repository (theorem named), with the hypotheses each takes today:
   - `ExpansionMatchData.bfEquiv_of_expansionMatch` (`Comparison/GradedMatchingApplications`): abstract expansion-match data (atomic agreement, lowering, forth, back), the initial match from the separate hypotheses `exists_empty_chart` and `compat`; conclusion on the empty tuples;
   - `Expansion.bfEquiv_of_modelExpansions`, `Expansion.realize_iff_of_modelExpansions`, and `Expansion.mem_modelsOf_iff_of_modelExpansions` (`Expansion/Agreement`): two base structures with model expansions to \(\lambda_\eta\) (`Realization.IsExpansionOf`: modelhood and the base-reduct equation), conditional on `Expansion.FiniteExtensionReceiving`, a hypothesis about every model at a countable limit stage, equivalent to `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1), still to be proved); initial match the empty chart (`ModelExpansion.exists_covers_zero`, `StageType.eq_of_zero`); conclusions on the empty tuples and on sentences. `ExpansionDomains.hasLogicalAgreement_of_modelExpansions` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`) applies them to the expansion domains, a step of the main theorem;
   - `ModelExpansion.bfEquiv_comp_of_covers` and `ModelExpansion.realize_comp_iff_of_covers` (`Expansion/Agreement`): model expansions to \(\lambda_\eta\) and the same hypothesis, with initial match an arbitrary common chart (covers of one stage type at \(\lambda_\eta\) in the two expansions) and a selector; conclusions on the selected tuples, empty and repeated included, and on formulas with free variables;
   - `Realization.exists_equiv_comp_eq_of_exactReceivingWithin` and `Realization.nonempty_equiv_of_exactReceivingWithin` (`Continuation/ExactAge`): model expansions of countable base structures, inclusion of their actual types in a common age, exact receiving within that age, and covers of one common stage type as initial match; the conclusion is an isomorphism of the base structures carrying cover to cover, an exact comparison, not a bounded one.

   Relative to these compiled theorems the milestone changes one thing: modelhood and the hypothesis about all models (`Expansion.FiniteExtensionReceiving`) are replaced by legal types, exact consistency, covering, and finite-cut receiving of the two given realizations. The explicit common chart of any arity as initial match, and the conclusions on selected parameter tuples, empty and repeated included, and on formulas with free variables, are already compiled (`ModelExpansion.bfEquiv_comp_of_covers`, `ModelExpansion.realize_comp_iff_of_covers`), under modelhood and that hypothesis. The structural form: prospective. Its finite counterpart on one realization, the comparison of the structural candidate with every coherent next-block assignment (structural successor leastness, Layer 4, "Status", output 2 refined; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, §4, the finite lifting statements, a roadmap target related to this item and not a row of the concordance), is a separate target: a comparison of labels at one stage, which supplies neither a receiving hypothesis nor back-and-forth equivalence.
5. **A concrete instance of the system of [AFK26] (Layers 4–6).** The development does not stop at an abstract system whose fields assume the difficult conclusions (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, last paragraph). The construction must prove each of the following about its concrete objects:

   | Statement | In this repository | Status |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | stage classes and their closure under projection | the models at \(\lambda_\xi\) and the model expansions of a base structure (`Realization.IsModel`, `ModelExpansion`), closed under stage reduction (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`, `ModelExpansion.reduce`, `ModelExpansion.reduceBlock`) | compiled in this repository (theorem named); that these are the stage classes of [AFK26] is still to be proved |
   | exact realization of each prescribed lawful finite seed | a model in which a prescribed lawful finite chart occurs as an actual chart (`COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, "An alternative route to the lower bound", step (ii)); for top-free seeds, the classical limit, whose age is the age of top-free charts (`exists_isFraisseLimit_topFreeAge`, conditional on `StageType.HasCoatomExtensions`), with exact extension of top-free donors (`exists_reconstruct_eval_eq_of_isTopFree`) and modelhood of its reconstruction (`isModel_reconstruct_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, conditional on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions`); for all legal seeds, top labels included, the classical limit of the age of all legal charts, a model realizing every legal stage type at a block stage with exact receiving of legal donors (`exists_saturated_reconstruct`, conditional on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions`), and on a prescribed tuple of any countably infinite carrier (`exists_sameLevelMaximal_covers`, also conditional on `ForcingDonors`, for terminality) | compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on the coatom extension property and its apex form, still to be proved; with cover-hollowness and terminality on a prescribed tuple of a prescribed carrier (`exists_sameLevelMaximal_covers`), also on `ForcingDonors`, still to be proved |
   | density at lower observations | item 3 | prospective |
   | satisfaction correspondence with the stage sentence | at \(\omega\): `baseLanguage.realize_toStructure_densitySentence_iff` (`Language/Density`) and `baseLanguage.realize_fourFamilySentence_iff` (`Language/Satisfaction`); at \(\lambda_\xi\), \(\xi>0\), the sentence of the stage chart language | at \(\omega\): compiled in this repository (theorem named); above \(\omega\): prospective |
   | countable levels of maximal presentations of base classes | through the class–level incidence (below), the field `countable_presentedAt` of `FullPresentations` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`), a hypothesis there; for the construction, the countable family of terminal conditions (Layer 4) with the top-free age; read with terminal model expansions of codes in place of maximal presentations, `MainTheorem.countable_isoClasses_terminalAt` (`MainTheorem/TerminalClasses`): countably many classes terminal at each countable level, among which lie the classes of codes with a maximal presentation (the loss) | maximal presentations: prospective; terminal model expansions: compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on (R1), the continuation criterion, (R2), and (R3), each still to be proved |
   | coverage by actual maximal presentations | through the class–level incidence, the field `exists_mem_presentedAt` of `FullPresentations`, a hypothesis; for the construction it currently rests on global termination ("The persistent core"), whose stopping half the countable-slot argument is to supply, or, for each base that is a model, on the second stopping proof, the Scott route to maximal presentations (below), which depends on conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction instead; for one base that is a model, in the raw base encoding, `MainTheorem.exists_maximalRefinement` (`MainTheorem/MaximalRefinement`) | for one base in the raw base encoding: compiled conditional on `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1)), `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, each still to be proved; for classes and in the common invariant encoding: prospective |
   | invariance over all admissible presentations, with an inhabited threshold | for each class, a threshold level from which the invariant read off an admissible presentation of the class does not depend on the presentation, with the set of thresholds inhabited; a form of the bound for each class covering all of its presentations ("Bounded levels", in the section "Reduction to full presentations"); for one literal base, in the raw base encoding, positive niceness of the base reduct of every model at a block stage on a countable carrier, one threshold serving every closed tuple (`exists_isNiceTupleAt_of_hasTerminalRefinement`, `isNice_of_hasTerminalRefinement`, `Realization.IsModel.isNice_toStructure_reduce`, `MainTheorem/Niceness`) | for one literal base: compiled conditional on `HasTerminalRefinement.{w}` and `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.{w}` (at `w = 0`, the refinement follows from (R1), next-block uniqueness, and the countable-block apex property; these premises remain open); for classes: prospective |
   | noncollapse of the base reducts | `UncountablyManyClasses` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`), a hypothesis; on the expansion-domain route, nonempty losses, `hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions` (`MainTheorem/LowerBound`), conditional on `CapToModel` (its hypothesis `hcap`, derivable from the apex form at \(\omega\) by `CapToModel.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`), on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, and on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, which is derived from (R1) and `ForcingDonors` (`Continuation/Normalization`, still to be proved) by `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.of_forcingDonors` (`Expansion/UniquenessOfForcing`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)) and remains conditional on those | conditional on statements still to be proved |

   The instance keeps **optional evaluations** apart from **positive labels on supported closed tuples**: the evaluation of a realization is partial (`Realization.eval` takes values in `Option`; its value `none` at an invisible or unsupported tuple is information, `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 4), while the observations of a presentation are defined exactly on closed tuples (the section "Reduction to full presentations", "Level observations"); neither is encoded by the other.

   **Least, not unique, in the instance.** No statement of the instance infers that a finite type has a unique lift to a higher index: the lifts of a type with a cell labelled the formal top are not unique, literal weakening being the greatest (Layer 3, 3.1, "Least, not unique"). The determination of the higher labels goes through normalization (Layer 4, output 2), not through uniqueness of finite lifts, and the modelhood of the stable candidate stays with output 3. These are roadmap targets related to this item (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, §4, the finite lifting statements), not rows of the concordance.

   **Unique reconstruction: a compact interface** (a target; prospective here; concordance row 39). The domain sentences are to recognize a unique expansion, with no hypothesis of countability of the carrier added: membership in an expansion domain and the uniqueness of model expansions are combined in one `∃!` statement, in two forms. *Carrier-general form:* for a base structure \(M\) on an arbitrary carrier, countable or not, and a countable index \(\xi\) (\(\xi<\omega_1\), an explicit hypothesis), the sentence of the domain guard at \(\xi\) (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Quantitative reconstruction", second row, prospective) holds in \(M\) exactly when \(M\) has a model expansion at `blockStage ξ`, and then it has exactly one. *Coded form:* for a code of a model of \(\Phi\) on \(\mathbb N\) whose class lies in `expansionDomain ξ`, its structure has exactly one model expansion at `blockStage ξ`; here \(\xi<\omega_1\) follows from `Expansion.expansionDomain_eq_empty` (`Expansion/Domains`; the domains are empty from \(\omega_1\) on). Neither combined statement is named. *The hypotheses, exactly.* None of the compiled statements used assumes the carrier countable: `ModelExpansion.subsingleton` (`Expansion/Uniqueness`) takes \(M\) arbitrary in the universe of its hypothesis, with \(\xi<\omega_1\), and is conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, still to be proved, which is itself stated for every carrier in that universe, countable or not; `ModelExpansion.eq_of_determines` (`Definability/BlockFormulas`), a second uniqueness, takes \(M\) arbitrary with \(\eta<\omega_1\) and is conditional instead on block determination at the blocks below \(\eta\) (`CoverThresholds.Determines`, still to be proved); `ModelExpansion.map` (`Realization/Expansion`) transports a model expansion along an isomorphism of base structures on arbitrary carriers; and existence on codes is `Expansion.mem_expansionDomain_iff` (`Expansion/Domains`), through `ModelExpansion.map`. The only countability used is that of the index. On a countable carrier it would also follow from the countability of the stage of a model (`Realization.IsModel.lt_omega_one`, which does assume the carrier countable); the interface does not use it. Existence from the domain sentence is the domain guard, prospective; a hypothesis that its proof needs is to be stated with it, not added to the uniqueness half. So the interface is conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness` (or on block determination), stated explicitly. Recorded with it, and not merged into it: the block formulas `blockFormula` (`Definability/BlockFormulas`), whose quantifier rank is at most \(\omega\cdot\eta\) with no hypothesis (`qrank_blockFormula_le`) and which hold exactly of the covers of their type in every model expansion to \(\lambda_\eta\) (`realize_blockFormula_iff`, conditional on block determination, `CoverThresholds.Determines`, still to be proved), with the chart relations defined by them (`ModelExpansion.relMap_toChartStructure_iff`, under the same hypothesis); the rank budgets (\(\omega\cdot\eta\) for the chart formulas, compiled; \(\omega\cdot(\eta+1)\) for the sentence of the domain guard, `COMPANIONS.md`, "Quantitative reconstruction", second row, prospective); the literal base-reduct equation of `Realization.IsExpansionOf` (its field `toStructure_reduce`, an equation of structures), with its realization form at \(\omega\), `ModelExpansion.val_eq_toRealization` (`Realization/Expansion`); and transport along isomorphisms, `ModelExpansion.map` (a definition), with, under block determination, the statement that an isomorphism of base structures carries every model expansion of one to every model expansion of the other by the same bijection (`ModelExpansion.map_eq_of_determines`). Each of these is compiled in this repository (theorem named; `ModelExpansion.map` and `blockFormula` are definitions), with the hypotheses stated. The interface is a theorem with explicit hypotheses: no new structure of hypotheses is introduced whose fields assume existence, uniqueness, or definability (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, last paragraph). It concerns higher-stage reconstruction (Layer 4, outputs 4 and 5, with the expansion domains of Layer 5), not the structural round trip of item 2, which it does not supply.

   **Maximal presentations and `FullPresentations`: the class–level incidence** (prospective; still to be proved). `FullPresentations` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`) is a structure of hypotheses on a type of classes: it stores, for each level \(\alpha\), a set `presentedAt α` of classes, with the hypotheses that this set is countable at each level below \(\omega_1\) (`countable_presentedAt`), that every class lies in it at some level below \(\omega_1\) (`exists_mem_presentedAt`), and that it is empty at the levels from \(\omega_1\) on. It stores no presentation object, no observation, and not maximality. The proposed correspondence with the maximal presentations of [AFK26] therefore concerns only their **incidence** with classes and levels. The translation: the classes are the base classes, the isomorphism classes of countable models of \(\Phi\) (`DensityClass`), and a maximal presentation at level \(\alpha<\omega_1\) of a countable model \(M\) of \(\Phi\) is sent to the incidence of the class of \(M\) at \(\alpha\); so the translated `presentedAt α` is the set of base classes some model of which has a maximal presentation at level \(\alpha\), and is empty for \(\alpha\ge\omega_1\). It is the maximal-presentation counterpart of \(Q_\alpha\), which is defined by full presentations (the section "Reduction to full presentations", "The least-level filtration") and is the reading intended in the docstring of `FullPresentations`; it is equal to \(Q_\alpha\) only through the separate identification below. Before any identification is claimed, the translation must be shown to preserve: (i) the incidence of classes at levels, in both directions and for every representative of the class (a class lies in `presentedAt α` exactly when some, equivalently every, model in it has a maximal presentation at level \(\alpha\), so that isomorphic models are presented at the same levels); (ii) the countability of each level below \(\omega_1\) (countably many base classes with a maximal presentation at that level); and (iii) coverage (every base class has a maximal presentation at some level below \(\omega_1\)). Maximality is not carried by the translation, so it does not identify the maximal presentations of [AFK26] with the full presentations of this roadmap; that identification is a separate statement, still to be proved. Until (i)–(iii) are proved for the construction, the concordance row is still to be proved, and `FullPresentations` is an instance of the system of [AFK26] only prospectively.

   **The two counting endpoints.** Both are endpoints in the sense of the section "Endpoints and shared foundations", and both are kept: maximal-presentation counting is the second endpoint (the main theorem by the full-presentation route), not a third.

   | Endpoint | Compiled composition (theorem named) | Termination | Hypotheses still to be proved |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | **Expansion-domain counting** (the first endpoint; the default) | `vaughtCounterexample_of_expansionDomains` and `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_expansionDomains` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`); `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_modelExpansions`, `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_forcingDonors`, and `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification` (`MainTheorem/ModelExpansionDomains`); `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions` (`MainTheorem/LowerBound`); `vaughtCounterexample_allCarriers_of_expansionDomains` (`MainTheorem/AllCarriers`) | no global termination premise: the persistent core has at most one class (`ExpansionDomains.core_subsingleton`), and no statement assumes departure | for the concrete expansion domains (`modelExpansionDomains`; the first two compositions take abstract `ExpansionDomains` with `HasLogicalAgreement` and losses as hypotheses): for `densitySentence_hasThinAlephOneSpectrum_of_terminalClassification`, `CapToModel`, (R1) (`Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving`), forcing donors (`ForcingDonors`), `ContinuationCriterion`, (R2) (`Realization.ResidualReceiving`), (R3) (`Realization.HollowReceiving` for `Realization.IsCoverHollowAtBlock`), and nonempty losses (compiled conditional on the coatom extension property with apex and `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, `hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`); countable losses are not a hypothesis (`Expansion.expansionDomain_loss_countable`), and next-block uniqueness is derived from (R1) and forcing donors (`Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.of_forcingDonors`); the earlier compositions take next-block uniqueness or countable losses as hypotheses (Layer 6, "Status") |
   | **Maximal-presentation (system) counting** (the second endpoint; the alternative stated in the terms of [AFK26]) | `vaughtCounterexample_of_presentations` and `vaughtCounterexample_of_scatteredTails` (`MainTheorem/Assembly`); `vaughtCounterexample_allCarriers_of_presentations` (`MainTheorem/AllCarriers`) | explicit: `FullPresentations` asserts a presentation of every class, the persistent class included, and its known proof goes through global termination ("The persistent core"); every use is a stated hypothesis | `FullPresentations` for the construction (prospective, through the class–level incidence above), `HasBoundedComparison` or `HasScatteredTails`, `UncountablyManyClasses`, and `CapToModel` |

   The second endpoint never silently justifies the first: a statement proved on the second route under global termination is quoted on the first only with that hypothesis restated. In particular, an eventual-invariance argument over all lifts (from some stage on, every presentation of a class gives it the same invariant) uses that the class has no lift above a terminal stage, which is the stopping half of global termination, and not merely a bound on the ranks of its invariants: a bound on fixing ranks or on Scott ranks does not provide it (informal; still to be proved, no example is compiled). The two readings of the lower bound in `COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, "An alternative route to the lower bound" (charts of every fixing rank; fixing ranks are zero or successors, and limit heights are unattained suprema, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"; each still to be proved, `COMPANIONS.md`) and "A second alternative route to the lower bound: Scott-bounded counting", are readings of the noncollapse statement of the second endpoint; each marks its uses of termination, neither changes the default, and no fixing rank is identified with a Scott rank.

   **Uniform fixing bounds from positive niceness** (source: [AFK26], no numbered statement; prospective here: no statement of this sub-item is a theorem of this repository unless one is named). This factors an argument of [AFK26] that fixes, at one countable level, the invariants read off the presentations of a class. It is stated in the alphabet of all labels (`Label`, `Label/Basic`), in which a stage type is a labelling and **projection** to an index \(\gamma\) is stage reduction to \(\lambda_\gamma\), label by label (`Label.reduce`; for stage types, `StageType.reduce`); it is distinct from the projections of level observations (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Manuscript concordance", row 23). Projections compose by the minimum: projection to \(\gamma\) after projection to \(\delta\) is projection to \(\min(\gamma,\delta)\), by `Label.reduce_reduce_of_le` when \(\gamma\le\delta\), and otherwise by `Label.atStage_reduce`, `Label.AtStage.mono` and `Label.reduce_eq_self_iff` (`Label/Basic`), with `blockStage_mono` (`Realization/Expansion`), each compiled in this repository (theorem named); the composite law is not named. Throughout this sub-item a tuple is an **injective tuple** (a tuple of distinct points, `Fin n ↪ M`, the domain of `Realization.eval`). An **assignment** sends each injective tuple of a carrier, of each arity, to `none` or to such a labelling (the evaluation of a realization, with no consistency, covering, or modelhood assumed); a tuple is **supported** in it when its value is not `none`. An assignment is **fixed by projection at** \(\gamma\) when projection to \(\gamma\) changes none of its labels, that is, every label lies at stage \(\lambda_\gamma\) (`Label.AtStage`: below \(\lambda_\gamma\), or the top; `Label.reduce_eq_self_iff`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); the fixing rank of one labelling is the least such \(\gamma\) (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"). Fix a countable carrier, a **base invariant assignment** \(R\), and, for every countable index \(\beta\), an arbitrary set \(F(\beta)\) of assignments; \(\beta\) is a **serving index** of the family, and \(\lambda_\beta\) a **serving stage**, when \(F(\beta)\) is inhabited (unrelated to the serving cells of the coatom extension construction, `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Checkpoints 2.1–2.7: the completion of the coatom extension construction").
   - *The result* (prospective). Assume, at every countable \(\beta\):
     1. every \(S\in F(\beta)\) is fixed by projection at \(\beta\) (**stage correctness**);
     2. every \(S\in F(\beta)\) projects literally to \(R\) at \(0\);
     3. (**positive niceness**) every supported tuple \(t\) of \(R\) has an actual family member at a countable threshold \(\theta_t\) realizing a value \(p_t\) at \(t\), and all family members at every higher countable index realize the same \(p_t\) at \(t\).

     Then one countable index \(A\) fixes every family member at every countable index (a **uniform fixing stage**); the bound is uniform over arities and tuples, and it bounds the least fixing rank of every realized finite invariant (every value of every member). *Not assumed:* modelhood, receiving, density, global termination, countability of the family or of the alphabet of labels, or that \(F(\beta)\) is inhabited at high indices; the empty family is allowed, and the set of uniform fixing stages is inhabited in every case, by a case split (when \(R\) has no supported tuple, every member is `none` everywhere and \(0\) is one). *The argument, arity by arity:* values are optional; positive niceness supplies an inhabited threshold for each supported tuple; an unsupported tuple of \(R\) is `none` in every member, because projection preserves supported and unsupported tuples (hypothesis 2); the bound for one arity is the uniform-fixation theorem of InfinitaryLogic, `StageProjection.exists_uniform_fixing_stage` (available at the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked, `#check`ed in `SuggestedInterfaces.lean`; its statement is quoted in `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"), applied at universe \(0\) (`Ordinal.{0}`, `Label.{0}`) with the type of optional values at the tuples of that arity as its label type `I`, projection to \(\gamma\) as its `project γ` (`Label.reduce (blockStage γ)`, label by label), the members of \(F(\beta)\) as its admissible presentations at \(\beta\), and, as its countable type of coordinates, the supported injective tuples of \(R\) of that arity only: hypothesis 1 is its stage correctness, and hypothesis 3 its eventual invariance of each coordinate, the member at the threshold \(\theta_t\) being the admissible witness of `EventuallyInvariant`; with no supported tuple the type of coordinates is empty and the stage is \(0\); a countable supremum combines the arities. No ordinal induction is used, and the argument of that theorem is not reproved here.
   - *Strictness and serving indices* (a separate requirement). The family is **strict** when every member of \(F(\beta)\) that is fixed by projection at \(\gamma\) has \(\beta\le\gamma\). Under strictness every serving index is at most \(A\). For models, strictness is to come from the statement that the fixing ranks of the realized finite charts of a model at block \(\eta\) have supremum \(\eta\) (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"; still to be proved). This step is essential and separate: the result does not give it, and it fails for arbitrary assignment families. *Negative special case* (prospective, not compiled here): the all-undefined assignment (every tuple `none`) is fixed by projection at \(0\), yet the constant family whose member at every index is that assignment is stage-correct at every index and satisfies hypotheses 1–3 with \(R\) all-undefined; its uniform fixing stage is \(0\) and every countable index is serving. Neither bound asserts that a model exists at \(A\), and neither constructs a maximal presentation.
   - *Milestones*, kept distinct, each with its own dependencies (completion criteria in `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Completion criteria of the uniform fixing bounds, milestone by milestone", for rows 29–32):
     1. **Closedness is supportedness.** In an actual realization an injective tuple is supported exactly when its set of points is closed, and projections preserve supported and unsupported tuples. It belongs with structural reconstruction (item 2). Compiled in this repository (theorem named): an injective tuple is typed exactly when its set of points is a support (`Realization.isSome_eval_iff_isSupport`, `Realization/Hull`, under exact consistency); a finite set is closed for the canonical closure exactly when it is a support (`Realization.isClosed_coe_iff`, `Realization.isClosed_iff_of_finite`, `Realization/Closure`, under exact consistency and covering); stage reduction keeps typed and untyped tuples and the closure (`Realization.isSome_reduce_eval`, `Realization/Transport`; `Realization.closure_reduce`, `Realization/Closure`). The combined statement is not named, and that these closed tuples are those of [AFK26] is still to be proved (item 2). Depends on: item 2 only.
     2. **Positive niceness for the construction.** For every actual closed tuple of a countable model of \(\Phi\), an inhabited threshold and one value, quantified across **all** admissible lifts (every admissible presentation at every index above the threshold), not one chosen lift: the row "invariance over all admissible presentations, with an inhabited threshold" of the table above. Compiled conditional on `HasTerminalRefinement` (the specified terminal refinement, below) and `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, both still to be proved: the niceness of [AFK26, Definition 2.19] (`IsNiceTupleAt`, one field for each of its two clauses; `IsNiceTuple`; `IsNice`; `MainTheorem/Niceness`), for the base reduct of every model at a block stage on a countable carrier (`Realization.IsModel.isNice_toStructure_reduce`, condition (c) of [AFK26, Definition 2.22]), with one threshold for all closed tuples and the invariant of each in a single maximal presentation as the actual witness (`exists_isNiceTupleAt_of_hasTerminalRefinement`); closed tuples are read as supported tuples of positive arity (the empty tuple, supported in every base structure with a lift but not closed in [AFK26], Definition 2.1, is excluded), whose identification with those of [AFK26] is milestone 1. Depends on: milestone 1 and a terminal presentation of each base, from either of the two stopping proofs, (i) the countable-slot argument or (ii) the Scott route to maximal presentations (below), with the injectivity of model reduction; an actual terminal presentation supplies positive niceness immediately (the status below).
     3. **The uniform fixing stage for the construction.** The uniform-fixation theorem of InfinitaryLogic applied to the data of milestone 2, with stage correctness retained explicitly; one bound precedes the quantifiers over indices, presentations, arities, and coordinates. Its form for fixing ranks is `StageProjection.exists_classwise_labelRank_bound` (one countable bound on `StageProjection.labelRank`, the least stage fixing a label, which is not a Scott rank), of the same module, available at the pin `e460cb6` (signatures checked). It proves no new theorem of infinitary logic and introduces no abstraction of ComputableModelTheory. Prospective. Depends on: milestone 2, and that theorem (available at the pin, signatures checked; no application compiled in this repository).
     4. **A bound on serving indices**, required for item 5 (row 32), whether or not the main theorem uses a bound of serving indices: strictness proved separately, for models; it is never inferred for arbitrary coherent assignment systems (the negative special case above). Prospective. Depends on: milestone 3 and strictness.
     5. **Presentations, terminal comparison, and noncollapse stay separate.** The existence and coverage of maximal presentations (through the class–level incidence above), the terminal comparison (Layer 4), and noncollapse (the last row of the table above) are separate statements the construction must prove; a bounded set of serving indices does not by itself supply a presentation at its supremum. Read for the presentations of one class, the bound of milestone 4 has the form of the first hypothesis of "Bounded levels" (the section "Reduction to full presentations"), and the uniform fixing stage of milestone 3 has the form of the bound for each class in step (iv) of `COMPANIONS.md`, "An alternative route to the lower bound"; neither supplies the unboundedness hypothesis there nor coverage. Depends on: the statements named for each conclusion below. The bounds of milestones 2–4 are not sufficient for any of these conclusions on their own; an argument may use them only together with additional hypotheses, which it states as dependencies. (a) *Existence of a maximal presentation, by bounded-stage attainment* (for the model presentations of one base): milestone 4 together with a model base (the base is a model, so \(0\) is a serving index), downward model reduction (serving indices are closed downward; `Realization.IsModel.reduce`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), the injectivity of model reduction at each countable index (a base structure has at most one model expansion to each block stage \(\lambda_\xi\), \(\xi<\omega_1\): `ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, `Expansion/Uniqueness`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, still to be proved), and limit coherence (model expansions below a countable limit, coherent by that injectivity, glue to one at the limit: `ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_forall_lt`, `Expansion/Uniqueness`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), under the same hypothesis; output 5 of higher-stage reconstruction, Layer 4). These give a greatest serving index, above which the base has no model presentation; the supplied bound itself need not be serving. Coverage by this route applies (a) to a model base of every base class. Route (a) is criteria 4 ⇒ 5 of the sub-item "Maximal presentations: equivalent criteria, uniqueness, the optimal bound" below, which restates these dependencies; the literal uniqueness and the optimal all-presentation bound of that sub-item depend on the same injectivity of model reduction and name it there. (b) *Noncollapse by the alternative route* (`COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, "An alternative route to the lower bound"): the uniform fixing stage of milestone 3, as the bound for each class of step (iv) there, together with steps (i)–(iii) there (charts of every fixing rank, a model realizing each, hence fixing ranks of realized charts cofinal in \(\omega_1\), which supply the unboundedness hypothesis) and the bounded-levels criterion in its form for relations between classes and countable ordinals ("Bounded levels"). (c) No argument through the bounds is recorded for the terminal comparison (Layer 4), which has its own; the default noncollapse statement, nonempty losses on the expansion-domain route (`hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, `MainTheorem/LowerBound`, with the hypotheses of the last row of the table above), uses none of milestones 1–4. Routes (a) and (b) use termination only through milestone 2, and only when milestone 2 is taken from the countable-slot argument (the status below).
   - *Status.* The conditional statements avoid termination.
