Positive niceness for the models of the construction (milestone 2) is to be derived from a terminal presentation: an actual terminal presentation of a base supplies it immediately, by terminal collision and the injectivity of model reduction, with no further stabilization argument ("Two stopping proofs; positive niceness from a terminal presentation", below); this derivation is compiled conditional on `HasTerminalRefinement.{w}` and `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.{w}` (at `w = 0`, the refinement follows from (R1), next-block uniqueness, and the countable-block apex property; these premises remain open) (`isNice_of_hasTerminalRefinement`, `MainTheorem/Niceness`). Terminal presentations come from either of two distinct stopping proofs, each still to be proved: (i) the countable-slot argument, the termination argument of [AFK26]; and (ii) the Scott-isolation bound followed by greatest-stage attainment (bounded-stage attainment, 4 ⇒ 5 below; the Scott route to maximal presentations, below), whose stated dependencies are Scott isolation for one class, conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction, the conversion between the two encodings with the transport of the base to a code on \(\mathbb N\), the injectivity of model reduction, and countable-limit existence, and which assumes no termination: it proves stopping for each base as its conclusion. Positive niceness obtained this way does not go through eventual invariance; an eventual-invariance proof of it over all lifts would need termination, since it uses that a class has no lift above a terminal stage ("The two counting endpoints" above). So this factorization clarifies the argument of the manuscript but does **not** by itself remove termination from the second endpoint (maximal-presentation counting): it takes its terminal presentations from (i), whose intended conclusion, once its steps (a)–(c) ("Two stopping proofs; positive niceness from a terminal presentation", below) are supplied, is the stopping half of global termination (not terminal fullness), or from (ii), which depends instead on conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction, a dependence it states. Nor does it replace the termination-free expansion-domain endpoint, which remains the default (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 9). That the conditional core does not use termination is a statement about the dependencies of its proof (no hypothesis or lemma about termination enters it), not a claim about the import closure of any module. Fixing ranks are not Scott ranks, and no bound here is identified with a Scott rank.

   **Maximal presentations: equivalent criteria, uniqueness, the optimal bound** (source: [AFK26], no numbered statement; prospective here: no statement of this sub-item is a theorem of this repository unless one is named, and no statement of it is counted as proved because an argument for it is known). For one literal base, this relates the bounds of the sub-item above to the maximal presentations whose incidence with classes and levels is the class–level incidence above; it quantifies over **all** actual model presentations of that base, not over one selected chain. Fix a carrier \(M\) and a base invariant assignment \(R\) on it (the sub-item above), taken literally, not up to isomorphism. A **model presentation** of \(R\) at a countable index \(\beta\) is an actual model at \(\lambda_\beta\) on \(M\) (`Realization.IsModel`) whose evaluation, read as an assignment, projects literally to \(R\) at \(0\); the model presentations at \(\beta\) form a family \(F_R(\beta)\) in the sense of the sub-item above, whose hypothesis 2 holds by definition and whose stage correctness holds for every member (the labels of a stage type at \(\lambda_\beta\) lie at that stage, `Label.AtStage`). The base \(R\) **is a model** when it is the evaluation of a model at \(\lambda_0=\omega\), that is, a model presentation of itself at \(0\). A **maximal presentation** of \(R\) is a model presentation at a countable index \(\rho\) such that \(R\) has no model presentation at any countable index above \(\rho\). A model presentation \(W\) at \(\rho\) is **terminal** when it is terminal at \(\rho\) in the sense of `Realization.IsTerminalAt` (`Continuation/Terminal`): no model at \(\lambda_{\rho+1}\) on \(M\) reduces to \(W\), equivalently no model at any higher stage that is zero or a limit does (`Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). **Terminal collision** is the following step, the inequality step of the literal uniqueness below: given a terminal \(W\) at \(\rho\) and a model presentation \(V\) of \(R\) at an index \(\eta>\rho\), the reduction of \(V\) to \(\lambda_\rho\) is a model presentation at \(\rho\) (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`), hence equal to \(W\) by the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\), while \(W\) is the reduction of no model at a higher stage (`Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt`); so no such \(V\) exists. The **injectivity of model reduction** for \(R\) is the statement that at each countable index \(\beta\) any two model presentations of \(R\) at \(\beta\) are equal (at most one model at \(\lambda_\beta\) on \(M\) projects to \(R\) at \(0\)). Its raw form, in the raw base encoding (*Two encodings* below), is `ModelExpansion.subsingleton` (`Expansion/Uniqueness`; compiled in this repository (theorem named)): a base structure has at most one model expansion at each block \(\lambda_\xi\), \(\xi<\omega_1\). That theorem is conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, still to be proved, which is derived from (R1) and `ForcingDonors` (`Continuation/Normalization`, still to be proved) by `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.of_forcingDonors` (`Expansion/UniquenessOfForcing`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)). So the injectivity of model reduction is not proved here, and until it is proved it is an explicit hypothesis of each statement below that uses it: bounded-stage attainment (4 ⇒ 5, route (a) of milestone 5 above), hence the equivalence of the five criteria and of criteria 2–5 for arbitrary carriers; literal uniqueness (both its steps) and its consequences; and the optimal all-presentation bound (through the inequality step only, so at \(\rho\) only). *Compiled conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, in the raw base encoding* (`MainTheorem/MaximalRefinement`, theorems named; for a base structure on any carrier, with its model expansions as the model presentations): bounded-stage attainment, 4 ⇒ 5 (`MainTheorem.exists_isGreatest_servingIndex_of_le`: a countable bound on the serving indices gives a greatest serving index, through the ordinal statement `exists_isGreatest_of_closed`, `Counting/OrdinalAttainment`, with no hypothesis), and literal uniqueness, both steps (`MainTheorem.exists_le_reduceBlock_eq_of_isTerminalAt`: every model expansion of the base of a terminal model expansion at a countable \(\rho\) has index at most \(\rho\) and is its stage reduction). *Compiled conditional on `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1)), `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage*, in the same encoding, for a countable carrier: criterion 4 with the bound of the Scott route (`MainTheorem.lt_qrank_of_isolates`), criterion 5 with an attained greatest serving index (`MainTheorem.exists_isGreatest_servingIndex`), and the terminal refinement below (`MainTheorem.exists_maximalRefinement`). Their forms for the common invariant encoding wait for the conversion between the two encodings (*Two encodings* below), still to be proved; the five criteria as an equivalence and the optimal all-presentation bound remain prospective.
   - *Five equivalent criteria* (prospective). For a base \(R\) that is a model, on a countable carrier, under the injectivity of model reduction (an explicit hypothesis until proved; above), the following are equivalent (the hypothesis is used only by 4 ⇒ 5):
     1. every supported injective tuple of \(R\) is positively nice for the family of all model presentations (hypothesis 3 of the sub-item above, with \(F=F_R\)): it has an actual positive label (a value other than `none`) in an actual model presentation at an inhabited countable threshold, and the same label in every model presentation at every higher countable index;
     2. one countable index fixes every model presentation under projection (a uniform fixing stage of \(F_R\));
     3. one countable index bounds every actual finite-invariant fixing rank in every model presentation (the fixing rank of each value at each injective tuple), simultaneously over all arities and tuples;
     4. the serving indices of \(F_R\), the countable indices at which \(R\) has a model presentation, have a countable bound;
     5. \(R\) has an actual maximal presentation at a countable index.

     *Countability* (prospective). Only the implication from 1 to a bound (1 ⇒ 2) uses the countability of the carrier: it is the uniform fixing stage of the sub-item above for \(F_R\), through `StageProjection.exists_uniform_fixing_stage` (available at the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"), whose type of coordinates is to be countable. Criteria 2–5 are equivalent for arbitrary carriers when \(R\) is a model, under the injectivity of model reduction (an explicit hypothesis until proved): 2, 3 and 4 are equivalent with no such hypothesis, and 5 ⇒ 4 is immediate from maximality, but 4 ⇒ 5 uses it. The three sorts of bound (fixing, criterion 2; fixing ranks, criterion 3; serving indices, criterion 4) are equivalent pointwise: an ordinal \(\xi\) fixes every model presentation exactly when it bounds every actual fixing rank of every model presentation, exactly when it bounds every serving index. These pointwise equivalences hold at every proposed bound \(\xi\), countable or not, and without inhabitation: with no model presentation all three hold vacuously.

     *Why the converses hold* (prospective; each step a statement to be proved). An assignment is fixed by projection at \(\xi\) exactly when every actual label is fixed, an undefined tuple being unchanged by projection; through the least fixing rank of each label (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"), fixation at \(\xi\) is then a bound \(\xi\) on the actual fixing ranks (2 ⇔ 3). For a model at \(\lambda_\beta\), strictness (the sub-item above; for models, from the supremum statement of `COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks of finite charts") identifies its least fixing index as a whole assignment with \(\beta\), so a bound on fixing is a bound on serving indices (2 ⇔ 4). The **bounded-stage attainment** statement (route (a) of milestone 5 above) produces from a countable bound on the serving indices a greatest serving index, the supplied bound itself need not be serving; it uses that \(R\) is a model (\(0\) is serving), downward model reduction (serving indices are closed downward; `Realization.IsModel.reduce`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and countable-limit existence (serving indices are closed under countable limits, which needs coherent choices of model presentations below the limit, supplied by the injectivity of model reduction, as in `ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_forall_lt`, `Expansion/Uniqueness`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`) (4 ⇒ 5); so bounded-stage attainment, and with it 4 ⇒ 5, is conditional on the injectivity of model reduction. Maximality excludes every model presentation at a higher index; so for a supported tuple \(t\) of \(R\), supported in a maximal presentation \(W\) at \(\rho\) since projection keeps supported tuples, its actual positive label in \(W\) is a niceness threshold at \(\rho\), agreement above \(\rho\) being vacuous since the threshold is strict (5 ⇒ 1); this step uses neither terminality nor the injectivity of model reduction. In the raw base encoding, with a maximal presentation read as a model expansion at a countable \(\rho\) such that \(\rho\) bounds the serving indices (`BoundsServingIndices`), 5 ⇒ 1 is compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis: `isNiceTupleAt_of_boundsServingIndices` and `isNice_of_boundsServingIndices` (`MainTheorem/Niceness`). A maximal presentation is also terminal (in the raw base encoding this step is `Realization.IsExpansionOf.isTerminalAt`, `Continuation/Terminal`, compiled in this repository (theorem named): an expansion of a base structure with no model expansion at the next block is terminal). **So the equivalence characterizes termination for the base \(R\); it is not a separate proof of termination.** A proof of positive niceness that uses termination (milestone 2 above) is not a separate proof of termination either. Milestone 5 above stands: the bounds are not sufficient alone, and for an arbitrary family a bound supplies by itself no presentation at its supremum; through criteria 4 ⇒ 5, its route (a), a bound gives a maximal presentation of one base only together with the additional hypotheses named there (a model base, downward model reduction, the injectivity of model reduction, limit coherence), which a proof taking this route states as dependencies, and only for the model presentations of a base that is a model.
   - *Literal uniqueness* (both steps compiled in the raw base encoding conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness` (below); under the injectivity of model reduction, an explicit hypothesis until proved, used by both steps). If a model presentation \(W\) of \(R\) at a countable index \(\rho\) is terminal, then every model presentation \(V\) of \(R\) at an index \(\eta\) has \(\eta\le\rho\), and \(V\) is literally the projection of \(W\) to \(\eta\) (its stage reduction to \(\lambda_\eta\)). The inequality is terminal collision: for \(\eta>\rho\), the reduction of \(V\) to \(\lambda_\rho\) is a model presentation at \(\rho\) (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`), hence equal to \(W\) by the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\), while \(W\) is the reduction of no model at a higher stage (`Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt`); in the raw base encoding this inequality is compiled conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, still to be proved (`ModelExpansion.boundsServingIndices_of_isTerminalAt`, `MainTheorem/Niceness`). The injectivity of model reduction at \(\eta\), applied to the reduction of \(W\) to \(\lambda_\eta\) and to \(V\), gives the equality. Both steps therefore use the injectivity of model reduction (at \(\rho\) and at \(\eta\)); without it a terminal \(W\) is not known to exclude a second model presentation at \(\rho\) that has a model presentation above it. No countability of the carrier is used. Depends on: the injectivity of model reduction, the same hypothesis as route (a) of milestone 5 above (raw form `ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`), at \(\rho\) for the inequality and at \(\eta\) for the equality; downward model reduction (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`) and the characterization of terminality `Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt` (`Continuation/Terminal`), for the inequality. Consequences, under the same hypothesis: two terminal model presentations of the same literal base have the same index and are equal (so their evaluations as assignments, in the common invariant encoding, are equal); the maximal presentation attained under criterion 4 does not depend on which bound was supplied. *Uniqueness at one block, and how it differs.* `ModelExpansion.subsingleton` (`Expansion/Uniqueness`; compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, still to be proved) says that a base structure has at most one model expansion at each block \(\lambda_\xi\), \(\xi<\omega_1\); its limit step is the separation of realizations at a limit block by their reductions to the earlier blocks (`Realization.eq_of_forall_reduce_eq`, `Realization/Limit`). It compares two expansions at one block, in the raw base encoding, and is the raw form of the injectivity of model reduction used above. The literal uniqueness here compares the model presentations at all indices with one terminal presentation: it bounds their indices and identifies each with a reduct of it. Uniqueness at one block gives neither the bound \(\eta\le\rho\) nor a terminal presentation. The terminality of the reconstructed top-free realization (`reduce_ne_reconstruct`, `ClassicalLimit/Modelhood`; compiled in this repository (theorem named), under its age hypothesis) is a statement about the realization, not about its base reduct; with uniqueness at its block it is the exclusion of a higher expansion in the lower bound of Layer 6 (prospective). *Scope:* this concerns the actual model presentations of a fixed base. It does not assert that an arbitrary partial diagram, approximate assignment, or projected donor extends: the round trips of structural reconstruction (item 2) and projected-donor lifting (Layer 3, 3.3) remain separate statements.
   - *The optimal all-presentation bound* (prospective; under the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\), an explicit hypothesis until proved, through the inequality step of literal uniqueness). For a terminal model presentation \(W\) of \(R\) at a countable index \(\rho\), and every ordinal \(\xi\): \(\xi\) bounds the actual finite-invariant fixing ranks of all model presentations of \(R\) exactly when \(\rho\le\xi\); and projection at \(\xi\) fixes every model presentation exactly when \(\rho\le\xi\) (by the inequality step of literal uniqueness, every model presentation has an index \(\eta\le\rho\), and it is fixed at that index; conversely, by strictness, \(W\) is fixed at no index below \(\rho\)). So \(\rho\) is the **least upper bound** of the fixing ranks of the actual finite invariants of all model presentations of \(R\), stated with the given terminal presentation, not through a separately defined rank of termination. Two attainment questions are kept apart. The model presentation at \(\rho\) exists: it is \(W\). But when \(\rho\) is a nonzero limit, no finite invariant has fixing rank \(\rho\), since the fixing ranks of finite charts are zero or successors (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks are zero or successors" and "Limit heights are unattained suprema", each still to be proved); so the least upper bound is not a maximum of the set of fixing ranks, and no finite tuple realizes a limit rank. Depends on: the inequality step of literal uniqueness (terminal collision), hence the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\) only (the hypothesis of route (a) of milestone 5 above; the equality step, at \(\eta\), is not used); strictness for models (the sub-item above); and, for a nonzero limit \(\rho\), `COMPANIONS.md`, "Fixing ranks are zero or successors". For a literal base, \(\rho\) is the counterpart of the height of `COMPANIONS.md`, "Greatest refinements" (the least bound across all presentations of a countable base model, "Fixing ranks of finite charts"). None of these ordinals is a Scott rank.
   - *Guidance.* The five criteria are alternative entry points into the **conditional** maximal-presentation theorem (the existence of a maximal presentation of a base that is a model, the row "coverage by actual maximal presentations" of the table above, for one base); the construction is to establish whichever criterion it can without circularity, and criterion 1 through milestone 2 above uses the countable-slot argument when milestone 2 is taken from it; the Scott route to maximal presentations (below) enters through criterion 4 instead. An entry through criteria 1–4 reaches criterion 5 only through bounded-stage attainment (4 ⇒ 5), with the injectivity of model reduction as its hypothesis until proved; that is route (a) of milestone 5 above, and a proof of the existence of a maximal presentation that takes it states the dependencies named there; it applies only to the model presentations of a base that is a model. Four statements are recorded separately, each with its own proof:
     1. *actual model reduction and countable-limit existence*, the statements needed for attainment. Compiled in this repository (theorem named) in the raw base encoding: `Realization.IsModel.reduce` (`Realization/Model`) with `ModelExpansion.reduceBlock` (`Realization/Expansion`), and limit existence from a coherent family, `ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_coherent` (`Realization/Limit`), and from expansions below the limit, `ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_forall_lt` (`Expansion/Uniqueness`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, whose uniqueness makes the family coherent). Their forms for the model presentations of a literal base \(R\): prospective, the countable-limit form under the injectivity of model reduction;
     2. *the literal uniqueness theorem:* every model presentation is a reduct of the unique maximal one, under the injectivity of model reduction;
     3. *the optimal all-presentation bound*, as a least-upper-bound conclusion under the same hypothesis, never read as "a finite tuple realizes a limit rank";
     4. *the statements reconstructing models from general invariant data* (structural reconstruction, item 2; finite-age and higher-stage reconstruction, the section "The intended construction"), which none of 1–3 supplies.
   - *Negative special case* (prospective, not compiled here). The everywhere-undefined assignment (every injective tuple `none`) has no supported tuple, so criterion 1 holds vacuously, and no model presentation (a model is nonempty and covering, so it has a supported tuple, and projection keeps supported tuples), so criterion 4 holds vacuously; criterion 5 fails. An inhabited base cannot be inferred from either vacuous property, and the hypothesis that \(R\) is a model is needed in the equivalence. This differs from the negative special case of the sub-item above, where every index serves the constant family of that assignment, which is not the family of its model presentations.
   - *Two encodings.* The base is read in two encodings, not silently identified: the **raw base realization** (the realization at \(\omega\) of a base structure, `baseLanguage.toRealization`, `Language/Structure`; the encoding of `ModelExpansion`, whose base-reduct equation in `Realization.IsExpansionOf`, `(R.reduce _).toStructure = ‹_›`, is an equation of structures, with realization form at \(\omega\) `ModelExpansion.val_eq_toRealization`, `Realization/Expansion`), and the **common invariant assignment** (an assignment in the alphabet of all labels, the encoding of \(R\) here and of the uniform fixing bounds). The conversion between them is a separate statement, still to be proved, and a theorem compiled in one encoding is quoted in the other only through it.
   - *Endpoints.* The default expansion-domain endpoint is unchanged, and this sub-item does not reintroduce global termination as a hypothesis: none of its statements is a hypothesis of the first endpoint, and a maximal presentation for every class (coverage through the class–level incidence above: `FullPresentations`, with `presentedAt`, on `DensityClass`, `MainTheorem/Assembly`) remains a hypothesis of the second endpoint (`vaughtCounterexample_of_presentations`), whose known proof goes through global termination. The five criteria, literal uniqueness, and the optimal bound use no Scott isolation; the terminal refinement below takes its terminal presentation from either stopping proof ("Two stopping proofs; positive niceness from a terminal presentation", below); and the Scott route uses Scott isolation for one class at a time, from statements available at the pin (step 1 there). The isolating level of a countable family of InfinitaryLogic (Layer 0; available at the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked) is not used by the route. Concordance rows 33–36, 38, and 40 and the completion criteria are in `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Manuscript concordance".
   - *Terminal refinement of a specified higher presentation* (a target; concordance row 38; compiled conditional on `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1)), `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, in the form below: `MainTheorem.exists_maximalRefinement`, `MainTheorem/MaximalRefinement`, through the Scott route, each hypothesis still to be proved; its compiled form takes \(V\) at an index \(\beta\) not assumed countable and returns \(W\) on the same carrier, terminal and maximal at a countable \(\rho\ge\beta\), with stage reduction \(V\) to \(\lambda_\beta\), and derives \(\beta<\omega_1\) from \(\beta\le\rho\), not from `Realization.IsModel.lt_omega_one`; the maximality clause compares base structures, in the raw base encoding). The statement (*specified terminal refinement*): every model \(V\) at a block stage \(\lambda_\beta\) on a countable carrier \(M\) is literally a reduct of a terminal model **on the same carrier**: a model \(W\) at \(\lambda_\rho\) on \(M\) itself, not on an isomorphic copy, for some countable \(\rho\ge\beta\), terminal at \(\rho\) (`Realization.IsTerminalAt`), whose stage reduction to \(\lambda_\beta\) is \(V\). The index \(\beta\) is not assumed countable; it is derived first, before \(V\) is read as a model presentation (an index that is countable by definition): \(V\) is a model on a countable carrier, so its stage \(\lambda_\beta\) is countable (`Realization.IsModel.lt_omega_one`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)), and \(\beta\le\lambda_\beta\) (`le_blockStage`, `Realization/Expansion`), so \(\beta<\omega_1\). The proof is to have two steps. (1) *A terminal presentation of the base:* the base \(R\) of \(V\) (its evaluation projected to \(0\)) is a model on \(M\) (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`), and it is to have a terminal model presentation \(W\) on \(M\) at a countable \(\rho\). Either stopping proof supplies it ("Two stopping proofs; positive niceness from a terminal presentation", the next sub-item): (i) the countable-slot argument, or (ii) the Scott route to maximal presentations, whose maximal presentation is terminal (above). Each is to produce \(W\) on \(M\) itself: in (ii), the passage to a code on \(\mathbb N\) (its step 4) concerns only the membership of the class of \(R\) in the domains, and the maximum is attained on \(M\); in (i), whose conclusion concerns classes, read on codes on \(\mathbb N\), the terminal expansion of a code of the class of \(R\) is carried to \(M\) along an isomorphism of that code with the base structure of \(R\) (`ModelExpansion.map`, in both directions, so that terminality is carried too), after the conversion between the two encodings and the transport of step 4 of the Scott route, which (i) then depends on. (2) *Literal-reduct uniqueness:* \(V\) is a model presentation of \(R\) at the countable index \(\beta\), so literal uniqueness (above) gives \(\beta\le\rho\) and that \(V\) is the stage reduction of \(W\) to \(\lambda_\beta\); it is conditional on the injectivity of model reduction (an explicit hypothesis until proved; raw form `ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`). Through (ii), no global termination theorem is used: step (1) concerns the one base \(R\), and step (2) compares the model presentations of that base only; through (i), step (1) is the instance at \(R\) of the stopping half of global termination, once (i) supplies it (its steps (a)–(c), the next sub-item). Compiled in this repository (theorem named): `Realization.IsTerminalAt` with `Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt` (`Continuation/Terminal`), `Realization.IsModel.reduce`, `Realization.IsModel.lt_omega_one`, and `le_blockStage`; the existence of the terminal extension is compiled conditionally through the Scott route (`MainTheorem.exists_maximalRefinement`, with the hypotheses named at the head of this sub-item) and prospective through the countable-slot argument. It is the form, for one specified presentation, of the terminal expansion of `COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, item 3, and does not use the countable family of terminal conditions of Layer 4.

   The abstract hypothesis `HasTerminalRefinement.{w}` is retained for arbitrary carrier universe `w`. At `w = 0`, `HasTerminalRefinement.of_hasApexCoatomExtensions` derives it from (R1), next-block uniqueness, and the apex property at every countable block; these three hypotheses remain open.

   **Two stopping proofs; positive niceness from a terminal presentation** (source: [AFK26], the termination of its construction and the positive niceness of this item, no numbered statement; prospective here: no statement of this sub-item is a theorem of this repository unless one is named). Two distinct stopping proofs supply terminal presentations; they are kept as separate statements, with separate dependencies, and are not merged into one termination statement:
     - (i) *the countable-slot argument*, the termination argument of [AFK26]. Informally, there are countably many slots, and each is used at most once (its slots are not defined in this roadmap). That description alone does not establish even the stopping half of global termination. A proof along it is to supply (a) which events use a slot; (b) why every relevant continuation (each lift of a class to a higher stage) uses a fresh slot; and (c) why exhausting those events yields an actual terminal presentation. Countably many events may still continue through a countable limit: the supremum of their stages is a countable ordinal, but it is not by itself an attainment proof (a presentation at that stage) or a terminality proof (no lift above it), and (c) is to supply both. The identification of its conclusion with the stopping half of global termination, eventual departure ("The persistent core"), is prospective, to be supplied by (a)–(c): every class leaves the expansion domains at a countable stage and has a terminal expansion there (`COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, items 1–3). It would not conclude the second half, terminal fullness (the terminal expansion is full for its terminal exact age), which is the first special statement;
     - (ii) *the Scott-isolation bound followed by greatest-stage attainment*, the Scott route to maximal presentations (the next sub-item): for each base that is a model, a strict bound on its serving stages from Scott isolation for its class and conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction, then an attained maximum, which is terminal.

   Either supplies, for a base \(R\) that is a model, an actual terminal model presentation \(W\) at a countable \(\rho\). **An actual terminal presentation supplies positive niceness immediately**, with no further stabilization argument: by terminal collision and the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\) (the inequality step of literal uniqueness, above), every model presentation of \(R\) has index at most \(\rho\), so \(W\) is maximal (criterion 5), and 5 ⇒ 1 gives positive niceness; compiled conditional on `HasTerminalRefinement.{w}` and `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness.{w}` (at `w = 0`, the refinement follows from (R1), next-block uniqueness, and the countable-block apex property; these premises remain open) (`exists_isNiceTupleAt_of_hasTerminalRefinement`, `MainTheorem/Niceness`). Through (ii) the presentation is maximal by construction, and 5 ⇒ 1 uses maximality only. *Order of the argument, without circularity:* a terminal presentation first, from (i) or (ii); positive niceness afterwards; the uniform fixing bounds (1 ⇒ 2 of the five criteria above) after that, if they are used. Neither stopping proof may use positive niceness, the uniform fixing bounds, or criteria 1–3, so no bound obtained from positive niceness is used to produce it. Depends on: one stopping proof, (i) or (ii), with its own dependencies (for (i), the hypotheses of the termination argument of [AFK26], to be stated with it; for (ii), those marked in the next sub-item); and, for the passage from a terminal presentation to positive niceness, the injectivity of model reduction at \(\rho\) (raw form `ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`), downward model reduction (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`), and the characterization of terminality (`Realization.isTerminalAt_iff_forall_lt`), as in literal uniqueness. Neither stopping proof is a dependency of the default expansion-domain endpoint, which uses no termination (`SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 9).

   **The Scott route to maximal presentations** (source: [AFK26], the maximal presentations and the positive niceness of this item, no numbered statement; prospective here: no statement of this sub-item is a theorem of this repository unless one is named; concordance row 40; steps 1–5 compiled conditional on `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1)), `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, each still to be proved, in the raw base encoding, as described after step 6). The second stopping proof (the sub-item above): the Scott-isolation bound followed by greatest-stage attainment. For each base that is a model it proves stopping (an actual maximal, hence terminal, presentation: criterion 5) as its conclusion, from conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction; it does not use the countable-slot argument and assumes no termination. Positive niceness for the models of the construction (milestone 2 of the uniform fixing bounds above) follows from the maximum it attains. Fix a base \(R\) that is a model on a countable carrier \(M\), of base class \(q\). The chain:
     1. *Scott isolation for the class \(q\):* a countable level \(\gamma_q\) such that a countable model whose empty tuple is back-and-forth equivalent at \(\gamma_q\) to that of a fixed countable model \(M_q\) of \(q\) lies in \(q\). Equivalence at any \(\beta\ge\gamma_q\) gives it at \(\gamma_q\) (`BFEquiv.monotone`, `Scott/BackAndForth`, available at the pin, signatures not yet checked by CI), as step 4 uses. The level is available at the pin, over the base language, which is relational with countably many relation symbols (`Language/Basic`): \(\gamma_q\) is the stabilization ordinal of \(M_q\), countable by `stabilizationOrdinal_lt_omega1'`, at which back-and-forth equivalence of a countable structure with \(M_q\) is isomorphism (`stabilizationOrdinal_spec`, `Scott/RefinementCount`; these two available at the pin, signatures checked). Equivalently, the Scott sentence of \(M_q\), of quantifier rank at most \(\gamma_q\) (`scottFormula_qrank_le`, `Scott/QuantifierRank`), characterizes \(q\) among countable structures (`scottSentence_characterizes`, `Scott/RefinementCount`), and back-and-forth equivalence at \(\gamma_q\) transfers it (`BFEquiv_implies_agreeQR`, `Karp/CarrierTheorem`; these three available at the pin (signatures checked)). This form still uses `stabilizationOrdinal_lt_omega1'` (signatures checked): `scottFormula_qrank_le` takes the level below \(\omega_1\), which for the Scott sentence is that lemma. The level is for the one class \(q\). The isolating level of a countable family (`exists_isolating_level`, `Scott/IsolatingLevel`; available at the pin `e460cb6`, signatures checked; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins") is not used: it decides isomorphism between members of a countable family only, while the comparison partner \(p\) of step 4 ranges over the classes of a loss, which may be \(\aleph_1\) many. No termination is used.
     2. *Uniform comparison:* any two classes in \(D_\beta\) are back-and-forth equivalent at \(\beta\) on the empty tuple (condition 3; `Expansion.bfEquiv_of_modelExpansions`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `Expansion.FiniteExtensionReceiving`, equivalent to (R1), still to be proved). Step 4 needs only its agreement form: the Scott sentence of \(M_q\), read as a sentence, has quantifier rank at most \(\gamma_q\le\beta\), so agreement on the sentences of quantifier rank at most \(\beta\) (`Expansion.mem_modelsOf_iff_of_modelExpansions` for two codes, or `Expansion.realize_iff_of_modelExpansions` for base structures on any carriers, each compiled in this repository (theorem named), under the same hypothesis) already carries it from \(q\) to every class in \(D_\beta\); the back-and-forth form serves with `stabilizationOrdinal_spec` instead.
     3. *Local nonempty losses:* a nonempty successor loss \(D_\beta\setminus D_{\beta+1}\) at the blocks used, cofinally many sufficing (condition 4; `hasNonemptyLosses_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `CapToModel`, on `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, and on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, each still to be proved).
     4. *A strict bound on serving stages:* from 1–3, \(q\) does not lie in every \(D_\eta\). If the serving indices of \(R\) were cofinal in \(\omega_1\), choose \(\beta\ge\gamma_q\) with a class \(p\) in the loss at \(\beta\); \(\beta+1\) is serving (serving indices are closed downward), so \(q\in D_{\beta+1}\subseteq D_\beta\); by 2 and 1, \(p=q\), against \(p\notin D_{\beta+1}\). So some countable \(\eta\) has \(q\notin D_\eta\), and every serving index of \(R\) is below \(\eta\): criterion 4. This is eventual departure (`COMPANIONS.md`, terminal refinement, item 1) read for one class. The passage between the serving indices of the literal base \(R\) on \(M\) and the membership of its class in the domains, which are stated for codes on \(\mathbb N\), has three parts: the conversion between the two encodings (*Two encodings* above, still to be proved), which reads \(R\) and its presentations as a base structure on \(M\) with its model expansions; the transport of that structure to a code on \(\mathbb N\), which needs the base structure of \(R\) to satisfy the density sentence (`realize_toStructure_densitySentence_iff`, `Language/Density`, with finite-cut receiving of models, (R1), already a dependency through condition 3) and \(M\) infinite (`CapToModel.infinite`, `MainTheorem/Assembly`, conditional on `CapToModel`; the code itself, `exists_mem_modelsOf_densitySentence_equiv_of_capToModel`, same module, under the same hypothesis), with model expansions carried along the isomorphism by `ModelExpansion.map` (`Realization/Expansion`); and `Expansion.mem_expansionDomain_iff`, stated for codes. These are compiled in this repository (theorem named; `ModelExpansion.map` is a definition), except the conversion. The code is used only for this comparison; the maximum of step 5 is attained on \(M\).
     5. *An attained maximum:* bounded-stage attainment (4 ⇒ 5 above) gives a maximal presentation of \(R\), using that \(R\) is a model, downward model reduction (`Realization.IsModel.reduce`), and countable-limit existence (`ModelExpansion.nonempty_of_forall_lt`, `Expansion/Uniqueness`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`), with the coherence of the presentations below a limit given by the injectivity of model reduction (raw form `ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, under the same hypothesis), an explicit hypothesis until proved. The maximal presentation is terminal (above): this is the stopping conclusion for \(R\).
     6. *Positive niceness:* 5 ⇒ 1 above, from maximality and the strict threshold only, derived after the terminal presentation as in the sub-item above.

     *Compiled form* (`MainTheorem/MaximalRefinement`, theorems named; compiled conditional on `Expansion.FiniteCutReceiving` ((R1)), `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, and `StageType.HasApexCoatomExtensions` at every countable block stage, each still to be proved; steps 1–5, in the raw base encoding, for the base structure \(B\) of a model on a countable carrier). Step 1: the Scott sentence of \(B\), read as a sentence, isolates \(B\) among the countable base structures (`MainTheorem.exists_isolates`, from `scottSentence_characterizes` and `Formulaω.realize_as_sentence_iff_toSentenceω` of InfinitaryLogic at the pin, both used by the compiled proof), with countable quantifier rank (`MainTheorem.qrank_lt_omega_one`, `MainTheorem/Spectrum`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), by induction on the formula; not stated at the pin); the bound is \(\delta=\) the quantifier rank of this chosen isolating sentence, not a Scott rank, and `stabilizationOrdinal_lt_omega1'` is not used. Step 2 is used in its agreement form, between \(B\) on its own carrier and the codes on \(\mathbb N\) of the classes in \(D_\delta\) (`Expansion.realize_iff_of_modelExpansions`), so neither the conversion between the encodings nor a code of \(B\) (nor `CapToModel`) enters. Step 3 is used at \(\delta\) and \(\delta+1\): \(D_\delta\) contains a class not in \(D_{\delta+1}\) and a class in \(D_{\delta+1}\) (`MainTheorem.expansionDomain_nontrivial`, from `nonempty_loss_of_hasApexCoatomExtensions`). Step 4, in this form: if \(B\) had a model expansion at \(\lambda_\delta\), every class in \(D_\delta\) would be the class of a structure isomorphic to \(B\), so \(D_\delta\) would be a subsingleton (`MainTheorem.expansionDomain_subsingleton_of_isolates`), against step 3; so every serving index is below \(\delta\) (`MainTheorem.lt_qrank_of_isolates`), with no cofinality argument. Step 5: `MainTheorem.exists_isGreatest_servingIndex` and `MainTheorem.exists_maximalRefinement_of_modelExpansion`. Step 6 and the forms in the common invariant encoding: prospective.

     *Dependencies, marked.* The route uses conditions 3 and 4 of the expansion-domain reduction (hypotheses of the count, not its conclusions), Scott isolation for one class (at the pin), the conversion between the two encodings (still to be proved) with the transport of \(R\) to a code on \(\mathbb N\) (step 4), the injectivity of model reduction, and countable-limit existence. It is a stopping proof: it proves stopping for each base that is a model (criterion 5) as its conclusion. It does not assume termination: neither the countable-slot argument nor any other hypothesis or lemma about termination enters it. This is a statement about the dependencies of a prospective proof: no step is compiled as a theorem about the construction, and the steps with compiled ingredients are conditional on the hypotheses named. *No circularity.* Both directions are kept: the uniform fixing bounds above go from positive niceness to a bound (1 ⇒ 2, the conditional of that sub-item), and this route goes from a bound on serving indices to a maximal presentation and then to positive niceness (4 ⇒ 5 ⇒ 1). A proof may use either, but not the two in a cycle: a bound obtained from positive niceness is never used to produce that positive niceness, the bound of step 4 comes from steps 1–3 only, never from criteria 1–3, and positive niceness is derived after the terminal presentation (step 6), never used to produce it. *Not a dependency of the default proof.* The expansion-domain endpoint uses conditions 3 and 4 directly and does not use this route; a statement proved through it is quoted on the second endpoint only with its dependence on conditions 3 and 4 stated ("The persistent core").
6. **The tree discussion (source [AFK26]): a tested illustration, not a foundation.** The introductory example of full trees ([AFK26, Definition 3.10], the \(\beta\)-full trees; the same-index equivalence was stated in the version of 1 October 2026 of [AFK26], its Proposition 8.6, and is absent from the current version) illustrates the organization; neither route uses it. Its refutations are recorded as such (`LITERATURE.md`, §9; `COMPANIONS.md`, "Further companion results", "Full trees"; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Checkpoint order and acceptance", non-implications 1 and 5): the same-index equivalence of that earlier version is false (an informal counterexample, `LITERATURE.md`, §9; not compiled); the printed branching criterion misses nodes of low rank (branching at nodes of high rank does not give the extension property at nodes of low rank); and rank domination alone (a labelling at least as large at a parent as at each child) does not give strictly decreasing labels, so it need not be a rank. All three are prospective here: no example is compiled. A corrected positive classification of density for trees is a separate optional target, not established by these negative special cases and not part of this milestone.

### Acceptance criteria of the correspondence

Beside the completion criteria of `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Manuscript concordance", the correspondence is accepted only when the following hold.

- **Reconstruction.** Domain guards characterize existence (membership in the expansion domain, `Expansion.mem_expansionDomain_iff`, compiled in this repository (theorem named); the sentence of the domain guard, `COMPANIONS.md`, "Quantitative reconstruction", prospective); the formulas identify every actual expansion (`realize_blockFormula_iff`, in every model expansion, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on block determination); and uniqueness (`ModelExpansion.subsingleton`, conditional on `Expansion.NextBlockUniqueness`, or `ModelExpansion.eq_of_determines`, conditional on block determination; neither assumes a countable carrier) and literal transport (`ModelExpansion.map`) are separate proved statements (item 5, unique reconstruction).
- **Density.** Scalar cutoffs (`HasFiniteCutReceiving`, one permitted cutoff at a time), invariant observations (density at block observations, item 3, with its witness-bounded form), and projected-root receiving (receiving over a root of a stage reduction, which needs projected-donor lifting, Layer 3, 3.3; `SEMANTIC_CONTRACT.md`, item 12) are kept distinct. The donor-bounded and returned-invariant-bounded forms are equivalent formulations on a realization fixed at \(\beta\) (item 3); exact face preservation is supplied by consistency, not by a density predicate. Density at lower blocks is not an established substitute for modelhood.
- **Finite objects.** The static semantic rows are retained under projection (`StageType.reduce` changes labels only); coherent variable rows are an equivalent presentation of lawful labels, not arbitrary replacement data (item 1).
- **Rank budgets.** The reconstruction formulas have the stated budget \(\omega\cdot\eta\) (`qrank_blockFormula_le`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), with no hypothesis); comparison on the domains gives agreement through \(\eta\) (`Expansion.bfEquiv_of_modelExpansions` and `Expansion.realize_iff_of_modelExpansions`, compiled in this repository (theorem named), conditional on `Expansion.FiniteExtensionReceiving`). These are different results, and neither is derived from the other.
- **Priorities.** The concrete constructions (the completion of the coatom extension construction of Layer 3, `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, checkpoints 2.1–2.7, from which forcing donors follow), receiving ((R1)), the cap-to-model theorem (`CapToModel`), and the terminal classification (Layer 4) remain the main work of the implementation, ahead of further conditional compositions of the main theorem.

Both counting endpoints are kept (item 5), and neither stopping proof (the countable-slot argument, the Scott route to maximal presentations) is a dependency of the default expansion-domain proof.

## Optional developments, not required for the main theorem

**Closure and definability.** The definitional expansion by binary hull operations of `HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, with the equality of the canonical closure and generated-substructure closure for finite sets, the correspondence of finite charts with finite substructures, the preservation of the operations by embeddings (default values included), and closed images of finite closed sets, is part of the core (Layer 2), because the classical limit uses it. The inclusion \(\operatorname{cl}\subseteq\operatorname{dcl}\) in the full stage chart language is a Layer 2 statement ("Hull closure inside definable closure"). What remains optional: global preservation of the closure of infinite sets, deduced from finite preservation; hull cardinality bounds; and the consequences for uncountable models. Equality with definable or algebraic closure is a separate theorem requiring more information.

**Status of the optional results.** The table separates established results, whose proofs are known and which remain formalization targets here, from statements that are only deduced or are open.

| Result | Status |
| --- | --- |
| The binary hull operations and the equality of the canonical closure with generated-substructure closure (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, (1)) | established, proof known; part of the core (Layer 2) for finite sets, compiled for finite charts (Layer 2, "Status for finite charts"); for exactly consistent covering realizations the operations and their values in the hull of a pair are compiled, and the generation of the hull of a pair (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §3 for pairs) under legality along the hull of the pair (Layer 2, "Status for realizations"), and (1) for finite sets is to be formalized here; for infinite sets optional |
| The companion targets A1, A2, A3, B2, and B3 (`COMPANIONS.md`), and B1, the amalgamation of top-free charts, which is step 2 of the core construction of the top-free witnesses | established, proofs known; to be formalized here |
| Companion target C: every set of absolute indiscernibles in the full stage chart language has at most two elements, from exact consistency and covering (`COMPANIONS.md`) | argument given; a statement still to be proved here |
| The direct cardinal ceiling from cardinality-preserving infinite hulls and countable proper closed sets (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, §6) | argument given; a statement still to be proved here; no free-set theorem |
| The inclusions \(\operatorname{cl}\subseteq\operatorname{dcl}\subseteq\operatorname{acl}\) (`HULL_ALGEBRA.md`, (2)) | \(\operatorname{cl}\subseteq\operatorname{dcl}\): a Layer 2 statement, by the unique-coordinate formulas, compiled for finite charts (`StageType.definable₁_singleton_of_mem_hull`) and still to be proved for realizations; \(\operatorname{dcl}\subseteq\operatorname{acl}\) is standard; not counted among the established results |
| Any reverse inclusion, and equality of \(\operatorname{cl}\) with \(\operatorname{dcl}\) or \(\operatorname{acl}\) | open; requires a separate argument |
| The coatom extension property at successor stages, and with it [Kni26, Corollary 4.3.22] and [Kni26, Proposition 4.3.23] at those stages (Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)) | open; not needed, since (R6) is used at limit stages |
| The bountifulness of the completion of [Kni26, Definition 4.3.14], the statement of [Kni26, Lemma 4.3.20] for that completion (Layer 3, 3.1, (R6)) | unproved, not refuted; not relied on |
| The bountifulness of the flat variant of that completion: full-scope cells indexed by all lawful short coded patterns over a fixed finite alphabet, with agreement-height rows (Layer 3, 3.1, (R6), "The naive completion") | refuted at arity zero for every block bound at least 3 (in particular \(2N+1\) for \(N\) cells) (`SmallArityExamples.not_isBountiful_flatRows`, compiled in this repository (theorem named)); not relied on |

None of the optional consequences blocks the finite-age reconstruction of the top-free witnesses.

**Characteristics and stopping.** Retain characteristic arity, stable spectra, full continuation criteria, canonical stopping towers, eventual departure, and rank comparisons as separate developments built on the core. Eventual departure, the last admitted stage, and the terminal expansion are the conditional targets of terminal refinement, and the rank comparisons include the prospective rank-filtration comparison and a one-sided comparison with the stabilization ordinal (`COMPANIONS.md`, "Further companion results"); neither is used by the count, eventual departure uses hypotheses of the count and not its conclusions, and the rank comparisons may use its conclusions.

**Alternative stabilization.** The existing structural construction and finite synchronization suffice. An alternative proof using product closedness may remain useful in its own right, but is not a prerequisite.

**Scott processes, computability, and Polish actions.** Build pointed comparison, exact rank relationships when proved, and applications to Polish group actions and computability as separate libraries. A one-way back-and-forth bound does not identify the expansion tower with a Scott process or its stage index with Scott rank. The classical existence theorem gives no computable enumeration of the top-free witnesses; an effective presentation of them belongs to this separate development, where a construction by an explicit enumeration of requirements, such as the retained chain construction, is the natural starting point. That route is conditional on effective input data, an effective enumeration of the age and an effective amalgamation procedure; the classical Fraïssé construction uses choice and supplies no computable presentation.

## Validation and dependencies

Use the pinned Lean, infinitary-logic and computable-model-theory versions together, all three built on one Mathlib commit and one toolchain (`IMPLEMENTATION.md`, "Dependency pins"). Do not mix the current forked Mathlib pin with a separately pinned TauCeti dependency; “TauCeti-style” describes the layered roadmap, not a requirement to import the TauCeti repository.

At every summit, elaborate all modules, audit all declarations for placeholders and unexpected axioms, and check the literal statement against the semantic contract. Maintain separate checks for module imports and proof-term dependencies. A small theorem can import a large compatibility layer; a smaller file count does not prove a smaller mathematical dependency. Modules kept for historical compatibility should depend on the core, not conversely.
